# D-Store — HANDOVER.md
*Read this file first, every session. It tells you exactly what to work on and how to hand off when you're done.*

---

## 0. How this file works

Work is broken into a fixed hierarchy:

```
Phase        1, 2, 3, 4
 └─ Track     a, b, c, d
     └─ Milestone   i, ii, iii
         └─ Leaf        zi, zo
```

A **leaf** (`zi` or `zo`) is always exactly **one task**. That is the rule: no leaf ever bundles more than one task, and no leaf is ever split into less than one. A session's job is to take **the single next open leaf**, complete it, and hand off — never more than one leaf, never a partial leaf.

Each leaf is addressed by its full path, e.g. `1.a.i.zi`. Use that path in commit messages, patch filenames, and status updates so any future session can locate exactly where work stands.

### Repos in this program — clone all three, every session

This is no longer a single-repo effort. As of Phase 6 (`6.a`/`6.b` below), work is split across three repositories, and this file — the one you're reading — is the single place that says which of the three a given task needs and in what order. A session that only clones D-Store will be missing the other two the moment it touches anything under Phase 6.

| Repo | URL | Role |
|---|---|---|
| **Zealot** | `github.com/Zapier-codes/zealot` | The Console — Rails app, org signing, staged rollout, the signed catalog index. Has its own `handover.md` task board (Task 27 onward = "Zealot as the Play Console"; Task 32 = the Updater). |
| **D-Store** | `github.com/Zapier-codes/D-Store` | This repo — the public storefront (Next.js). Read-only consumer of Zealot's signed index; owns its own Supabase data (reviews/reports/counters) and, from `6.b` on, per-tenant storefront rendering. |
| **Storeapp** | `github.com/Zapier-codes/Storeapp` | The on-device client. Under the hood this is a fork base of **Vyxel Apps** (Kotlin/Android, AGPL-3.0, originally by NikhilKain) — a pre-existing multi-source app-store client with six sources (GitHub, GitLab, F-Droid, IzzyOnDroid, APKPure, Aptoide) already wired for install/signature-verify/WorkManager/Shizuku. Zealot/D-Store is being added as a **seventh source, additively** — none of the existing six sources, screens, or features are touched or replaced. |

Any leaf below tagged `(Zealot-repo leaf)` or `(Storeapp-repo leaf)` is tracked here **for visibility only** — it is not built by this repo, the same convention `5.j.i` already uses for Zealot-side registry leaves. A session working a Phase 6 leaf clones whichever repo(s) that leaf's tag names; a leaf with no repo tag is this repo's own work.

### Status markers
Every leaf carries one of:
- `[ ]` open — not started
- `[~]` in progress — a session started but did not finish (should be rare; a leaf is sized to finish in one session)
- `[x]` done — completed and handed off via patch (see Section 3)
- `[-]` superseded — no longer this repo's work because another system owns it; kept (not deleted) so the leaf ID stays addressable. Never pick one up as the next leaf.

### Architecture pivot (resequencing note)

The Symfony/Doctrine backend is no longer the direction of record. Going forward:
- **Supabase (Postgres)** is the metadata store only — app info, ratings, counters, developer/agreement status. It never holds APKs or other release binaries.
- **GitHub Releases** is the binary store *and* the source of usage metrics — it already exposes per-asset download counts natively, so those feed install/trending numbers instead of a custom increment endpoint built against Doctrine.

Because of this, Phase 1.a leaves already completed or in flight against the Doctrine `Application` entity (`1.a.i.zi`, done via Doctrine/YAML mapping) will need to be re-expressed against the Supabase schema once `5.f.i` lands — they are not being redone right now, just noted as superseded-pending-migration. New leaves should not add further Doctrine-only data model work until the Supabase schema exists.

**Unresolved — SQL-vs-Doctrine reconciliation for `1.a` (flagged mid-session, deliberately not decided — read before touching `1.a` again):**

Partway through one session, the guidance above ("no further Doctrine-only data model work") was acted on directly: `1.a.i.zo` and `1.a.ii.zi` were redone as Supabase SQL migrations and their Doctrine edits dropped locally — see `supabase/migrations/20260915140000_add_is_featured_is_editors_pick.sql` and `supabase/migrations/20260915140100_add_sha256_checksum.sql`, both written and structurally verified (parsed with Python's `sqlparse` as a single well-formed `ALTER` statement each; no live Postgres was available in-sandbox to actually run them). But by the time that redo was ready to hand off, `origin/master` had *already* merged the original Doctrine versions of those same two leaves (pushed from an earlier patch in the same session, before the SQL redo was requested) — and a separate session had since built three more Doctrine-based `1.a` leaves on top of them: `1.a.ii.zo` (`play_store_rejection_reason`), `1.a.iii.zi` (new `Review` entity — anonymous, rate-limited), `1.a.iii.zo` (new `ReportFlag` entity). `1.a` is now fully closed out on `origin/master`, entirely on Doctrine, not SQL — `2.b.iii.zo`'s report review queue is also already built against the `Review`/`ReportFlag` entities specifically.

So two things are sitting side by side, unreconciled:
1. **`origin/master`** — all five `1.a` leaves, Doctrine-based, fully merged, with real work built on top of two of them.
2. **This session's local-only work** — two Supabase SQL migration files reproducing `1.a.i.zo`/`1.a.ii.zi`'s exact column changes, prepared but not reconciled with what's already merged. Committed here, unpushed as leaf work (nothing in the `1.a` checklist below was changed by adding them), specifically so they aren't lost and are ready to use if needed.

**Decision needed before any further `1.a`-adjacent data-model work — pick one:**
- **(a) Retroactively convert all five merged Doctrine leaves to Supabase SQL migrations.** Bigger than originally scoped: also means writing SQL equivalents for the `Review`/`ReportFlag` entities (not just columns on `application`) and confirming `2.b.iii.zo`'s admin queue doesn't hard-depend on Doctrine-specific behavior (relations, repository methods, etc.) before it's safe to drop those PHP files.
- **(b) Leave the merged five as Doctrine.** Matches this file's original framing above ("superseded-pending-migration, re-express once `5.f.i` lands") — the SQL-migration direction would then apply only to genuinely new `1.a`/`5.f` data-model leaves from here on, not retroactively. In this case the two prepared SQL migration files above are redundant and should be deleted.

This was put to the user mid-session and deliberately left unanswered pending more information — **do not assume either direction.** Surface this note and get an explicit decision before writing or reverting any further `1.a` schema work.

**Resolved — (a), retroactively convert all five merged Doctrine leaves to Supabase SQL.** Decided by reading `Zapier-codes/zealot`'s `handover.md` (Task 27, "Zealot as the Play Console") rather than guessing, per the operator's direction to model this split on how the real Play-Store-caliber platforms do it:
- Zealot's own research into the giants (Play, Apple, Huawei, Samsung, F-Droid) concluded the console (Zealot) is the single write-owned source of truth and the store (this repo) stays a read-only consumer — and Zealot's own mechanism for publishing that truth is a **signed, versioned index** (Ed25519, F-Droid-style), not a shared Doctrine-mapped database. Zealot's ❓1 explicitly frames this as "index-not-Supabase," and its own schema changes ship as plain numbered Postgres/ActiveRecord migrations (`db/schema.rb`), never as an entity-mapping ORM layer — the same versioned-migration-as-source-of-truth convention this repo's `supabase/migrations/` already follows (see Section 5's rules) and structurally incompatible with Doctrine's code-first entity/YAML-mapping style.
- The data this repo actually owns outright — `Review` and `ReportFlag`, per Zealot's own Play-mapping table ("Reviews | Owned by D-store; Zealot reads and replies") — belongs in this repo's real backend. That backend is Supabase (this file's own "Architecture pivot" note above), not the parked `legacy-symfony/` app: Phase 0 already moved every live route to the Next.js app at the repo root, so the Doctrine `Application`/`Review`/`ReportFlag` entities and `2.b.iii.zo`'s `/admin/reports` controller sit in a codebase no live traffic reaches. Extending a data model nothing serves, instead of the one Vercel actually runs (`lib/catalog.ts`'s Supabase-bound seam), is the opposite of the "rival the giants" standard just cited.
- `is_featured`/`is_editors_pick` and `sha256_checksum` are trending the other way — toward Zealot's signed index as their source of truth (`5.g.v.zi`, still open) — but that leaf hasn't landed yet, so this repo still owns those columns today; converting their existing Doctrine representation to SQL now costs nothing extra and avoids a second migration once `5.g.v.zi` eventually deprecates local admin toggles.

The two prepared SQL migrations are kept (not deleted) and now count as the `1.a.i.zo`/`1.a.ii.zi` conversions. Remaining under this decision, in path order: `1.a.ii.zo` (`play_store_rejection_reason`) next, then `1.a.iii.zi`/`1.a.iii.zo` (`Review`/`ReportFlag` as SQL tables), then confirming `2.b.iii.zo`'s report queue has no Doctrine-specific hard dependency (relations/repository methods) before its PHP files are safe to drop. Each is its own leaf, per the one-leaf rule.

Sequencing is overridden below: the next leaves pull forward from Phase 5 (`5.f` — Catalog Database & Platform Risk) rather than continuing further down Phase 1 in path order.

**Resolved — `3.b` counter sourcing (was flagged unresolved by `0.i.iii.zo`, decided this session):** product owner chose **app-native counters** — Supabase-backed increment endpoints hit directly by the storefront on click/view — over waiting for Telegram/Cloudflare Worker request logs (`5.b.iii.zi`) or skipping `3.b` for now. This avoids the dependency on Phase 5's distribution pipeline entirely: install/view counts are the storefront's own click/view events, not derived from where the binary happens to be served from, so they're decoupled from the Telegram-vs-GitHub storage question altogether.

This still leaves the sequencing question the original note didn't reach: `5.f.i.zi` (provision Supabase) is gated behind Phases 1–4, so `3.b.i.zi` can't call a real Supabase project yet. Resolved the same way `1.a`'s real-backend schema work stayed ahead of its own infra (no live Postgres/PHP interpreter in-sandbox either): build against `lib/catalog.ts`'s existing data-fetch seam (its own header comment already commits to "when the real backend exists, this file's internals get swapped for Supabase queries and every caller keeps working unchanged") rather than importing a Supabase client directly from the new route handler. The route handler is real, callable, and exercised today against the in-memory dummy `apps` array; swapping `lib/catalog.ts`'s internals for real Supabase queries once `5.f.i` lands changes nothing at the route-handler or `InstallButton` call sites.\n\n**Storage roles (update) — SUPERSEDED by "Resolved — binary ownership" directly below, kept for history:** the Telegram S3-compatible drive is **primary** storage for APKs/splits — it's existing infrastructure that has been continuously active, not something to newly deploy. Telegram's job is storage only: it holds the finished assets and nothing else. The entire build/sign/split/checksum/changelog/catalog-sync pipeline runs end to end inside one GitHub Actions workflow, with the assets attached to that workflow run throughout — the workflow's final step is the one that releases the finished artifacts to the Telegram drive. GitHub Releases is **not** used to store or serve app binaries. The Cloudflare edge worker fronts the Telegram drive as the sole storage backend, with no GitHub fallback. This is reflected in the revised `5.a`–`5.c`, `5.f`, and `5.g` leaves below.

**Resolved — binary ownership (product owner, this session; supersedes the `Storage roles (update)` paragraph above and `5.a` / `5.b` / `5.e` / `5.g.ii` as originally written):** the Developer Console — the separate Zealot repo (`github.com/Zapier-codes/zealot`) — **compiles, signs and stores** the organisation-signed APK. It runs the AAB → signed APK/split step in its own pipeline with its own org signing key and keeps the result in its own release storage. **D-Store is only the front-facing storefront**: it shows every app — icon, screenshots, reviews, download button and the rest of the Play-Store-parity feature set — and does not build, sign, split or store binaries.

**Cross-checked — D-Store's Supabase is not, and must never be, Zealot's Supabase (operator flagged this as a risk to verify, not assumed):** confirmed by reading both repos directly, not inferred.
- **Zealot already has its own Supabase-hosted Postgres**, provisioned and live: `render.yaml` configures `ZEALOT_DATABASE_URL` as "Supabase Postgres (Session Pooler), not a Render-managed database," set directly in the Render dashboard. `db/schema.rb` shows what's in it: `apps`, `releases`, `users`, `publisher_profiles`, `android_signing_keys`, `play_credentials`, etc. — Zealot's own Rails/ActiveRecord schema for its own console data. No table there is named `application`, `review` or `report_flag`.
- **D-Store has provisioned no Supabase project at all yet** — `5.f.i.zi` is still open (`[ ]`, gated behind Phases 1–4). Confirmed by absence: no `supabase/config.toml`, no `.env*`, no client init code, no project ref or connection string anywhere in this repo. The `1.a` migrations converted so far (`1.a.i.zo`, `1.a.ii.zi`/`.zo`, `1.a.iii.zi`) are prepared SQL with no live target, same as their own headers already say.
- **The design intent was always two separate projects, not one shared database** — Zealot's own `docs/catalog_index_v1.md` states it in as many words: *"Instead of Zealot writing fields directly into D-Store's Supabase, Zealot publishes a signed catalog index and D-Store reads it — the F-Droid model, not the 'shared database' one."* Catalog data (listing text, versions, `is_featured`/`is_editors_pick` once `5.g.v.zi` lands, verified-developer flag) comes from Zealot's **signed index**, already read today by `lib/sources/zealot.ts`/`zealot-trust.ts` (`5.g.i.zi`) — never a live query against Zealot's own database. D-Store's own (not-yet-provisioned) Supabase project is meant to hold only what this repo itself generates and owns: `Review`, `ReportFlag`, install/view counters, ratings — matching `docs/D-STORE.md`'s "Store-owned data" row and the reasoning behind converting `Review`/`ReportFlag` to this repo's own SQL migrations (`1.a.iii`) rather than Zealot's schema.
- **Two stale spots said otherwise and are fixed by this note's companion patch:** `docs/D-STORE.md` §8 ("Its only relationship to the Console is reading the metadata the Console's pipeline writes to Supabase") and a comment in `app/api/admin/apps/[slug]/featuring/route.ts` ("sourced from the Console's Supabase-side agreement status") both describe the shared-database model Zealot's own docs explicitly rejected in favor of the signed index — predating that decision, never corrected. Both now say "signed index," matching `5.g.iii.zi`'s already-correct checklist wording ("sourced from the verified-developer flag in the Console's signed index") and the actually-built `5.g.i.zi` reader.
- **Action for whoever provisions `5.f.i.zi`:** create a new, dedicated Supabase project for D-Store. Do not reuse `ZEALOT_DATABASE_URL` or any Zealot-issued connection string — that project belongs to the Console's own Rails schema and must stay that way for the F-Droid-style read-only/write-owned split to hold.

What follows from that:
- No GitHub Actions compile/publish pipeline in this repo, and no Telegram drive or Cloudflare Worker as binary storage. The leaves that assumed them are marked `[-]` (superseded) or held; none were deleted, and none had been started.
- The Console writes listing metadata to Supabase and hands this repo a pointer to the Zealot-held APK. The shared contract (`5.g.i.zo`) has to carry that pointer.
- The download button links to Zealot's stable download route, never a stored signed storage URL (those expire). **This is an assumption the product owner has not confirmed** — confirm before `5.g.ii.zi`.
- Sections 7 and 8 of `docs/D-STORE.md` were updated to match.

**Resolved — catalog contract (product owner approved this direction, after researching how Google Play, Apple, Huawei, Samsung and F-Droid split console from store):** the model is Play Console (Zealot) and Play Store (this repo). The Console **publishes a signed, versioned catalog index** — F-Droid-style — and this repo only **reads** it. That replaces the earlier plan of the Console writing catalog rows into Supabase, and settles both of the old open questions (the field contract, and how the Console writes) with one artifact.

- **The index** carries everything the storefront shows for an app: listing text, icon and screenshot references with a SHA-256 for every file, versions, the pointer to the Zealot-held APK (stable download URL, SHA-256, size, signing fingerprint), publisher name, and the verified-developer flag. The Console signs it with an index-signing key that is separate from the APK-signing key. It is published atomically with a strictly increasing timestamp, so a reader never sees a half-written or older index.
- **This repo's side:** fetch the index, verify its signature and timestamp, verify every referenced file by SHA-256, cache the result in `storage/downloads` (Section 3), and read only from that cache. `lib/catalog.ts` remains the seam; its internals read the cached index rather than Supabase for catalog data.
- **Supabase's job narrows** to data the store itself owns: ratings, reviews, view/install counters, abuse reports. Play works the same way — users write reviews on the store, and the developer console only reads and replies. Catalog and developer/agreement metadata no longer live in Supabase.
- The index schema is defined on the Console side (Zealot Task 27a); `5.g.i.zo` here is this repo's review of it as the consumer.

Still open, and not decided here: where the Console publishes the index (its own endpoint, object storage, or a static host); whether D-Store's admin and editorial tools (`2.b.iii.zo` report queue, `3.c`) move to the Console's admin, as Google runs the equivalents on its own side (they are done leaves, nothing was changed); how the Console reads and replies to reviews (this repo would need to expose them); and staged rollout, since a no-account store has no stable device identity to hash.

**Resolved — catalog sources (product owner, this session): the store is populated through the Aptoide MCP, and Zealot's apps are always seen first.** Two sources feed the storefront:
- **First-party:** Zealot's signed catalog index (Zealot Tasks 27, 29). Verified, org-signed, downloaded from Zealot's stable route.
- **Third-party:** Aptoide, through its MCP. Breadth of catalog, not verified by us.

Rules that follow (recorded now, built in `5.h`):
- **Home page:** a first-party section is always first, and the hero prefers a first-party app. If Zealot has no live apps yet, the section is omitted (no empty shell) and Aptoide content fills the page. Shelves below rank first-party ahead of third-party.
- **Merge:** the same package in both sources shows only the Zealot entry.
- **Trust labelling:** third-party apps are labelled "Third-party (via Aptoide)", download from Aptoide's own delivery, and never show Zealot-derived claims: no Verified developer badge, no org signing-fingerprint "Verify this APK" (`0.f.i`). Fields Aptoide does not provide (data safety, content rating and the like) render "Not provided", not an invented default, following the honesty rule `0.j.iii` already set for dummy data.
- **Ingestion:** a server-side job calls the MCP on a schedule and snapshots the result into `storage/downloads`. Never per-request calls, the same cache rule as Section 3. MCP servers are built for AI clients, so this repo should treat it as a data source to snapshot, not as a runtime dependency.

**Open ❓ (do not guess):** (1) which Aptoide MCP: the official `Aptoide/aptoide-mcp` (Python, MIT, listed on Aptoide's GitHub) or the third-party "Aptoide Ultimate API" actor on Apify (pay-per-query, hosted by Apify); neither was inspected beyond its public listing. (2) Aptoide's terms for re-presenting its catalog and linking to its downloads: not checked. (3) Ranking outside the home page: recommendation is relevance first, first-party wins ties, origin always labelled. (4) How third-party apps get region availability and counters. `5.h.ii.zo` stays held until (1) and (2) are answered.

**Priority override — first-party-first home page (`5.h.i`, new):** direct product-owner rule, same mechanism as the `0.h`/`0.i`/`0.j` overrides: **Current position moves to `5.h.i.zi`.** It is buildable now on dummy data through the `lib/catalog.ts` seam, like Phase 0, with no backend needed. This is a sequencing choice made by the session that recorded the rule; the product owner can move it.

**Cross-repo review with Zealot — recommended answers (session recommendations, awaiting product-owner confirmation; no leaf status was changed on their basis except the holds and additions named below):**
1. **Index fields (`5.g.i.zo`) — field-by-field review, re-checked this session against the real `5.g.i.zi` reader** (`lib/sources/zealot.ts`) rather than left as a static comparison, since that reader landed between this review's first pass and this update. Confirmed compatible: `id`/`package_name`/`listing_status`, `publisher.name`, `listing.title`, `slug`, `category` (the five-list here matches v2's enum exactly), `available_regions`, `editorial.featured`/`editors_pick` ↔ this repo's `is_featured`/`is_editors_pick`, `data_safety.collects_data`/`data_types`/`shared_with_third_parties`, `contains_ads`/`has_in_app_purchases`, and `publisher.bio`/`profile_url`/`joined_at` ↔ `Developer.bio`/`profile_url`/`joined_at`. `install_count`/`view_count`/`avg_rating`/`rating_count` reconfirmed correctly excluded (store-owned); `primary_color`/`secondary_color`/`tertiary_color` and the third-party-only fields (`origin`, `not_provided`, `developer_name`/`website`) are correctly this repo's own, not index gaps.
   **Found a real bug in Zealot's serializer (not just a documented mismatch), now fixed on their side:** `lib/sources/zealot.ts` types the per-version `changelog` as `string | null` and calls `.trim()` on it directly — Zealot's `Release#changelog` is a jsonb array of `{message}` hashes, and their serializer was passing that through unconverted, which would have thrown on the first real release with a changelog entry. This repo's reader needed no change (it was already coded correctly against the *intended* contract); see the `5.g.i.zo` leaf entry below for what changed on Zealot's side.
   **Two earlier "requested changes" turned out to be non-issues**, resolved by reading this repo's own reader rather than guessing: `data_safety.encrypted_in_transit`/`deletion_request_url` are both already normalized inline (lines ~135-136 of `lib/sources/zealot.ts`) into this repo's `data_encrypted_in_transit`/`can_request_data_deletion` shape — not mismatches needing a decision.
   **Still open:**
   - `sponsored_slots[]` shape mismatch: v2 nests it per-app (`{starts_at, ends_at}`, not read by this reader yet); this repo's `SponsoredSlot` is a standalone entity with its own `name`/`summary`/dates, not tied to any app slug. Two different products — needs a decision before Zealot's 31a builds real authoring for this.
   - `content_rating`: v2's schema allows any string or null; this repo's `ContentRating` is a closed five-value union, and this reader already defensively maps anything unrecognized to `"Adults only 18+"` (its own most-conservative fallback — confirmed by reading `mapContentRating`). Recommend v2 adopt this repo's exact union as an enum, same pattern already used for `category`, so a malformed rating is schema-rejected rather than silently reinterpreted.
   - `collections[]`: no `Collection` concept exists in this repo yet at all — both sides equally unbuilt, not a mismatch.
   - Naming (confirmed working, not blocking): `versions[].size_bytes`→`size_mb`, `versions[].sha256`→`sha256_checksum`, `versions[].signing_fingerprint`→`signing_certificate_fingerprint`, `compatibility.min_sdk`→`"API <n>"` (not yet a human version name) are all mapped inline in this reader already.
   Recorded in the same pass on Zealot's side (`handover.md`'s Task 29 entry, `29d`) — a different repo, delivered as its own separate patch per that repo's own handoff process.
2. **Vercel has no persistent runtime disk** — resolved as of `5.g.i.zi`/`5.g.iv.zo` above, with two known gaps (SHA-256 not re-verified against the actual binary at read time; anti-rollback state not yet persistent across Vercel cold starts pending `5.g.iv.zi`'s build-time snapshot) rather than the build-time-snapshot design originally recommended here.
3. **Key trust:** resolved as of `5.g.iv.zo` above — pinned key `key_id dbfa9202d7894165`, rotation supported but not yet exercised against a real second key.
4. **Admin tools (Zealot ❓6):** editorial controls move to Zealot and arrive in the index (`5.g.v.zi`); traffic, searches and reports need a read-only, token-authenticated endpoint here (`5.g.v.zo`); moderation actions stay in a small authenticated admin here so Zealot never writes to this repo. `3.c.iii.zi`/`zo` are held. The current `/admin/*` and `/api/admin/*` routes are unauthenticated by design and are deployed on Vercel: gate or remove them before real data exists (`3.c.iv.zi`).
5. **Reviews (Zealot ❓4):** `Review` holds only `id`, `app_slug`, `stars`, `created_at`; there is no text to reply to. Owners get aggregates only until text reviews exist, and those need moderation and throttling (`5.d.ii.zo`) first.
6. **`1.a` reconciliation:** option (b), leave the merged Doctrine leaves. The two prepared SQL migrations alter an `application` table that no longer exists in Supabase's plan (catalog fields now come from the index), so they should be deleted, and `5.f.i.zo` should be superseded. Not done here; awaiting confirmation.
7. **`5.g.ii.zi`:** confirm the download link to Zealot's stable route. Caveat from Zealot's code: that route sends people to a login page if the release's channel is password-protected, so store apps need a public channel.
8. **Geolocation in production:** replace ipapi.co (free tier is about 1,000 lookups a day) with Vercel's country request header in the existing middleware, keeping ipapi.co only as a development fallback. No leaf added yet.
9. **Held leaves** `5.a.iii.zo`, `5.b.iii`, `5.e.ii`/`5.e.iii`, `5.f.ii`, `5.f.iii`: Zealot's index v1 publishes a whole APK from its own route, with no Telegram or Cloudflare edge and no chunking, so these can be superseded. Not changed here; awaiting confirmation.
10. **Staged rollout:** skipped (Zealot decision); no leaf here depends on it.

**Play-parity program:** Zealot's `handover.md` Tasks 28–36 lay out the full Play Console / Play Store mirror. This repo's leaves in it are `5.c.i` (the Updater's store side), `5.g.iv`, `5.g.v` and `5.h`.

**Priority override — UI revamp first:** before any further backend/infra work, the storefront UI gets rebuilt end to end on dummy data and deployed to Vercel for real-time preview. See the new **Phase 0** at the top of Section 2. It pulls forward the UI-facing leaves from Phase 1 (`1.b`–`1.d`) and Phase 2 (`2.a`–`2.c`), so those original leaves are marked superseded rather than duplicated — check them off once their Phase 0 counterpart ships. Backend/infra sequencing (starting at `5.f.i.zi`, provision Supabase) resumes once Phase 0 is complete.

**Repo layout (update, part of `0.a.i.zi`):** the Next.js app now lives at the **repo root** (`package.json`, `app/`, `next.config.mjs`, `tsconfig.json`), not a `frontend/` subdirectory — this lets Vercel auto-detect the framework on import with zero manual configuration, permanently, not just on the first import. The old Symfony app (`app/`, `src/`, `web/`, `composer.json`) was moved into **`legacy-symfony/`** to make room; its internal relative paths (composer autoload, kernel bootstrap, parameters file) are all resolved relative to `legacy-symfony/composer.json` itself, so nothing inside it needed to change. Their internal Linux server deployment for the Symfony app should point at `legacy-symfony/` going forward.

**Scope addition — Geo-regionalization foundation (`0.h`, new):** future sessions need to build region-aware catalogs (Play-Store-style per-country listings/availability) on top of the real backend once it exists. To make that possible without a rewrite later, Phase 0 gains a new `0.h` track: a global **ipapi.co** IP-geolocation client, wired in now while the rest of the UI is still dummy-data-only.

- This is a deliberate, single, narrow exception to Phase 0's "no real backend calls" rule stated at the top of Section 2 — ipapi.co is locale/geolocation detection, not catalog/store data, so it doesn't reintroduce a Supabase/Telegram/Doctrine dependency. The catalog itself stays on `lib/mock-data.ts` (plus a new dummy `available_regions` field) until Phase 5 lands real data.
- **ipapi.co's free tier is explicitly not meant for production** — up to ~1,000 lookups/day (~30K/month), positioned by ipapi.co as a testing/evaluation plan, not a deployment plan. The client (`0.h.i.zi`) must (a) fail soft to a default region on any error/429/timeout — never block rendering — and (b) cache the detected region (e.g. a cookie or edge-cached response) so it's looked up once per visitor session, not on every request. Moving to a paid ipapi.co plan, or swapping the client for a Vercel/Cloudflare edge geolocation header instead, is an open question for whoever picks up production-readiness — noted here, not decided.
- Placed as `0.h` (after `0.g`) rather than resequenced to the front: nothing already planned in `0.a`–`0.g` depends on it, so it doesn't jump the queue — it just needs to land sometime during Phase 0 so the leaves after Phase 0 (real backend, Supabase) can build region-aware queries against a catalog shape that already has a `available_regions` field and a working region-detection hook.

**Priority override — Category-adaptive theming, "Contextual Skins" (`0.i`, new):** direct product-owner request, same weight as the original "UI revamp first" override above, so it jumps ahead the same way: **Current position moves to `0.i.i.zi` below, ahead of `3.b.i.zi`.** `3.b` and everything after it resumes once `0.i` is complete, the same "resumes once X is complete" pattern the original UI-revamp override used for Phase 5.

The direction: the storefront should feel different depending on *what* the visitor is looking at, not just *when* (dark vs. light) — the Play Store is the explicit reference point for this whole rebuild (docs/D-STORE.md), so this is framed as a mirroring feature, not a departure from it. Concretely: opening a finance/banking-type app should read as dense, professional, and moneyed — a "Vault" register (deep greens/charcoals, a metallic-gold accent, a heavier wealth-signaling gradient) — while opening a Bible/scripture/reflective-reading-type app should read as calm and serene — a "Sanctuary" register (soft cloud-blues, pale lavenders, airy rather than dense). Both registers still work in both dark mode and light mode, and — the part a naive implementation would miss — **a category's dark-mode mood and its light-mode mood are allowed, expected, to diverge in what they lean on, not just invert brightness.** "Vault" dark can lean on near-black plus glinting gold, the way `0.b`'s "Cinematic Gold" dark theme already does; "Vault" light should lean on crisp paper-white plus deep emerald/navy ink, not a lightened copy of the same gold. "Sanctuary" light leans on soft daylight cloud-blues; "Sanctuary" dark should lean on deep indigo/starlight-on-navy, not a darkened copy of the same pale blue. So this is a second, orthogonal token axis layered on top of `0.b`'s existing dark/light axis, not a per-category recolor of it.

- Scope stays Phase-0-shaped even though Phase 0 was already closed out by `0.h.ii.zo`: category theme definitions are data (a typed table alongside `lib/mock-data.ts`), applied via scoped CSS custom properties — never a global override of the header/footer/nav chrome, and never a real backend call. A category with no defined register must fall back cleanly to the plain `0.b` tokens rather than rendering unstyled — new categories get added routinely and must never be a blocking dependency for a theme designer.
- The catalog's current dummy categories (`lib/mock-data.ts`: System, Multimedia, Games, Internet, Navigation, Science & Education, Theming, Time, Reading, Writing, Development) don't yet include a Finance/Banking category or a named Bible/scripture app — `0.i.i.zo` below adds representative dummy entries for both, specifically so "Vault"/"Sanctuary" have something real to attach to and preview.
- Every leaf here is `[ ]` open — none of this is built yet, only scoped and prioritized.

**Priority override — Play Store Parity Pass (`0.j`, new):** direct product-owner feedback: several already-shipped screens are thinner than the real Google Play Store equivalent they're meant to rival, specifically around trust/social-proof signals near the primary Install action. Same weight as the `0.h`/`0.i` overrides, same mechanism: **Current position moves to `0.j.i.zi` below, ahead of resuming `0.i.ii.zi`.** `0.i.ii` and everything gated behind it resumes once `0.j` is complete.

Grounded in a live check of current (2026) Play Store behavior rather than guesswork:
- The app detail-page hero is Play's densest trust-signal moment: icon, verified-developer mark, name, star rating + review count + install count, an Editors' Choice ribbon where earned, and age/content rating — all above the fold, all before the Install button, not buried in a lower section. (Source: live product research, Sept 2026.)
- Play's Install control itself is a pill-shaped Material You button (already matched by `InstallButton`, `3.a.iv.zi`/`zo` — that piece is not being redone) that swaps to an **Open** primary action once installed, with **Uninstall** reachable from the same control rather than being a dead end. (Source: Android Authority/9to5Google APK teardowns, 2022–2026.)
- Long listings get a persistent/sticky install affordance so the action never scrolls out of reach. (Source: Android Authority, Oct 2024 teardown — rolling out.)

This repo already has real pieces of this (`Hero.tsx` on the home page already renders rating/review/install-count; `RatingSummary` already does a Play-accurate fractional-star histogram; `AppCard` already has an Editors' Pick badge) — `0.j` is specifically about carrying that same rigor onto the one screen that's currently thinner than its own home-page hero: the app detail page header.

- 0.j.i — App detail page header, trust signals
  - [x] 0.j.i.zi — Add a compact stats row to the app-detail header (`app/app/[slug]/page.tsx`), next to/above the Install button: star rating + review count + install count (reuse `Hero.tsx`'s existing formatting convention rather than inventing a new one) and an Editors' Choice ribbon when `app.is_editors_pick` (reuse `AppCard`'s existing badge convention) — closing the gap where this page currently shows less trust signal than the home page's own hero for the same app — also added a size/version/min-Android line beside the Install button (`{size_mb} MB · v{version} · Android {min_android_version}+`), matching Play's pattern of surfacing that info right next to the install decision rather than only in a lower section. `page.module.css`: new `.stats`/`.rating`/`.statMuted`/`.badge` rules copy `Hero.module.css`/`AppCard.module.css`'s existing values verbatim rather than inventing a third variant of the same rating-pill/badge look. `next build` passes clean.
  - [x] 0.j.i.zo — Post-install state: replace the current dead-end checkmark-only `installed` state (`InstallButton.tsx`) with a primary **Open** action, plus an **Uninstall** affordance reachable from the same control (overflow or secondary action) — same "no real APK/launch, click-to-simulate" dummy convention the button already uses, just carrying it one state further per Play's actual current install-control pattern — implemented as a secondary action (not an overflow menu): once `installed`, the component now renders a `.group` of two pills — a primary "Open" button that falls through to the same accent-filled `.button` style `idle`'s "Install" uses (matches Play's pattern of Open being the next primary action, not a muted end state; its click handler is intentionally a no-op since there's no real APK to launch) and a new secondary `.uninstallButton` (outlined, quieter) whose click resets state back to `idle` — the actual state transition this leaf adds, replacing the old permanent dead end. `next build` passes clean.
- 0.j.ii — Long-listing install accessibility
  - [x] 0.j.ii.zi — Sticky/anchored install action for long app-detail listings, so Install/Open/Update never scrolls out of reach on a long description/screenshot/permissions page — `components/StickyInstallBar.tsx` + `.module.css`, a fixed bottom bar watching the header's `#primary-install-row` via a plain `IntersectionObserver` (native-API preference this repo already follows) and revealing only once that row has scrolled above the viewport. Renders its own `InstallButton` instance, kept in sync with the header's via `lib/install-status.ts`'s same-tab change event. Reveal/pin transition (slide+fade) gated behind `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)`, instant show/hide otherwise — same convention every other animated leaf here follows. Wired into `app/app/[slug]/page.tsx` below the Similar Apps rail. `next build` passes clean; manually verified via `next start` that `/app/f-droid` and `/` render 200 with the new sentinel id and SVG icons present.

**Session note (out-of-scope additions, same session as `0.j.ii.zi`, direct product-owner request — not a formal leaf, logged here for traceability):**
- **Lively dummy icons** — `components/AppIcon.tsx`, a generated gradient+glyph SVG tile (multi-stop gradient from each app's own `primary_color`/`secondary_color`/`tertiary_color`, a subtle gloss highlight, the initial as glyph) replacing the flat single-color initial tiles in `AppCard`, `Hero`, `CategoryCard`, `SponsoredCard`, and the app-detail header. Still 100% local/generated — no external image fetch, no `/mock/...` paths that would 404 — swapping in real `<img>` icons later still only touches this one file plus each call site's existing icon-container `className`.
- **Device-aware install status** — `lib/install-status.ts`, a `localStorage`-backed per-app install record read/written via a new `useInstallStatus` hook, layered onto `InstallButton.tsx` (3.a.iv.zi/zo, 0.j.i.zo). Renders "Install" with no record, "Open"/"Uninstall" when the recorded version matches the catalog version, or "Update"/"Uninstall" when the recorded version is older — derived, not separately tracked state, so a catalog version bump flips a previously up-to-date app to "Update" on its own. See that module's header comment for why this is a device-local simulated record rather than a true OS-level install query: there's no general browser API for a site to enumerate arbitrary installed apps (a deliberate platform privacy boundary), and the one narrow real exception (`navigator.getInstalledRelatedApps()`) only pairs a site with *its own* manifest-declared app via Digital Asset Links, not arbitrary sideloaded APKs — so it doesn't apply to a multi-app catalog like this one. Both `InstallButton` instances for the same app (header + `StickyInstallBar`, `0.j.ii.zi`) stay in sync via a same-tab custom event. `next build` passes clean.
- 0.j.iii — Disclosure gaps found by a full audit against live Play Store (new, added mid-track — see below)

**Audit note, `0.j.iii`:** direct product-owner request to cross-check the *entire* live Play Store surface (nav, detail page, developer/"company" page, settings) against this repo's full plan, not just the Install-area gaps `0.j.i`/`0.j.ii` already cover. Cross-referenced against both `docs/D-STORE.md`'s complete feature framework (Section 4, A–G) and every leaf already in this file — most of what a naive audit would flag is already scoped (developer/"company" pages exist today, `0.g.iii.zo`; account-only Play Store menu items — Library & devices, Payments, Play Pass, My Play activity — are correctly *excluded* since `docs/D-STORE.md` §3 rules out accounts entirely, substituting local-only `4.d` favorites instead of just dropping the feature). Three genuine blind spots turned up — zero mentions anywhere in `docs/D-STORE.md` or this file before this note (confirmed by search):

- **Content/age rating** (e.g. "Everyone" / "Teen" / "Mature 17+") — Play Store shows this prominently in the detail-page hero, alongside the rating/review/install stats `0.j.i.zi` just added. Not in the original data-model field list (`docs/D-STORE.md` §5) at all.
- **"Data safety" section** — what data an app collects/shares and its security practices. Distinct from the `permissions[]` disclosure already built (`0.f.ii.zo`) — permissions are the Android runtime grants; data safety is Play's separate privacy-nutrition-label-style disclosure of what's actually done with data. Given `docs/D-STORE.md`'s whole "trust has to be built by the store itself" framing (§0), this is arguably a bigger miss than most.
- **"Contains ads" / "In-app purchases" indicator** — a small disclosure line/badge Play shows near the install decision. Ties into the existing `0.j`/Section 4A ads track but nothing currently flags *per-app* whether the app itself shows ads or sells IAP, only that D-Store's own shelves might.

Also flagged, lower-severity: no general "About D-Store" / Help / FAQ page distinct from the Privacy/Terms/DMCA legal pages (`0.c.iii`) — Play Store's Help Center is a real, separate surface from its legal pages.

  - [x] 0.j.iii.zi — Add `content_rating` (e.g. "Everyone", "Teen", "Mature 17+") to the `App` data model (`lib/mock-data.ts`) as a plausible dummy value per app, alongside the existing §5 field list, and surface it in the detail-page header stats row (`0.j.i.zi`) next to the star rating — new exported `ContentRating` type (`"Everyone" | "Everyone 10+" | "Teen" | "Mature 17+" | "Adults only 18+"`, Play Store's actual tiers) plus a required `content_rating: ContentRating` field on `App`. All 15 apps in this catalog are genuinely `"Everyone"` — FOSS wallpaper/theming/utility/reading apps, nothing here plausibly warrants a higher tier — so unlike `available_regions` (`0.h.ii.zi`), this isn't introducing dummy variety; the value is uniform because that's honest, not because variety wasn't considered. The full typed vocabulary still exists so a future catalog addition that *does* need a higher tier has a real value to reach for. `app/app/[slug]/page.tsx`'s `.stats` row gains one more `<span className={styles.statMuted}>{app.content_rating}</span>`, positioned right after the star rating — reuses the existing `.statMuted` class verbatim, no new CSS, matching this whole `0.j` track's "reuse the existing formatting convention rather than inventing a new one" discipline. `next build` passes clean (TypeScript's required-field check is itself an integrity check — all 15 app literals satisfying it confirms none were missed); manually verified via `next start` that `/app/f-droid` renders "Everyone" in the stats row and the two newer catalog entries (`/app/ledger-vault`, `/app/quiet-verse`, added by `0.i.i.zo`) both still return 200 with the new required field present.
  - [x] 0.j.iii.zo — "Data safety" section on the app detail page: what data (if any) the app collects/shares and its security practices, as a new dummy-data field set distinct from the existing `permissions[]` disclosure (`0.f.ii.zo`) — own section, not folded into Permissions, since Play treats them as genuinely separate disclosures. `lib/mock-data.ts` gains a `DataSafetyInfo` type (`collects_data`, `data_types: string[]`, `shared_with_third_parties`, `data_encrypted_in_transit`, `can_request_data_deletion`) plus a required `data_safety: DataSafetyInfo` field on `App`. `shared_with_third_parties` is `false` for all 15 apps on purpose — no app in this FOSS-only catalog ships an ad SDK or analytics vendor, so there's no honest way to model real third-party sharing; `collects_data` genuinely varies though (`true` for `f-droid`, `battery-live`, `night-mode-enabler`, `mincal-widget`, `ledger-vault`, `quiet-verse` — each reads something purely on-device to do its actual job, e.g. Battery Live reading battery stats — `false` for the remaining 9 theming/icon/font apps that read nothing). New `components/DataSafety.tsx` + `.module.css`: same always-visible-callout shell `PermissionsDisclosure` (0.f.ii.zo)/`PlayStoreDisclosure` (0.f.ii.zi) already use (`.wrapper`/`.label`/`.list`/`.item`/`.code`/`.empty` copied verbatim), plus one new `.facts` rule for the three always-shown yes/no lines below the data-types list — unlike `PermissionsDisclosure`'s "swap the whole list for one sentence" empty state, here every fact line renders regardless of `collects_data`, only the data-types list itself swaps for "This app collects no data." Wired into `app/app/[slug]/page.tsx` as its own `<section>` right after Permissions, before Report a Problem. `next build` passes clean; manually verified via `next start` (on a genuinely fresh server after an earlier stale-process false negative) that `/app/f-droid` renders the "App info and performance" data type plus all three fact lines, `/app/simply-solid` renders the "collects no data" empty state, and `/app/ledger-vault`/`/app/quiet-verse` both still return 200.
  - [x] 0.j.iv.zi — Per-app "Contains ads" / "In-app purchases" indicator near the Install button, sourced from new dummy `App` fields, distinct from D-Store's own shelf-level Sponsored cards (Section 4A) — new `contains_ads`/`has_in_app_purchases` boolean fields on `App` (`lib/mock-data.ts`), uniformly `false` across all 15 apps: unlike `available_regions`, this isn't a case for inventing narrower dummy variety — F-Droid's real inclusion criteria prohibit ads and non-free/anti-features dependencies outright, so a catalog entry with either field `true` would misrepresent what this catalog actually is, the same honesty-over-invented-variety reasoning `content_rating`/`shared_with_third_parties` already established. New `components/MonetizationDisclosure.tsx` + `.module.css`: renders `null` when both are `false` (matching real Play Store — no reassurance tag for the common case, only a disclosure tag when needed), otherwise a `.installMeta`-styled line joining whichever labels are `true` with " · ". Wired into `app/app/[slug]/page.tsx` directly below the install row (`.installRow`), after the size/version/Android line. Because no real catalog entry can honestly exercise the "renders something" branch, that branch was verified separately from the live app: a faithful reimplementation run through `react-dom/server`'s `renderToStaticMarkup` outside Next's build pipeline, confirming all four cases — `both false → ""`, `ads only → "Contains ads"`, `IAP only → "In-app purchases"`, `both true → "Contains ads · In-app purchases"`. `next build` passes clean (TypeScript's required-field check confirms all 15 literals were updated, not just some); manually verified via `next start` that `/app/f-droid` renders the real `contains_ads: false, has_in_app_purchases: false` data and correctly shows neither tag.
  - [x] 0.j.iv.zo — General "About D-Store" / Help & FAQ page, distinct from the existing legal pages (`0.c.iii`) — mission/what-this-is copy plus pointers to the anonymous Report flow (`0.f.iii.zi`) and RSS feed (`4.c.i.zi`), closing out `0.j` (Play Store Parity Pass) in full. `app/about/page.tsx` + `page.module.css`, reusing the privacy/terms prose-page shell (`2.d.i.zi`/`zo`) verbatim plus one new `.faqItem` rule for tighter Q&A spacing. Content is grounded in real, already-shipped features, not invented placeholder questions: checksum/signature verification (`0.f.i`), the Permissions-vs-Data-safety distinction (`0.f.ii.zo`/`0.j.iii.zo`), regional availability (`0.h`), and the real `Review`-vs-`ReportFlag` rate-limiting asymmetry (`1.a.iii.zi`/`zo`) each get their own FAQ answer. The Report flow is described rather than linked as a route, since `ReportAppForm.tsx` lives on each app's own page, not a standalone URL; the RSS feed is linked as a forward reference (`/feed.xml`, not built until `4.c.i.zi`), same pattern `Footer.tsx` already used. `components/Footer.tsx` gains an "About" link in its nav, placed first — caught and fixed a real staleness bug while touching that file: its header comment claimed `/privacy`/`/terms`/`/dmca` "don't exist as real pages yet," which was true when `0.c.iii.zi` wrote it but has been false since `2.d.i.zi`/`zo`/`2.d.ii.zo` shipped; corrected to reflect that only `/feed.xml` (and, until this commit, `/about`) remain genuine forward references — `/search`/`/categories`' similarly stale claim in `Header.tsx` was left alone as out of scope for this leaf. The nav's `aria-label` changed from `"Legal"` to `"Site"` since About isn't a legal page. `next build` passes clean (`/about` now a real route); manually verified via `next start` that the page renders the correct `<title>`, that all its cross-links (`/privacy`, `/terms`, `/dmca`, `/feed.xml`) resolve to the expected paths, and that the footer's new `/about` link appears on the home page.

**Session note (out-of-scope visual/animation requests, direct product-owner request, not formal leaves — logged as `0.j.v` for traceability):**
- **`0.j.v.zi` — Circular wavy/crinkled install-progress ring.** `InstallButton`'s own linear `.fill` bar was a reasonable web-native stand-in, but the product owner specifically wanted parity with the real Play Store *Android app*: a ring wrapped around the app's own icon. Web-searched to confirm the current design rather than guess — per a September 2026 Android Authority report, current Play Store builds (Material 3 Expressive) replaced the old plain circular progress ring around app icons with a "crinkled" (scalloped/wavy-edged) ring that sweeps clockwise as install/download progresses. Implemented as `components/WavyProgressRing.tsx` (a closed sine-modulated-radius SVG path, `pathLength={100}` so `stroke-dasharray`/`stroke-dashoffset` work in plain percentage units, `.sweep` animating dashoffset 100→0 over the same `SIMULATED_INSTALL_MS` `InstallButton`'s own timeout uses, plus a slow continuous rotation for the "curling" motion) and `components/AppIconLive.tsx` (wraps `AppIcon` with the ring, driven by a new small transient pub-sub in `lib/install-status.ts` — `setInstallProgressActive`/`useInstallProgress`, a plain in-module `Map` + same-tab custom event, deliberately not `localStorage`-persisted since a reload mid-"install" shouldn't resume a ring for a real download that isn't actually happening). `InstallButton.tsx` now imports the shared `SIMULATED_INSTALL_MS` constant (moved to `lib/install-status.ts` as the single source of truth) instead of declaring its own, and calls `setInstallProgressActive(appSlug, true/false)` at the start/end of its existing `runSimulatedInstall`. Wired into the app-detail header and `StickyInstallBar` (both sit next to a live `InstallButton`); `AppCard`/`Hero`'s grid/featured icons stay on plain `AppIcon` since nothing there triggers an install. Reduced-motion: the ring doesn't render at all, same "no substitute animation" posture every other decorative animation here takes.
- **`0.j.v.zo` — Hero card redesign (rectangular, blended glassmorphism, light flare).** Direct product-owner report that the home-page featured-app hero (F-Droid, via `getFeaturedApps()`) was "too big" and read as a loud solid-color banner sitting *on* the page rather than a glass surface blended *into* it. `Hero.module.css` rewritten: the glass panel (`.panel`, `color-mix(...var(--color-surface)...) ` + `backdrop-filter: blur()`) now covers the *entire* card instead of just a bottom strip over a solid gradient fill, so the page's own background shows through the whole thing; the inline per-app background (`Hero.tsx`) went from a bold saturated two-color gradient fill to a soft low-opacity radial glow hinting at the app's own palette. Card height dropped from `min-height: min(70vh, 560px)` (near-viewport-height) to a compact rectangular banner sized by its own padded content. Added `.flare`/`.flare::before` — a soft diagonal highlight band with `mix-blend-mode: overlay`, sweeping left-to-right on a 6.5s loop (`prefers-reduced-motion`-gated, doesn't render at all under reduced motion, same posture as the wavy ring above) — the "glossy sheen catching the light" motif real frosted-glass surfaces use. `next build` passes clean; manually verified via `next start` that `/` renders 200 with the new compact `.panel` markup.

**Priority override — Light theme corrections (`0.b.iv`, new):** direct product-owner report: "the light mode the text isn't visible and it's not having the futuristic feel as the dark mode." Same weight as the `0.h`/`0.i`/`0.j` overrides, same mechanism: **Current position moves to `0.b.iv.zi` below, ahead of resuming `3.b.iii.zo`.** `3.b.iii` and everything after it resumes once `0.b.iv` is complete.

Two distinct problems bundled in one report, both real:
- **An actual bug, not a design opinion:** `body` (`app/globals.css`) and `Hero.module.css`'s `.name`/`.summary`/`.metaMuted` were hardcoded to `#f2ece0` — the *dark* theme's off-white — instead of running off a token like every other color in this file does. Dark mode never exposed this since `#f2ece0` reads fine on near-black. Light mode renders that exact same near-white on a near-white background: contrast ~1.05:1, nowhere close to the 4.5:1 AA floor every other pairing in this file already clears and documents. This is what "the text isn't visible" is describing.
- **A design call the product owner is now overriding:** `0.b.ii.zi`'s original note defended light theme's flat, gradient-less background as "clinical precision," explicitly "not an oversight." That defense doesn't survive this feedback — reversed here, not re-argued.

**0.b.iv — Light theme corrections** *(new — see "Priority override" note above.)*
  - [x] 0.b.iv.zi — Fix the text-contrast bug: add a themed `--color-text` token (dark keeps the existing `#f2ece0` value unchanged; light gets its own deep near-black, not a lightened copy of dark's off-white, matching `0.i`'s "must genuinely diverge" rule for anything theme-adjacent) and point `body`/`Hero.module.css` at it instead of the hardcoded literal. WCAG AA re-check required for the new light value, same relative-luminance method `0.b.ii.zo` used — `--color-text: #12151c` vs `--color-bg #f7f8fa`: 17.19:1; vs `--color-surface #ffffff`: 18.26:1 — both comfortably clear AA (4.5:1) and land right alongside dark theme's own text ratios (16.91:1/15.74:1), rather than merely scraping past the floor. `app/globals.css`: `--color-text` added to both `[data-theme]` blocks; `body`'s hardcoded `color: #f2ece0` → `color: var(--color-text)`. `components/Hero.module.css`: the three hardcoded `#f2ece0` occurrences (`.name`, `.summary`, `.metaMuted`) → `var(--color-text)` — same bug, same fix, since that file's own header comment already claimed "everything else here... still runs off those tokens," which wasn't actually true until now. `next build` passes clean; manually verified via `next start` with an explicit `Cookie: d-store-theme=light`/`=dark` header on each request (rather than relying on the default) that `data-theme="light"`/`"dark"` render correctly and that zero occurrences of the literal `#f2ece0` remain anywhere in the light-mode HTML or its compiled CSS — the string appears exactly twice in the built CSS, once as each theme's own `--color-text` *value* (`#f2ece0` for dark, `#12151c` for light), never as a hardcoded property.
  - [x] 0.b.iv.zo — Give light theme its own `--gradient-vignette`, reversing `0.b.ii.zi`'s "flat, crisp base" call: same two-layer glow-plus-vignette shape dark theme uses, built from light theme's own cobalt accent and neutral tones — not a lightened copy of dark's warm gold glow — so both themes read as equally considered rather than one being the "real" theme and the other an afterthought. Implemented alongside `0.b.iv.zi` above (small, tightly-coupled diff to the same file, same report) rather than as a separate session. `app/globals.css`'s light block gains: a cool cobalt radial glow near the top (echoing "Scientific Blue"'s own accent hue, at low opacity — 0.09 — rather than dark theme's 0.10, since a light background needs less to read clearly), a faint 135° diagonal linear-gradient sheen underneath it for a crisp, high-tech feel distinct from dark theme's purely-radial cinematic glow, and a soft neutral highlight fading in from the bottom edge using `--color-border`'s own gray-blue tone rather than dark theme's near-black vignette (fading toward black on a near-white page would read as a dirty smudge, not a vignette — the "must diverge" principle applied to the *shape* of the treatment, not just its colors). The original "clinical precision calls for a flat, crisp base; not an oversight" note is left in place rather than deleted, with this leaf's note appended alongside it — the doc's own append-don't-erase convention (e.g. `3.b.ii.zo`'s "Correction" note) applied to a design decision this time, not just a technical one. `next build` passes clean; manually verified via `next start` that `data-theme="light"` responses render the new three-layer `--gradient-vignette` value on `body`'s computed `background-image` (confirmed present in the compiled CSS output, distinct in both color values and gradient shape from the dark theme's own two-layer value), and that dark mode's existing vignette is byte-for-byte unchanged.

**Priority override — Aptoide source adapter (`5.h.ii.zi`), operator request:** operator asked directly to start removing dummy data from D-Store using real Aptoide data, and supplied a live probe of `ws75.aptoide.com` (`aptoide-probe.txt`) to build against — same mechanism as the `0.h`/`0.i`/`0.j`/`0.b.iv` overrides above: **Current position moved to `5.h.ii.zi`, ahead of resuming `5.h.i.zo`.** `5.h.i.zo` (home-page first-party section) was not touched and remains the next leaf once this thread closes out.

**Resolved — category/genre taxonomy relationship (Zealot↔D-store), modeled on Play Console↔Play Store, this session:** operator asked to mirror how Play Console and Play Store actually relate their taxonomies, using more modern methods than the current design. Researched directly rather than assumed:
- **Current state confirmed as the problem:** D-store's 12 categories (`system, multimedia, games, internet, navigation, science-education, theming, time, reading, writing, development, finance`) are F-Droid's taxonomy verbatim, not Play's — inherited from this project's F-Droid-modeled index/signing design, not chosen to match Play. Play Console's real 2026 taxonomy (`support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9859673`) is 32 app categories (Art & Design, Auto & Vehicles, Beauty, Books & Reference, Business, Comics, Communications, Dating, Education, Entertainment, Events, Finance, Food & Drink, Health & Fitness, House & Home, Libraries & Demo, Lifestyle, Maps & Navigation, Medical, Music & Audio, News & Magazines, Parenting, Personalization, Photography, Productivity, Shopping, Social, Sports, Tools, Travel & Local, Video Players & Editors, Weather) plus a separate Games branch with its own genre list (Action, Adventure, Arcade, Board, Card, Casino, Casual, Educational, Music, Puzzle, Racing, Role Playing, Simulation, Sports, Strategy, Trivia, Word) — Games is not one of the 32, it's a parallel branch.
- **How Play Console actually hands this to Play Store — checked against Google's own current schema, not inferred:** Google's App Catalog Export (`developers.google.com/android-publisher/catalog-export`, `edition = "2024"` protobuf) is the real, current mechanism a Play-side catalog is exported through. Its shape: `AppCategory` is a tiny **closed enum** (`APP_CATEGORY_UNSPECIFIED`, `GAME`, `APP`) that answers only "is this a game," and the actual category (`app_subcategory`, e.g. `"FINANCE"`, `"GAME_ACTION"`) is a **plain string field, not a closed proto enum**. The business vocabulary (which category names exist) is kept out of the wire schema entirely, so Play can add or rename a category without a breaking schema version bump for every downstream catalog-export consumer.
- **What this repo has instead, and why it's the opposite of that:** `zealot`'s `docs/catalog_index_v2.schema.json` bakes `category` in as a **closed JSON Schema `enum`** with `additionalProperties: false` (confirmed by reading that file and `docs/catalog_index_v2.md` directly) — the exact pattern Play's own export deliberately avoids. That schema's own doc already flagged this as unresolved (Zealot `handover.md` Task 29's ❓1: "fixed list vs. Zealot-owned free text... this section and the schema's `category` enum both need to change together") — this decision answers that ❓ by choosing the string-field direction, informed by how Play itself does it, not by guessing.
- **Decision:** mirror Play Console→Play Store's real shape, not just its category names:
  - `app_type`: closed two-value field, `"app" | "game"` — the D-store/Zealot analogue of Play's `AppCategory` enum. Stays tiny and essentially never changes.
  - `category`: becomes a **string**, not a JSON Schema enum — the analogue of Play's `app_subcategory`. Carries either an app category (`"finance"`, `"tools"`, ...) or, when `app_type` is `"game"`, a game genre (`"action"`, `"puzzle"`, ...) — same "genre only makes sense once you know it's a game" split Play's naming convention (`GAME_ACTION`) already encodes.
  - **The vocabulary itself (which strings are valid) moves out of the signed index's schema and into its own small, separately-versioned taxonomy manifest** that Zealot publishes (own `schema_version`/`sequence`, covered by the same signing key) — this is the piece with no Play equivalent to point at directly, since Play keeps its vocabulary in Console's UI/docs rather than a signed artifact, but it's the closest analogue given this repo's whole index is signed, not just exported. Adding "Podcasts" next year is then a taxonomy-manifest publish, not a breaking `catalog_index` schema bump.
  - **D-store's reader is tolerant, not strict, on `category`:** an unrecognized string doesn't reject the app or the index — it renders as "Uncategorized" and the app still ingests. This is the same principle Play's own string-typed (not enum-typed) `app_subcategory` field structurally encodes, and the opposite of today's closed-enum-rejects-the-whole-index posture.
- **What this changes about the current F-Droid-inherited list:** it's replaced, not extended — the 12 current slugs get a migration mapping onto Play's 32 (see leaf below), and `games` is pulled out of the flat list into `app_type: "game"` with Play's 17 genres as its `category` values, matching Play's real branch split instead of treating Games as a 13th peer category.
- **Split into new leaves below (`5.i`), doc-only this session** — no schema or code changed yet. `5.i.i.zo` is Zealot-repo work, tracked here for visibility only (same convention `5.g.i.zo`/`5.g.iv.zo` already use for the Zealot side of a shared contract); this repo doesn't have write access there and isn't assuming it will be picked up on any particular timeline.

**Resolved — sponsored placement & collections shape (Zealot↔D-store), full Play-model parity, operator direction:** operator decided directly, closing the `5.g.v.zi` question left held above: expand both to mirror exactly how the industry giants (Play Console/Play Store) do sponsored placement and collections, rather than either of the placeholder shapes already flagged as mismatched/unbuilt. Researched, not guessed:
- **Real Play sponsored placement is a paid-ads auction, not an authored slot.** Google's actual mechanism, unchanged in substance since its 2015–2017 rollout, is Universal App Campaigns (UAC) inside Google Ads: developers bid, targeting is by keyword/audience/budget (tCPA/tROAS), and the "creative" for the resulting placement — Play homepage, search results, app listing pages — is the app's own existing store listing, not separately authored ad copy.
- **This means Zealot's already-reserved shape (`app.sponsored_slots: [{starts_at, ends_at}]`) is the correct analogue, not D-store's own standalone `SponsoredSlot` entity.** A real Play sponsored placement carries no separate name/summary — the app's own listing *is* the creative — exactly what a bare per-app date window encodes, and exactly what Zealot's own `handover.md` (item 5, cited in the prior session's note above) already pointed at once a decision was made. D-store's `SponsoredSlot` (`id`/`name`/`summary`/dates as a standalone entity, `lib/mock-data.ts`) matches no real Play mechanism and is the piece to retire, not extend.
- **What "mirror exactly" does NOT mean here, flagged rather than silently assumed:** the full real mechanism is a bidding/auction system wired to an ads platform — advertiser accounts, budgets, keyword targeting, impression/click billing. Building that has no analog anywhere in this project (no advertiser accounts, no billing, no ad-serving infrastructure) and is a separate initiative, not this leaf's scope. In scope: the per-app date-window shape Zealot already reserved, read-only here, no separately-authored creative.
- **Real Play collections are editorially curated, named and described theme groupings** — e.g. the rotating "Android Excellence"/Editor's Choice round-ups ("Get Motivated With These 5 Fitness Apps"), refreshed periodically, split by apps/games, with a short editor's note per collection explaining the picks (confirmed against current reporting on how Play/App Store featuring and collections work today).
- **D-store's own `Collection` type (`4.c.ii.zo`) already matches this model almost exactly** — `slug`/`name`/`description`/`app_package_names[]` is the same "named theme + curated app list" shape Play uses. The gap isn't the model, it's that Zealot has no registry to publish it from yet (confirmed last session: a reserved per-app `collections[]` field exists, but no top-level registry). Full Play parity means Zealot's index needs a **new top-level `collections[]` registry** (slug/name/description/curated package list — the Console-authored counterpart to what D-store already models locally); the existing per-app `collections[]` array becomes derived membership in that registry, not separately authored.
- **Split into new leaves below (`5.j`), doc-only this session** — no schema or code changed yet. `5.j.i.zi`/`5.j.i.zo` are Zealot-repo work, tracked here for visibility only, same convention as `5.i.i.zo`/`5.g.i.zo`; this repo doesn't have write access there and isn't assuming it will be picked up on any particular timeline.

**Priority override — Aptoide source adapter (cont.), full dummy-data removal, operator request:** operator asked directly to remove *all* remaining dummy data and populate the store from real Aptoide results, and supplied a second, larger curl batch (`aptoide-batch.txt`) — one real `app/get` response per D-Store category, hand-labelled by category in the file's own `=== <category> :: ...` headers — to ingest and check the response structure against. Same mechanism as the overrides above; does not move Current Position (still `5.h.i.zo` below), since this closes out remaining `5.h.ii` scope rather than opening new sequencing. Done:
- All 13 dummy `"aptoide"`-origin `App` entries (F-Droid + the 12 Theming-shelf apps) removed from `lib/mock-data.ts`'s `apps` array, along with their 13 now-orphaned `Developer` entries — nothing "aptoide"-origin is hardcoded in this file anymore. The 2 `"zealot"` entries (`ledger-vault`, `quiet-verse`) are untouched.
- 11 of the batch's 12 apps ingested into `storage/downloads/aptoide-snapshot.json` (raw `app/get` responses, unmodified), alongside the pre-existing WhatsApp seed from `5.h.ii.zi` — 12 real third-party apps total. The 12th, `com.mojang.minecraftpe` (intended for "games"), came back a genuine Aptoide 404 (`APP-1: Application 'package:com.mojang.minecraftpe' not found`) and was **not** ingested — "games" has zero apps until a real, resolvable package is picked; not backfilled with an invented one, same honesty posture this file already uses elsewhere (`apps`'s own header comment, `available_regions`, etc.).
- `lib/sources/aptoide.ts`: added `CATEGORY_BY_PACKAGE`, a real package-name → category table transcribed from the batch file's own category labels (Aptoide's raw response carries no category field at all — confirmed against every response in the batch, not assumed), and pointed `normalizeAptoideApp`'s `category` field at it, replacing the old hardcoded `"internet"` placeholder this leaf's groundwork note had explicitly deferred. `com.whatsapp` added to the same table (`"internet"`) since it predates this batch and needed a real category too, not just the new 11.
- Verified end-to-end in-sandbox (no live Aptoide network needed — same as `5.h.ii.zi`'s WhatsApp seed, this reads the checked-in snapshot, not a live call): `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both pass clean; `getApps({})` via `npx tsx` returns 14 apps — 2 `zealot`, 12 `aptoide` — each under the correct real category (`termux`→system, `videolabs-vlc`→multimedia, `firefox`→internet alongside `whatsapp`, `waze-...`→navigation, `khan-academy`→science-education, `launcher-dev-team-go`→theming, the alarm/reading/writing/dev/finance apps → their matching categories); `next start` smoke-tested `/`, `/app/termux`, `/app/whatsapp`, `/categories/games` (200, correctly empty), `/categories/system`, `/search?q=firefox` — all 200.
- **Flagged, not fixed here:** removing the 13 dummy developers means `/developer/[slug]` for any of the 12 real third-party apps has no matching `Developer` row (`getDeveloperBySlug` only reads the static `developers` array; Aptoide-origin `developer_slug` is derived from Aptoide's own response, never backed by a static entry) — pre-existing since `5.h.ii.zi`'s WhatsApp seed, not new, and not this leaf's to fix; belongs with `5.h.iii.zi` (third-party trust labelling), which already suppresses other first-party-only UI for third-party apps.
- ❓2 (Aptoide's re-presentation/linking terms) is still genuinely unchecked — unaffected by this leaf, still worth checking before any of this runs at real scale/traffic.

**Priority override — real data everywhere, last dummy data removed (`5.h.iv.zi`), operator request:** operator asked to remove all remaining dummy data and load the store fully from real Aptoide data, and (via the unofficial-route probe, `aptoide-probe.txt`) chose Aptoide's public web API over the official MCP. Both were already true in code by the time this session checked `origin` (the adapter, ingestion script, scheduled workflow, 12 real apps and first-party-first home page had landed from other sessions), so this session did **not** rebuild any of that and instead audited what was still fake or unwired. Same mechanism as the overrides above; does not move Current Position. Done, as leaf `5.h.iv.zi` (below):
- **Found and fixed — half of `lib/catalog.ts` never saw the real apps.** `getFeaturedApps`, `getEditorsPicks`, `getTopFreeApps`, `getNewAndUpdated`, `searchApps`, `getSimilarApps`, `getAppsByDeveloper`, `getCategoryAppCount` (via `mock-data.ts`'s `appCountByCategory`) and `getTrafficSummary` all read the static `apps` array directly instead of `getMergedApps()`. With the 12 Aptoide apps ingested, the chart pages, category counts, "Similar Apps" shelf and developer app lists were still computed from the two dummy first-party entries alone. All now read the merged catalog.
- **Removed the last dummy data:** `ledger-vault` and `quiet-verse` and their two `Developer` rows are gone; `apps` and `developers` in `lib/mock-data.ts` are now empty arrays with the reason written above them. `appCountByCategory` (now unused) deleted.
- **Fixed a flagged 404:** `/developer/[slug]` had no row for any real third-party publisher. `getDeveloperBySlug` now derives one from the merged catalog (`developer_slug`, `developer_name`, and a new `App.developer_website` fed from Aptoide's `developer.website`, only carried through when it is an http(s) URL). `Developer.bio`, `profile_url` and `joined_at` became nullable, and `app/developer/[slug]/page.tsx` omits each line when null instead of inventing a bio, a join year or a dead link.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` clean. Against the real snapshot via `tsx`: `getApps()` returns 12 (all `aptoide`), first-party 0, category counts sum to 12 (games correctly 0), similar-apps/search/new-and-updated/traffic all see the 12, `getDeveloperBySlug("whatsapp-inc")` returns `{name: "WhatsApp Inc.", bio: null, profile_url: "http://www.whatsapp.com/", joined_at: null}` and an unknown slug returns `null`. Smoke-tested with `next start`: `/`, `/app/whatsapp`, `/app/firefox`, `/developer/whatsapp-inc`, `/charts/top-free`, `/charts/new`, `/categories/system`, `/categories/games`, `/categories`, `/search?q=fire` and `/sitemap.xml` all 200, the sitemap now lists all 12 real apps and 12 real developers, and the home page contains no trace of the removed apps. `/admin/traffic` returns 503 locally because `ADMIN_PASSWORD` is unset (the `3.c.iv.zi` gate failing closed, as designed).
- **Flagged, not fixed here:** (1) the counter mutators (`incrementInstallCount`, `incrementViewCount`, rating submission, `setAppFeaturing`) still look up the static `apps` array, which is now empty, so they return "not found" for every app until the product call already flagged under `5.h.ii.zi` is made (do third-party apps get store-native counters at all?). (2) `getTopFreeApps` ranks by store-native `install_count`, which is 0 for every third-party app, so "Top Free" is currently an arbitrary order; `5.h.v.zi` below fixes that with Aptoide's own download numbers, labelled as Aptoide's. (3) Unknown routes (`/app/x`, `/developer/x`, `/categories/x`) return HTTP 200 with the 404 page body (a soft 404); identical on all three, so framework-level and pre-existing, not from this change. (4) The Vault and Sanctuary category themes (`0.i.i.zo`) lost their two invented preview apps; the categories still exist and now preview through the real third-party apps in them (`my-expenses` in finance, `readera` in reading).
- **Audit findings that need their own leaves (real Aptoide responses, not assumptions):** `AptoideRawApp.malware` is typed at the top level of the response but the real path is `file.malware.rank` (nothing reads it yet, so no live bug, but the type is wrong); and `scripts/ingest-aptoide.ts` has **no trust filter**: a keyword search returns third-party-store uploads too (the live `whatsapp` search returned a `catappult` app named "WhatsApp Spy" with `malware.rank: "UNKNOWN"` next to the two `TRUSTED` WhatsApp apps), so scaling coverage by search without a gate would put unvetted APKs in the store. Also unused from the real response: version history (`nodes.versions.list`), Aptoide's real rating average, vote histogram and download counts, MD5 and SHA-1 signing fingerprint, developer email/privacy URL, and the feature graphic. The catalog is also **regional**: Aptoide appended `?country=NG` to a request made from Nigeria, so what a scheduled GitHub Actions run sees depends on the runner's country. `apps/list/sort=downloads7d` and `app/versions/package_name=…` were probed and return 404, so there is no known browse or top-charts endpoint yet: coverage today is only the packages you name.
