Every field pulled from the real `App` shape (`lib/catalog.ts`) — `softwareVersion`/`fileSize`/`license` map directly to their `App` fields, `aggregateRating` only renders when `rating_count > 0`, `applicationCategory` uses the category's real display name (new `getCategoryBySlug` fetch on this page, alongside the existing `getDeveloperBySlug`) rather than its raw slug. `offers` is a flat free/USD offer since every dummy app in the catalog is free — there's no `price` field to read a real value from. Deliberately omits `image`: no real icon/screenshot assets exist in the repo yet (same gap `AppCard`/`Hero`/`ScreenshotCarousel` already work around with colored placeholder tiles), and a fabricated 404ing image URL would make the structured data actively wrong rather than just incomplete. Closes out `2.d.iii`, and `2.d` (Legal & Compliance) as a whole. `next build` passes clean; manually verified via `next start` that `/app/f-droid` renders a valid, parseable JSON-LD `SoftwareApplication` block with the expected fields.

### Phase 3 — Motion, Metrics & Charts

**3.a — Animation System**
- 3.a.i — Scroll reveal
  - [x] 3.a.i.zi — Intersection-observer reveal utility — `lib/useScrollReveal.ts` (a plain `IntersectionObserver` hook, no library, matching `ScreenshotCarousel`'s (`0.e.i.zi`) existing preference for native browser APIs) plus `components/ScrollReveal.tsx` + `.module.css`, a wrapper consumers pass children through. Per D-STORE.md §4.C — "Scroll-triggered reveal, respecting `prefers-reduced-motion`" is one requirement, not two — `prefers-reduced-motion` is checked directly in this leaf (both in the hook, which returns already-revealed with no observer for a reduced-motion visitor, and as a CSS backstop for the gap before that effect runs), not deferred to the later `3.d.iii` reduced-motion audit. Reveals once per element and disconnects. Utility only — not applied to any page yet, that's `3.a.i.zo` next, the same "utility this leaf, consumers next leaf" split `ShelfGrid`/`AppCard` (`0.c.ii.zi`/`0.c.ii.zo`) already used. `next build` passes clean.
  - [x] 3.a.i.zo — Apply reveal to shelves/hero — wrapped `Hero` and each `Shelf` (Featured, Trending, Editor's Picks) in `ScrollReveal` (`3.a.i.zi`) in `app/page.tsx`, closing out `3.a.i` in full. `ScrollReveal` only takes `children`, so neither `Hero` nor `Shelf` changed — both stay server components, composed as children of the client wrapper, the standard Next.js pattern for adding a client-only concern (the `IntersectionObserver`) around server-rendered content. Per `ScrollReveal.module.css`'s own header comment ("every shelf/hero it eventually wraps"), the hero was in scope despite already having its own separate `0.d.i.zo` mount animation — the two don't conflict: `ScrollReveal` fires immediately for the hero since it's already in the viewport on first paint, so it just layers a no-op transition on top rather than fighting the mount animation. `next build` passes clean (`next build` output confirms `/` compiles with no new client-boundary warnings).
- 3.a.ii — Micro-interactions
  - [x] 3.a.ii.zi — Button/card hover & press states — every existing tappable card/button (`AppCard`, `CategoryCard`, `Hero`'s `.cta`, `ThemeToggle`, `Header`'s nav link/toggle, `ConsentBanner`, `ReportAppForm`/`RateThisApp`/`CategoryFilters` submits, `SignatureInfo`/`ChecksumDisplay` copy buttons, `ExpandableDescription`'s toggle, `ScreenshotCarousel`'s slide button, `Lightbox`'s close/nav buttons) now gets an explicit, smoothly-transitioned hover (previously an instant color snap — none of these had a `transition` property) plus a distinct `:active`/press state, which didn't exist anywhere in the app before this leaf. Along the way, fixed a real gap found in `app/globals.css`: `--color-accent-strong` (0.b.ii.zo's "separately checked variant for hover/active/pressed states") only existed for the light theme — the dark theme had no equivalent, so any component using it would silently fall back to unset in dark mode. Added the dark-theme value (`#b8942e`, ~7.5:1 contrast against `--color-bg`/`--color-surface`, same relative-luminance method 0.b.ii.zo used) and put it to use: filled/pill-shaped buttons now press to `--color-accent-strong` rather than repeating their hover color, so a press reads as a distinct darker tone rather than "the hover, held." Bordered cards and icon-only buttons (`ThemeToggle`, nav toggle, carousel/lightbox controls) instead get a `scale()` press — except `RateThisApp`'s star picker, which scales *up* (a "pop") since a star rating widget conventionally emphasizes the tapped star rather than shrinking it, and text-style link-buttons (`ExpandableDescription`'s toggle, `Header`'s nav link), where a scale-transform would look out of place on bare text, get an opacity dim instead. Every `scale()` press is gated behind `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)`, the same convention this repo already applies to `ScrollReveal`, the hero mount animation, and the `Lightbox` fade; one gotcha caught along the way — `Lightbox`'s `.navButton` already carries a static `translateY(-50%)` for vertical centering, so its `:active` composes `translateY(-50%) scale(0.9)` rather than a bare `scale(0.9)`, which would have silently un-centered the button on press. `next build` passes clean; manually verified via `next start` that `/` returns 200 and the compiled CSS contains both the new dark-theme `--color-accent-strong` value and the new `:active` rules (e.g. `AppCard_card__*:active{transform:scale(.97)}`).
  - [x] 3.a.ii.zo — Install-button progress animation — **split, no code this leaf, see below.** This leaf assumed an install button already existed in the dummy UI to animate. It doesn't: despite the Phase 0 intro (top of Section 2) listing "install button" among the interactions that "use[] local dummy logic," none of the actual `0.e`/`0.f`/`0.g` app-detail leaves ever built one — ratings, search, filters, and the report form all shipped; the install button was never more than that one line of intro-text framing. Per Section 1's standing rule ("if a leaf turns out to be bigger than one session, stop and split it... before writing any code"), no animation code was written against a nonexistent element. Split into a new milestone, **`3.a.iv` — Install Button** (added after `3.a.iii` rather than renumbering it, since `3.a.iii`'s leaves are both still open and untouched): `3.a.iv.zi` builds the missing dummy button itself (idle/installing/installed states, click-to-simulate, no real APK fetch — same "anonymous, local-state-only" dummy convention `RateThisApp`/`ReportAppForm` already use), then `3.a.iv.zo` is this leaf's original scope, the progress-fill animation, now correctly sequenced after its own prerequisite exists. `3.a.ii` itself is done: both its leaves are now closed, one directly, one by this redirect.
- 3.a.iii — Loading states
  - [x] 3.a.iii.zi — Skeleton shimmer components — a small family built on one shared primitive: `components/Skeleton.module.css` (the `.shimmer` keyframe, gated behind `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` like every other animation in this repo — falls back to a static muted block, not no block) and `components/SkeletonBlock.tsx` (generic shimmering rectangle, `aria-hidden`), then `SkeletonCard.tsx` (mirrors `AppCard`, also reused for `CategoryCard`'s grid), `SkeletonHero.tsx` (mirrors `Hero`), and `SkeletonShelf.tsx` (title bar + `ShelfGrid` of `SkeletonCard`s, reusing `Shelf.module.css`'s own exported class names for exact spacing parity). Wired into Next.js's automatic per-route-segment Suspense boundary via a `loading.tsx` at every route that does an async data fetch: `app/loading.tsx` (home — hero + 3 shelves), `app/app/[slug]/loading.tsx` (header mirrored exactly via `page.module.css`'s own classes, generic shimmering blocks standing in for the page's many sections — see that file's comment for why), `app/categories/loading.tsx`, `app/categories/[slug]/loading.tsx`, `app/search/loading.tsx` (all three: heading + `SkeletonCard` grid), and `app/developer/[slug]/loading.tsx` (header + `SkeletonShelf`). Every skeleton mirrors its real component's exact dimensions so there's no layout shift on swap, and every `loading.tsx` wraps its output in a single `role="status" aria-label="Loading"` region so a screen reader announces once, not once per shimmering block. This is exactly the case `lib/catalog.ts`'s own header comment flagged `SIMULATED_LATENCY_MS` for. `next build` passes clean across the full route tree (10 routes)
  - [x] 3.a.iii.zo — Empty/error/404 state designs — one shared `components/EmptyState.tsx` + `.module.css` (icon, heading, message, optional action — a `Link` or an arbitrary node) rather than four bespoke components, matching this repo's existing "one primitive, several call sites" pattern (`SkeletonBlock`, `3.a.iii.zi`). Icon is a plain inline SVG in `currentColor`, four variants (`search`/`filter`/`error`/`not-found`), no external image assets, same convention as every other visual in this repo. **3.b — Metrics Pipeline**
*(Re-scoped twice — see the "Resolved" note in Section 0. Final scope: app-native counters, storefront-driven. `3.b.i.zi`/`3.b.i.zo` are real Next.js Route Handlers calling into `lib/catalog.ts`'s existing data-fetch seam, not a direct Supabase client — `5.f.i.zi` hasn't provisioned Supabase yet, so `lib/catalog.ts`'s dummy-data internals stand in until it does, same as every other real-backend leaf ahead of its infra.)*
- 3.b.i — Counters
  - [x] 3.b.i.zi — Install-count increment endpoint — resolves the "app-native counters" decision above. New `app/api/apps/[slug]/install/route.ts`, a POST-only Next.js Route Handler (no body — slug comes from the route segment, matching every read in `lib/catalog.ts`). Delegates the actual mutation to a new `incrementInstallCount(slug)` in `lib/catalog.ts` — the first *write* in that file (every function before it is read-only) — which follows the exact seam that file's own header comment already commits to: mutates the in-memory dummy `apps` array today, returns the new count or `null` for an unknown slug (mapped to a 404, same shape `getAppBySlug` already uses for "not found"), and gets swapped for a real Supabase call once `5.f.i` provisions it with no change needed at the route-handler or call-site level. Wired into `components/InstallButton.tsx`'s `runSimulatedInstall`: a fire-and-forget `fetch` POST fired only on a genuinely fresh install, not on an "Update" re-run of the same simulated flow (checked via `status !== "outdated"`, read before `markInstalled` changes it) — matching real install-counter semantics, where reinstalling an update doesn't bump the count. Errors are deliberately swallowed (`.catch(() => {})`): a failed counter ping must never block or roll back the dummy local-only install itself. No auth, no dedup — same anonymous posture `Review`/`ReportFlag` (`1.a.iii.zi`/`zo`) already take; a user re-triggering their own already-simulated install would double-count, same as a real re-install would, and fingerprint/rate-limit throttling for this exact class of counter is already separately scoped (`5.d.ii.zi`), not something to half-build here. `next build` passes clean, new route shows up in the build's route table (`/api/apps/[slug]/install`); manually verified via `next start`: `POST /api/apps/f-droid/install` returns `200` with an incrementing `install_count` (128430 → 128431 → 128432 across two calls), `POST /api/apps/does-not-exist/install` returns `404`, `GET` on the same route correctly 405s (no `GET` handler defined), and `/app/f-droid` still renders `200` with the Install button present.
  - [x] 3.b.i.zo — View-count increment endpoint — same shape as `3.b.i.zi`'s install-count endpoint, but fired once per page load rather than per click, since a "view" is the page loading, not a user action on it. `view_count` is a genuinely new field (`lib/mock-data.ts`) — like `signing_certificate_fingerprint` (`0.f.i.zo`) before it, not in §5's/§7's original field list, added specifically to back §4E's "View counters (powers Trending)" row, which had never had a backing field; a plausible dummy seed per app, roughly 1.5–1.7× that app's `install_count` (varies per app, not a fixed ratio). New `incrementViewCount(slug)` in `lib/catalog.ts`, same seam/shape as `incrementInstallCount` immediately above it. New `app/api/apps/[slug]/view/route.ts`, same POST-only/slug-from-segment/404-on-unknown-slug shape as the install-count route. New `components/ViewPing.tsx`: a small "use client" component rendering nothing, firing a fire-and-forget POST on mount via `useEffect` — needed because `app/app/[slug]/page.tsx` is a Server Component and `useEffect` only runs in a Client Component, same reason `InstallButton`/`RateThisApp` already are; a `useRef` guard prevents React Strict Mode's dev-only double-invoke from double-counting a view. Wired in as `<ViewPing appSlug={app.slug} />` at the top of `<main>`. Same anonymous, no-dedup, no-auth posture as `3.b.i.zi` — a repeat page load is genuinely another view, real view counters don't dedupe per visitor either; rate-limiting is the same already-separately-scoped `5.d.ii.zi`. Closes out `3.b.i` (Counters) in full. Noted but out of scope for this leaf: `getTrendingApps` (`lib/catalog.ts`) currently sorts by `install_count`, not the new `view_count`, despite §4E's framing ("View counters (powers Trending)") — that's `3.b.ii`'s (Aggregation/Trending cache) job to reconcile, not this leaf's. `next build` passes clean, both new routes show up in the build's route table; manually verified via `next start`: `POST /api/apps/f-droid/view` increments `214800 → 214801 → 214802` across two calls, unknown slug 404s, and `/app/f-droid` still renders `200`.
- 3.b.ii — Aggregation
  - [x] 3.b.ii.zi — Denormalized `avg_rating`/`review_count` job — this is the leaf the milestone is named for: rather than computing the average fresh from every `Review` row on each read, the aggregate lives denormalized on the `App` row itself and updates incrementally the moment a new review comes in. `Review` (`lib/mock-data.ts`) is a new, deliberately narrow entity — §5's "new entities" list names `Review` generically without specifying fields; scoped here to exactly what `RateThisApp` (`0.e.iii.zo`) already collects (a 1–5 star value, no written text), rather than inventing a text-review UI that doesn't exist anywhere else in this codebase. The `reviews` store starts empty on purpose — every app's existing `avg_rating`/`rating_count` is itself an aggregate-only dummy seed with no real rows behind it (same as `install_count`/`view_count` before it), so there's nothing honest to backfill. New `submitReview(slug, stars)` in `lib/catalog.ts`: validates nothing itself (the route handler does, before ever calling in) — appends the raw `Review` row (not read by anything yet, but there for a future real histogram or the eventual Phase 5 migration to read from), then folds the new rating into the existing aggregate via the standard incremental-average update (`newAvg = (oldAvg × oldCount + stars) / (oldCount + 1)`) rather than deriving `avg_rating`/`rating_count` purely from `reviews` — the latter would make the very first real submission crash `rating_count` from e.g. 47 down to 1, a more visibly broken dummy behavior than the thing `RateThisApp`'s own prior doc comment had already flagged as too misleading to fake. New `app/api/apps/[slug]/reviews/route.ts` — resource-shaped (`POST .../reviews`), not verb-shaped like `/install`/`/view`, since a review has its own identity; validates `stars` is an integer 1–5 (or valid JSON at all) before calling `submitReview`, 400s otherwise, 404s on an unknown slug. `RateThisApp.tsx` now actually calls this endpoint on submit (fire-and-forget, same "never block the dummy confirmation on a failed ping" posture as `InstallButton`/`ViewPing`) instead of only setting local `submitted` state — its doc comment updated to reflect that the "deliberately doesn't reach up" scope line from `0.e.iii.zo` no longer applies now that there's a real aggregation to reach into; `RatingSummary` still reads the aggregate fresh every request and still synthesizes its histogram from it, unchanged. `next build` passes clean, new route in the build table. Manually verified via `next start` against `paper-foss-theme` (seed `avg_rating: 4.2`, `rating_count: 47`): a 5-star submission returned `{avg_rating: 4.2, rating_count: 48}` (197.4+5=202.4/48=4.2166…→4.2), a follow-up 3-star returned `{avg_rating: 4.2, rating_count: 49}` (201.6+3=204.6/49=4.1755…→4.2) — both arithmetically correct against the rounding rule; `stars: 0`, `stars: 6.5`, and a missing/empty body all 400; unknown slug 404s; `GET` 405s; `/app/paper-foss-theme` still renders `200` with "Rate this app" present.
  - [x] 3.b.ii.zo — Daily materialized Trending cache — two changes to `getTrendingApps` (`lib/catalog.ts`). **Sort key**: `install_count` → `view_count`, resolving the note `3.b.i.zo` flagged — §4E frames this row as "View counters (powers Trending)," and a cumulative install total barely reorders day to day besides not matching what "trending" means (an install from six months ago counts forever). **Materialization**: previously a full-catalog sort on every call; now a lazy TTL cache (`TRENDING_CACHE_TTL_MS`, 24h — the "daily" in the leaf name) via `materializeTrendingCache()`/`getOrRefreshTrendingCache()`, snapshotting the *ranked slug order* (not `App` objects) so a view event landing mid-window doesn't retroactively reorder an already-served ranking — the point of "daily" is the order holds steady for the window — while `App` data is still looked up fresh on every call, so display fields are never stale even though the order intentionally is within the window. Documented honestly as a same-process in-memory cache (a real multi-instance deployment wouldn't share it — that needs an actual scheduled job writing to Supabase once `5.f.i` lands, which this stands in for). Also updated the stale comment on `app/page.tsx`'s Trending shelf (still described the old `install_count` sort) with an addendum rather than rewriting the original note, matching this doc's own append-don't-erase convention. `next build` passes clean (needed a small TS fix: a module-level `let` cache variable doesn't narrow past a function-call boundary on its own, so `getOrRefreshTrendingCache` returns via a documented non-null assertion rather than fighting the narrowing). Manually verified via `next start`: home page's Trending shelf now renders `f-droid, mincal-widget, …` — the `view_count`-sorted order (computed independently and matched) — and a `POST .../amexia/view` in between two page loads did not reorder the shelf, confirming the snapshot (not a live re-sort) is what's being served within the TTL window. Closes out `3.b.ii` (Aggregation) in full. (**Correction, made this session:** this entry originally also claimed to close "all of `3.b`" — wrong, `3.b.iii` (Charts) still had two open leaves at the time; that overclaim is fixed here rather than left standing. `3.b` as a whole isn't closed until `3.b.iii.zo` lands.)

Deliberately skipped the lazy-TTL-snapshot treatment `getTrendingApps` got (`3.b.ii.zo`): that framing was specific to Trending's "daily materialized" naming, and a cumulative install count is naturally far more stable per-request than a raw view count anyway, so a plain live sort stays honest without inventing a caching layer this leaf's name doesn't call for. `AppCard` (`components/AppCard.tsx`) grew an optional `rank` prop — a small numbered badge over the icon — rather than a second, bespoke ranked-list component, keeping every listing page (search, categories, shelves, and now charts) on the one grid/card §3 already locked in. New `app/charts/top-free/page.tsx`, reusing `ShelfGrid`/`AppCard` exactly like `/search` and `/categories/[slug]` do. New nav link in `Header.tsx` ("Top Free" → `/charts/top-free`) — not a forward-reference this time (unlike `/search`/`/categories` originally), the page exists before the link does. `next build` passes clean, new route in the build table. New `app/charts/new/page.tsx` + `page.module.css`, reusing `ShelfGrid`/`AppCard` exactly like `/charts/top-free` does, reading the existing `getNewAndUpdated` (`lib/catalog.ts`) — genuinely no changes needed there: unlike `getTopFreeApps` (which never had a `limit` param), `getNewAndUpdated` already had one defaulting to 12 for the home-shelf preview, and that default has to stay a default since the home page still calls it bare. `getNewAndUpdated(Infinity)` at this page's own call site reads as "no cap" without touching the function or its signature — `Array.prototype.slice(0, Infinity)` returns the whole array. **Decided the open rank-badge question this leaf was flagged with:** no `rank` prop here — recency isn't a competitive standing the way an install-count position is, so a numbered badge would imply an ordering it doesn't actually have. `AppCard` (`components/AppCard.tsx`) instead grew a second optional prop, `caption` (a plain muted line under the rating row, own new `.caption` class in `AppCard.module.css`), which this page uses to show "Updated <date>" per app (`Intl.DateTimeFormat`, e.g. "Updated Feb 18, 2017") — the one piece of information this chart is actually about, same "small optional addition to the one existing card" shape `rank` established rather than a second bespoke chart-card component. `Header.tsx` gains a "New & Updated" nav link to `/charts/new`, same as `3.b.iii.zi` did for Top Free — also corrected that file's now-stale comment claiming this route "doesn't have a nav entry yet since it doesn't exist yet either," which was true when written and false as of this commit. `next build` passes clean, new route in the build table. **3.c — Admin/Editorial Tools**
- 3.c.i — Featuring
  - [x] 3.c.i.zi — Admin toggle for `is_featured`/`is_editors_pick`
  - [x] 3.c.i.zo — Sponsored-slot scheduling tool
- 3.c.ii — Analytics
  - [x] 3.c.ii.zi — Traffic dashboard
  - [x] 3.c.ii.zo — Top-searches dashboard
- 3.c.iii — Moderation
  - [ ] 3.c.iii.zi — Report-flag queue UI *(Held — Zealot ❓6 outcome: moderation stays here only as a small authenticated admin; see "Cross-repo review" in Section 0. Do not start until revisited.)*
  - [ ] 3.c.iii.zo — Review moderation queue UI *(Held — same reason; also `Review` has no text today.)*
- 3.c.iv — Admin hardening *(new)*
  - [x] 3.c.iv.zi — Gate or remove the unauthenticated `/admin/*` and `/api/admin/*` routes before any real data exists (they are deployed on Vercel). Editorial toggles and sponsored slots go away once `5.g.v.zi` makes the index the source of truth; anything kept sits behind real authentication. — Done: **gated, not removed** — deleting the editorial/sponsored tools ahead of `5.g.v.zi` is a product decision, and gating is reversible. D-Store has no accounts, so "real authentication" is HTTP Basic auth against one shared secret in Vercel env vars: **`ADMIN_PASSWORD` (required, ≥16 characters) and optional `ADMIN_USERNAME` (default `admin`)** — `lib/admin-auth.ts`. **Fails closed:** with the password unset or shorter than 16 characters, every `/admin/*` and `/api/admin/*` route answers 503 to everyone, so this patch closes the exposure on deploy, before the secret is set. Three layers share one check: `middleware.ts` (primary gate; admin paths skip the region lookup, get `Cache-Control: no-store` and `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`), `guardAdminRequest` at the top of all five `/api/admin` handlers, and `requireAdminPage` (404 if unauthenticated) at the top of the four `/admin` pages — defence in depth in case middleware is bypassed (CVE-2025-29927 style) or a future route forgets a check. Credentials are compared in constant time (SHA-256 both sides, byte-XOR, both comparisons always run). Because browsers re-send Basic credentials cross-site, mutating methods (PATCH/POST/DELETE) also require an `Origin` header matching the host (CSRF guard); the admin UI's same-origin `fetch` already sends one. **Owner action required:** in Vercel → Project → Settings → Environment Variables, add `ADMIN_PASSWORD` (a long random value; Basic auth has no throttling, so length is the brute-force defence) for Production, then redeploy. `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` pass clean; tested against `next start`: env unset → 503 everywhere (public pages still 200); 15-char password → 503; configured → no/wrong user/wrong password 401 with a `WWW-Authenticate` challenge, correct credentials 200 on all four pages and the API GET; mutating calls: no auth 401, authed without `Origin` 403, authed with a foreign `Origin` 403, authed with the same `Origin` reaches the handler; a forged `x-middleware-subrequest` header still gets 401 on both a page and an API route (the handler-level checks hold on their own).
    - **Flagged, not fixed:** (1) **Third-party apps get 404 from every counter/rating/feature call.** `incrementInstallCount`, `incrementViewCount`, `submitRating` and `setAppFeaturing` (`lib/catalog.ts`) look up the static `apps` array only, not the merged catalog, so Aptoide apps return "App not found". Consequence: the install-counter ping that `ThirdPartyDownloadButton` fires on a click (`5.h.iii.zi` — its Done note says it "still fires" the ping, which is true but it is silently discarded) and the view ping never count for third-party apps, and Editors' Pick/Featured toggles can't touch them (consistent with the first-party-only editorial rule, `5.g.v`). Needs its own leaf; the durable fix is `5.f.i` (Supabase counters), since in-memory counters don't survive across Vercel serverless instances anyway. (2) Same in-memory limit applies to everything the admin tools write (sponsored slots, featuring): edits are per-instance and lost on cold start, so on Vercel the tools are of limited use until `5.g.v.zi`. (3) `/admin` has no index page (each of the four sub-pages is its own route), and there is no login/logout UI beyond the browser's Basic prompt.

**3.d — Accessibility & Performance**
- 3.d.i — Accessibility
  - [x] 3.d.i.zi — WCAG 2.1 AA contrast audit (both themes) — Done: audited every `color:`/`opacity:` declaration in `app/globals.css` and all `*.module.css` files against both themes' `--color-bg`/`--color-surface`, using the relative-luminance contrast formula (same method already used inline for `0.b.ii.zo`/`0.b.iv.zi`). Findings: (1) `opacity: 0.4` on `:disabled` submit buttons (`ReportAppForm`, `RateThisApp`) drops light-theme text to ~2.55:1 and dark-theme to ~3.37:1, both under the 4.5:1 body-text bar — **not fixed**, WCAG 2.1 SC 1.4.3 explicitly exempts inactive/disabled UI components, and `cursor: not-allowed` plus the shared `:disabled` state already communicate non-interactivity. (2) Every other `opacity`-reduced text value found (0.6–0.85, used for meta/secondary text across `Header`, `ThemeToggle`, `ConsentBanner`, `RatingSummary`, `Hero`, and others) clears AA at its lowest value, 0.6 → 4.68:1 light / 6.36:1 dark — no change needed. (3) **Real failure, fixed:** the two admin form-validation stylesheets (`app/admin/featuring/page.module.css`, `app/admin/sponsored/page.module.css`) hardcoded `color: #c94b4b` for error text at 12–12.8px (under the large-text threshold, so it needs 4.5:1, not 3:1) — that fixed hex doesn't re-theme and measured 4.30:1 on light `--color-bg` and 4.05:1 on dark `--color-surface`, both fails. Replaced with a new pair of theme tokens, `--color-error` (`app/globals.css`, one per `[data-theme]` block next to `--color-text`, same convention as `--color-accent-strong`): `#a83232` for light (6.24:1 bg / 6.63:1 surface) and `#e57373` for dark (6.66:1 bg / 6.20:1 surface), both files switched to `var(--color-error)`. (4) `Lightbox.module.css`'s three `color: #fff` instances are intentionally unthemed — white text on the component's own fixed near-black overlay background, not `--color-bg`/`--color-surface` — checked, not a token candidate, no change. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` pass clean.
  - [x] 3.d.i.zo — Keyboard nav + focus-visible audit — Done: audited every interactive element (native `<button>`/`<a>`/`<input>`/`<select>`, no custom `role="button"` divs found anywhere) across `components/` and `app/` for keyboard operability, `:focus-visible` coverage, and any `outline: none` suppression (none found sitewide). Findings: (1) **Real failure, fixed:** `Header.tsx`'s mobile-nav `<input type="checkbox">` is visually hidden but deliberately kept in the tab order (per `Header.module.css`'s own comment) — it also carried `aria-hidden="true"`, which is a real WCAG 4.1.2 (Name, Role, Value) failure: a keyboard-reachable, operable control exposed to assistive tech with no accessible name, since `aria-hidden` strips it from the tree before any name is computed. Fixed by removing `aria-hidden` and moving `aria-label="Toggle navigation menu"` from the `<label>` onto the checkbox itself, the element that actually receives focus. (2) **Real gap, fixed:** the entire `app/admin/**` surface had zero `:focus-visible` styling on its custom controls — unlike every storefront component, which all define one — specifically the featuring-table toggle switches (`app/admin/featuring/page.module.css` `.toggle`) and the sponsored-slots form/buttons (`app/admin/sponsored/page.module.css` `.input`, `.primaryButton`, `.secondaryButton`). Added the same `outline: 2px solid var(--color-accent); outline-offset: 2px;` convention used everywhere else. `app/admin/search` and `app/admin/traffic` have no custom interactive elements, so nothing to add there. (3) **Flagged, not fixed (bigger than this leaf):** `RateThisApp`'s star picker (`role="radiogroup"`/`role="radio"`) has no roving-tabindex/arrow-key navigation — each star is individually tabbable via Tab+Space, which is keyboard-operable and not a hard WCAG failure, but doesn't match the ARIA APG's expected radiogroup pattern (arrow keys move selection, one tab stop for the group); real screen-reader users would likely expect arrow-key behavior here. Implementing that is its own piece of work, not scoped into this audit leaf. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` pass clean.
- 3.d.ii — Performance
  - [x] 3.d.ii.zi — Image CDN + responsive `srcset` — Done (completed this session; carried over as `[~]` WIP from a prior session — see that session's note, preserved in git history on this same leaf's earlier commit, for the icon-path half of this work). Real icon/screenshot URLs exist for third-party (Aptoide) apps (`App.icon`/`App.screenshots[]`, real `https://pool.img.aptoide.com/...` URLs since `5.h.ii.zi`); this leaf wires `next/image` (CDN optimization + responsive `srcset`) to those real URLs everywhere they render, falling back to the existing generated tile/placeholder for any dummy/malformed/non-`https://` value. Prior session: `lib/image.ts` (`isRealImageUrl` guard), `next.config.mjs` (`images.remotePatterns` allow-listing `pool.img.aptoide.com`), `AppIcon.tsx`/`AppIconLive.tsx`/`AppCard.tsx`/`Hero.tsx`/`StickyInstallBar.tsx`/`app/app/[slug]/page.tsx` (icon path), and `ScreenshotCarousel.module.css`'s `.frame` class (screenshot box, not yet wired to any render site). This session finished the screenshot half: `ScreenshotCarousel.module.css`'s `768px` max-width media query now covers `.frame` alongside `.placeholder` (was `.placeholder`-only); `ScreenshotCarousel.tsx` now branches per-slide on `isRealImageUrl(src)` — a real Aptoide screenshot URL renders `next/image` `fill` inside `.frame` (`sizes="(min-width: 768px) 260px, 62vw"` matching the box's own responsive width), anything else falls through to the unchanged colored-tile placeholder; the resolved `screenshots` array is now also passed to `Lightbox` (new optional `screenshots` prop, parallel to the existing `colors`) so the enlarged view makes the same real/placeholder choice per slide — `Lightbox.module.css` gained its own `.frame` (mirrors `ScreenshotCarousel.module.css`'s box at the lightbox's own `min(80vw, 360px)` size). `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both pass clean. Runtime-verified via `next start`: `/app/whatsapp` (a real Aptoide app) renders `next/image`-optimized `pool.img.aptoide.com` URLs (with a full responsive `srcset`) for both its icon and every carousel screenshot, confirmed in the served HTML. **Note for whoever reads this next:** the placeholder branch could not be exercised live in this sandbox — `5.h.iv.zi` (operator request, a separate leaf) emptied `lib/mock-data.ts`'s `apps` array entirely, so every one of the 12 apps currently in the merged catalog is Aptoide-sourced with real `https://` screenshot URLs (confirmed against `storage/downloads/aptoide-snapshot.json` directly: zero non-`https://` screenshot entries across all 12 apps) — there is no dummy app left to hit the fallback path against. The branch is exercised by the same `isRealImageUrl` guard `AppIcon`'s already-verified real/placeholder split uses, and mirrors it exactly, so this is a coverage note, not an open defect.
  - [x] 3.d.ii.zo — Lighthouse LCP < 2.5s budget pass — Done: ran Lighthouse (headless Chrome, `CHROME_PATH=/opt/google/chrome/chrome npx lighthouse ... --only-categories=performance`) against a production build served via `next start`. Home page failed the budget at 2.7s LCP — `lcp-breakdown-insight` pinned it precisely: `elementRenderDelay` alone was 1.9s of the 2.7s total, on an `AppCard` icon (`next/image`) in the first shelf rendered below the hero, because every `AppIcon` real-image render used Next's default `loading="lazy"` regardless of whether it was above the fold — Chrome defers even starting a lazy image's fetch until it's confirmed near-viewport. Fix: `AppIcon` gained an optional `priority` prop threaded straight to `next/image` (default `false`, so every off-screen icon keeps lazy-loading correctly); `Hero`'s icon sets it unconditionally (always above the fold by construction); `AppCard` and `Shelf` gained matching `priority`/`priorityCount` props, and `app/page.tsx` computes `firstNonEmptyShelf` (mirroring `Shelf`'s own empty-array skip logic) so only the first two cards of whichever shelf *actually* renders first below the hero get `priority` — not every shelf, which would just add network contention for off-screen images and could hurt LCP the other way. Re-measured post-fix: home page LCP 2438ms / 2291ms / 1937ms across three separate runs (comfortably under budget, no flakiness above the line), with the LCP element having moved on to a text node (`ConsentBanner`'s copy) — the image path is no longer the bottleneck. Spot-checked the other page types while in there: `/app/whatsapp` (app detail) 2313ms, `/categories` 1834ms, `/search` 1881ms, `/charts/top-free` 1877ms — all already under budget, none touched (app detail's own LCP element is a `Changelog` text block, unrelated to image loading, so out of scope for this leaf). `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both pass clean.
- 3.d.iii — Reduced motion
  - [x] 3.d.iii.zi — `prefers-reduced-motion` audit sitewide — Done: audited every `@keyframes`/`animation:`/`transform:` declaration across `app/globals.css` and all `*.module.css` files. Findings: (1) all five files declaring `@keyframes` (`Hero`, `InstallButton`, `Lightbox`, `Skeleton`, `WavyProgressRing`) already gate their `animation:` property behind `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)`, most with an explicit `reduce` fallback alongside — no change needed. (2) Every transform-based `:active`/press state sitewide (`AppCard`, `CategoryCard`, `CategoryFilters`, `ChecksumDisplay`, `ConsentBanner`, `EmptyState`, `Header`, `Hero`, `Lightbox`, `RateThisApp`, `ReportAppForm`, `ScreenshotCarousel`, `SignatureInfo`, `StickyInstallBar`, `ThemeToggle`) already follows the `3.a.ii.zi`-established convention of gating the transform's `transition` behind `no-preference` — no change needed. (3) Plain color/background/border/opacity transitions (`app/globals.css`'s theme-toggle transition, `AppCard`'s border-color hover) are correctly left ungated, consistent with WCAG 2.3.3's scope being motion, not color, and with how `0.b.iii.zo` already reasoned about the theme-toggle transition specifically. (4) `ScreenshotCarousel.module.css`'s `scroll-behavior: smooth` is correctly left ungated too, per that file's own existing comment — it only affects programmatic scrolls, and none exist yet (plain server component, no scroll-to call site). (5) **Real gap, fixed:** `app/admin/featuring/page.module.css`'s `.toggleKnob` had an ungated `transition: transform 0.15s ease` — the one transform-transition in the whole codebase that didn't follow the `3.a.ii.zi` convention, missed because the admin surface's own focus-visible gap (`3.d.i.zo`) was audited from the *keyboard-nav* angle, not the motion angle. Moved the transition into a `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` block around `.toggleKnob`, same shape as `StickyInstallBar.module.css`'s `.bar`; a reduced-motion visitor now gets the toggle's on/off position change with no animated slide, same as everywhere else in the repo. Verified in the compiled CSS output: `.toggleKnob`'s `transition` now sits inside the media-query rule, not the base rule. No JS-driven motion (`requestAnimationFrame`/`setInterval`) exists anywhere in the codebase to audit separately — `useScrollReveal.ts` is the one place JS checks `matchMedia(\"(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)\")` directly, already correct. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both pass clean.
  - [x] 3.d.iii.zo — Static fallback states for all animations — Done, audit only, no code changes needed: checked every animation found by `3.d.iii.zi` for what a `reduce`-motion visitor actually sees once the animation is skipped, not just that it's skipped. (1) The five `@keyframes` components (`Hero`, `InstallButton`, `Lightbox`, `Skeleton`, `WavyProgressRing`): in every case the `animation` property itself sits entirely inside the `no-preference` block, so a `reduce` visitor never applies it at all and simply renders each element's own un-animated base styles — confirmed none of those base styles are hidden/zero-opacity/mid-transform (`Hero`'s `.panel`/`.icon` have no `opacity`/`transform` outside the keyframes; `Lightbox`'s `.dialog`/`::backdrop` render fully opaque with no base transform; `Skeleton`'s `.shimmer` is a plain solid `--color-border` block, still legibly "loading" without the sweep). No fallback is "frozen mid-animation" anywhere. (2) `InstallButton`'s progress `.fill` and `WavyProgressRing`'s `.ring` go further and explicitly `display: none` under `reduce` rather than relying on the implicit base state, specifically because their base state (`width: 0%` / `stroke-dashoffset: 100`, i.e. permanently "0% progress") would otherwise read as a broken, stuck-at-zero indicator, not a neutral absence of one — checked that this doesn't leave the "installing" state illegible without them: `InstallButton.tsx`'s `[data-state="installing"]` button carries its own distinct background/dim styling (`InstallButton.module.css`) plus an "Installing…"/"Updating…" text label, `disabled` state, and `aria-live="polite"`/`aria-label` announcement — all independent of the fill bar, so the fallback communicates progress state through text and disabled-styling rather than motion, not through nothing at all. (3) `ScrollReveal` is the one component whose *base* (non-media-query) state is genuinely hidden (`opacity: 0`, since visibility is driven by an `IntersectionObserver` via a `.revealed` class, not by animation timing) — already correctly backstopped two ways: an explicit `@media (reduce) { .reveal { opacity: 1; transform: none } }` CSS rule, and `useScrollReveal.ts`'s own `matchMedia` check that sets `isRevealed` immediately for a `reduce` visitor, covering the gap between first paint and that effect running (per that hook's own header comment, this was deliberately built into `3.a.i.zi` rather than deferred to this audit). (4) `StickyInstallBar.module.css`'s `.bar` also starts `opacity: 0`/`visibility: hidden` outside any media query, but that's `data-visible`-driven show/hide logic (an `IntersectionObserver` watching the header), not animation-timing-driven — orthogonal to `prefers-reduced-motion`, which only strips the `.bar`'s `transition` (confirmed already present, `reduce` block sets `transition: none`), so the show/hide itself still functions correctly, just instantly. (5) Every transform-based `:active` press effect (`3.d.iii.zi`'s finding 2) has no separate "fallback" to design: removing the whole rule under `reduce` correctly returns the element to its plain resting style, which is a complete, non-broken state on its own — WCAG 2.3.3 requires interaction-triggered motion be disable-able, not replaced with a substitute non-motion cue, so no gap here. Grepped the full codebase for `requestAnimationFrame`/`setInterval`-driven visual state (none exist) and every base `opacity: 0`/hidden declaration sitewide (7 occurrences, all covered by the reasoning above) to make sure this audit wasn't just re-checking the `no-preference` gating `3.d.iii.zi` already confirmed. Closes out `3.d.iii` (Reduced motion) and, with it, all of `3.d` (Accessibility & Performance) and Phase 3 (Motion, Metrics & Charts) — `3.c.iii.zi`/`zo` remain open but Held (Zealot ❓6, unrelated to this track). `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both pass clean (no files changed by this leaf).

**4.b — Progressive Web App**
- 4.b.i — Installability
  - [x] 4.b.i.zi — Web app manifest — `app/manifest.ts` (Next's App Router manifest-file convention, served at `/manifest.webmanifest` and auto-linked from `<head>`, no manual `<link rel="manifest">` needed) plus `public/icon.svg` (new, this leaf's only image asset — a static, hand-authored brand mark reusing `AppIcon.tsx`'s gradient+glyph+gloss convention but with dark theme's token *values* hardcoded, since a manifest icon has no CSS-variable runtime; one SVG covers both `"any"` and `"maskable"` purpose entries). `id`/`start_url: "/"`/`display: "standalone"` are the fields that actually make the browser's install prompt available; `background_color`/`theme_color` reuse dark theme's own `--color-bg` (`#0a0908`) since no per-theme manifest variant exists yet (`4.b.ii`, "Theme-aware app icons," is the next milestone and where that gets addressed, not this leaf). `app/layout.tsx` gained a `viewport` export with the matching `themeColor` — Next 15 moved that key out of the `metadata` export into its own `viewport` export, so this keeps the deprecated-field warning from firing while staying the single place both the `<meta name="theme-color">` tag and the manifest's own `theme_color` are sourced from. `next build` passes clean (`/manifest.webmanifest` compiles as a static `○` route, same as `/sitemap.xml`); manually verified via `next start` that `/manifest.webmanifest` (200, well-formed JSON with the expected fields) and `/icon.svg` (200) both serve correctly, and that the rendered `/` head contains both `<link rel="manifest" href="/manifest.webmanifest"/>` and `<meta name="theme-color" content="#0a0908"/>`.
  - [x] 4.b.i.zo — Service worker offline shell — `public/sw.js` (plain vanilla worker, no Workbox/next-pwa dependency added, matching this repo's native-platform-API preference) plus `components/ServiceWorkerRegister.tsx` (registers it on mount, mounted from `app/layout.tsx` next to `Header`/`Footer` so it's active on every page) and the fallback route it serves, `app/offline/page.tsx` + `page.module.css` (copies the privacy/terms prose shell). Deliberately narrow scope, not full offline catalog browsing: every page here is server-rendered per request (theme/region cookies, `lib/catalog.ts`'s runtime catalog read), so there's no static page HTML this worker could correctly cache offline — instead it (1) tries the network first for any navigation and falls back to the precached `/offline` page only on failure, and (2) opportunistically cache-first's same-origin `/_next/static/*` requests, since those filenames are content-hashed and safe to cache indefinitely once fetched. API routes, `/manifest.webmanifest`, and everything cross-origin pass through untouched — no risk of serving stale dynamic data to an online visitor. `next build` passes clean (`/offline` a new `ƒ` route); manually verified via `next start` that `/offline` and `/sw.js` both return 200, `/sw.js` is served with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=0` (so browsers revalidate it promptly rather than caching a stale worker), and `/offline`'s body renders the expected "You're offline" copy.
- 4.b.ii — Icons/splash
  - [x] 4.b.ii.zi — Theme-aware app icons — `public/icon-light.svg` (new), a hand-authored light-theme variant of `4.b.i.zi`'s `public/icon.svg`, built from light theme's own token *values* (`--color-bg #f7f8fa`, `--color-accent #2454c9`, `--color-accent-strong #1a3d99`) rather than a brightness-inverted copy of the dark icon — same "must genuinely diverge" rule `0.i`/`0.b.iv` already established for theme-adjacent assets; the background gradient leans on the same cool-cobalt-glow shape `0.b.iv.zo`'s light `--gradient-vignette` introduced, and the gloss highlight uses a low-opacity accent tint instead of dark theme's white highlight, since a white highlight would be invisible against this near-white base. `app/manifest.ts` made async and now calls `getTheme()` (`lib/theme.ts`, 0.b.iii.zi) — the same theme-cookie read `app/layout.tsx` already does for `data-theme` — to resolve `icons[].src`/`background_color`/`theme_color` per visitor instead of always returning dark theme's values; this opts the route out of static generation (expected for a now-cookie-dependent response). `next build` passes clean; manually verified via `next start` that `/manifest.webmanifest` returns dark theme's `/icon.svg` + `#0a0908`/`#0a0908` with no cookie and light theme's `/icon-light.svg` + `#f7f8fa`/`#2454c9` with an explicit `Cookie: d-store-theme=light` header, and that both `/icon.svg` and `/icon-light.svg` serve 200.
  - [x] 4.b.ii.zo — Splash screens per theme — `public/splash.svg` (dark) and `public/splash-light.svg` (light, both new), reusing `icon.svg`/`icon-light.svg`'s gradient-background + "D"-glyph construction at a representative portrait-phone aspect ratio (1170×2532) rather than a per-device raster pipeline — a splash is a plain background wash plus a centered wordmark, with no fine detail that needs exact pixel-for-pixel matching per device, so one scalable vector asset per theme covers every breakpoint honestly. `app/layout.tsx`'s static `metadata` export replaced with `generateMetadata()` (same async, cookie-reading pattern `4.b.ii.zi` already gave `app/manifest.ts`) so `appleWebApp.startupImage` resolves the correct theme's splash per visitor; `appleWebApp.capable: true` is included because it's the flag that makes iOS treat the site as standalone at all — without it none of the `startupImage` entries would ever show, so it's necessary to this leaf's feature rather than scope creep, and `statusBarStyle` (`black-translucent` dark / `default` light) is included for the same "does the launch experience look right" reason `theme_color` already varies per theme in the manifest. `startupImage`'s four `media` breakpoints are a representative subset of Apple's own much longer device-splash list (one compact-phone, one standard-phone, one large-phone, one tablet class) — same "representative, not exhaustive" scope this repo already applies elsewhere (e.g. `5.h`'s curated Aptoide category set); a model outside those four still gets a correctly themed, correctly proportioned splash from the nearest breakpoint's vector asset, just not pixel-matched to that exact device's native resolution. iOS only shows a splash for a standalone-mode site and only reads it from these `<link rel="apple-touch-startup-image">` tags — it does not derive one from the web manifest the way Android/Chrome does, so this is genuinely separate work from `4.b.ii.zi`'s manifest changes, not a duplicate of them. `next build` passes clean; manually verified via `next start` that `/` (no cookie) renders all four `apple-touch-startup-image` links pointed at `/splash.svg` plus `apple-mobile-web-app-status-bar-style: black-translucent`, that an explicit `Cookie: d-store-theme=light` header swaps all four to `/splash-light.svg` plus `status-bar-style: default`, and that both `/splash.svg` and `/splash-light.svg` serve 200.

**4.b — Progressive Web App: complete.** Both `4.b.i` (Installability) and `4.b.ii` (Icons/splash) are now fully closed out.

**4.c — Growth Loops**
- 4.c.i — Syndication
  - [x] 4.c.i.zi — RSS/Atom feed for new & updated apps — Done: `app/feed.xml/route.ts` (new), a plain Next.js Route Handler (`app/feed.xml/route.ts` → `/feed.xml`, literal-extension segment, no rewrite/redirect config needed), same "framework convention over bespoke route" reasoning `app/sitemap.ts` already used. RSS 2.0 (no existing Atom precedent in this repo to match, and the leaf only requires one working format). Source is `getNewAndUpdated(Infinity)` — the exact same `updated_at`-descending list `/charts/new` already renders, so the feed syndicates an existing ranking rather than inventing a second one — capped to the 50 most recent items (a feed's job is "what changed lately," unlike `/charts/new`/`/sitemap.xml` which intentionally show everything). Each `<item>`'s `<link>`/`<guid>` point at `/app/<slug>`, the same canonical URL `app/sitemap.ts` uses. Reused `app/sitemap.ts`'s existing `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`-with-fallback `BASE_URL` constant rather than re-declaring a second copy. `App.developer_name` is only populated for Aptoide-origin apps (Zealot-origin apps key off `developer_slug` into the separate `Developer` table instead); rather than adding a per-item `getDeveloperBySlug` join here, `<author>` is simply omitted when the field is absent — valid per RSS 2.0 (`<author>` is optional per item), not a placeholder. All text fields (title, description, author, category) pass through a small `escapeXml` helper since none of them are pre-escaped catalog/source data. **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both pass clean (`/feed.xml` listed as a real route in the build output); smoke-tested with `next start` — `curl`'d `/feed.xml`, got `200` with `Content-Type: application/xml; charset=utf-8`, well-formed RSS 2.0 with all 12 real catalog apps in `updated_at`-descending order (Waze newest, GO Launcher Prime oldest), `&`/non-ASCII characters (e.g. GO Launcher's `ð`) rendering correctly. Did not touch `components/Footer.tsx` or `app/about/page.tsx` — their `/feed.xml` links already pointed at the right path per `0.c.iii.zo`/`0.j.iv.zo`, nothing to update there now that the route exists.
  - [x] 4.c.i.zo — Public stats footer widget — Done: `lib/catalog.ts`'s new `getPublicStats()` (per docs/D-STORE.md §4E, "total apps, downloads" — the coarse, catalog-wide subset; not `getTrafficSummary`'s per-app breakdown, which stays behind `/admin/traffic`'s password gate for a reason). `components/Footer.tsx` is now an async server component (was sync through `0.j.iv.zo`) that calls it and renders `"<N> apps · <N> downloads"` between the copyright line and the legal nav, styled from the existing `--color-border` token (`Footer.module.css`'s new `.stats` rule) — no new design tokens needed. `totalDownloads` sums `install_count` across the merged catalog; honestly reflects the same known gap `getTrafficSummary`/`5.h.ii.zi` already documented (Aptoide-origin apps' counters aren't wired up, so they contribute 0 each) rather than inventing or borrowing an Aptoide-sourced number — not re-solved here. Deliberately skipped this file's usual `resolveAfterDelay` simulated latency for this one function: `Footer` renders in the root layout on every page with no Suspense boundary, so the 200ms every other catalog call carries (justified by page-level loading states this component doesn't have) would otherwise tax every single navigation sitewide for nothing. **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both pass clean; `next start` + `curl` on `/` shows the real footer markup with real numbers ("12 apps · 0 downloads" — accurate today: the live catalog is all 12 Aptoide-origin apps per `5.h.iv.zi`, each starting at `install_count: 0`, so 0 total downloads is honest, not a bug).
- 4.c.ii — Sharing
  - [x] 4.c.ii.zi — Share button + QR code for direct install — Done: new `components/ShareButton.tsx`/`.module.css`, wired into the app detail page's `installRow` (`app/app/[slug]/page.tsx`) next to Install/`ThirdPartyDownloadButton`. Clicking it calls the Web Share API (`navigator.share`) directly where available — effectively every mobile browser, where sharing matters most — with no UI of this component's own involved; everywhere else it falls back to a `<dialog>` modal built on the same native-`<dialog>` conventions `Lightbox.tsx` (`0.e.i.zo`) already established (`showModal()`, focus trap, native Escape-close, click-on-backdrop-to-dismiss), showing a QR code plus a "Copy link" row (same copy-feedback pattern `ChecksumDisplay.tsx` uses: 2s "Copied!" state, `aria-live` status span). QR generation is local: added the `qrcode` package (pure JS, MIT, no network call) and its `@types/qrcode`, rendering to a `<canvas>` via `QRCode.toCanvas` — deliberately not a third-party QR-image API, so this repo's install doesn't pick up a new render-time network dependency for a small string encode. The shared/encoded URL is always `window.location.href` (the real current address bar URL, client-side only) rather than a reconstructed one from `app/sitemap.ts`/`app/feed.xml/route.ts`'s env-var `BASE_URL` — correct in every environment (dev, preview, prod) with no new env var needed. **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both pass clean (`/app/[slug]`'s bundle grew from 7.92 kB to 17.4 kB, expected — the QR library is now part of its client bundle); `next start` + `curl` on `/app/whatsapp` confirms the button renders in `installRow` with the correct `aria-label` ("Share WhatsApp Messenger") and prop (`appName`). The QR-canvas draw and native-share-sheet paths are both real browser APIs this sandbox's `curl`-based smoke test can't visually exercise; not run interactively here.
  - [x] 4.c.ii.zo — Editorial collections (e.g. "Privacy Tools") — Done: genuinely new concept (the `5.g.i.zo` cross-repo review confirmed no `Collection` exists on either side yet), distinct from `Category`/`is_editors_pick` — a human-curated cross-cutting theme, any number of apps from any category. `lib/mock-data.ts`'s new `Collection` interface (`slug`/`name`/`description`/`app_package_names[]`, keyed on package name rather than slug — the same stable, source-independent join key `mergeCatalogSources` already dedupes on) plus a seeded `collections` array. `lib/catalog.ts` grew `getCollections`/`getCollectionBySlug`/`getCollectionApps`/`getCollectionAppCount`, mirroring `getCategories`/`getCategoryBySlug`/`getCategoryAppCount`'s existing shape. New `components/CollectionCard.tsx`/`.module.css` (mirrors `CategoryCard`: no per-item color fields, so the tile uses the shared accent token; shows the collection's `description` instead of `CategoryCard`'s bare count, since a collection's whole point is *why* the apps are grouped). New routes `app/collections/page.tsx` (index) and `app/collections/[slug]/page.tsx` (per-collection listing, `notFound()` on an unknown slug, `EmptyState` if every member package has since left the catalog) — same "index + the page it links to, in one leaf" pattern `0.g.ii.zi` used for `/categories`. `Header.tsx` gained a "Collections" nav link, same "add the link once the page is real" convention `/charts/top-free`/`/charts/new` already followed.

    **Seed data, curated honestly, not padded:** the roadmap's own example, "Privacy Tools," is genuinely useful only against real catalog content, so it's populated by reading the live catalog's actual data, not invented. Checked `storage/downloads/aptoide-snapshot.json`'s real `media.description` text for all 12 real apps (`5.h.iv.zi`'s catalog): three make a specific, checkable privacy-relevant claim in their own listing — Firefox ("a private web browser designed to protect your data, block trackers"), Obsidian (works "on top of a local folder of plain text Markdown files" — local-first by architecture), ReadEra ("No register" — no forced account). The other 9 (WhatsApp, Termux, VLC, Waze, Khan Academy, GO Launcher Prime, Simple Alarm Clock, GitHub, My Expenses) make no such claim and are excluded rather than force-fit to pad the shelf. Deliberately **not** using `App.data_safety`/`contains_ads` as the criterion instead: `lib/sources/aptoide.ts` currently sets those identically (`false`) for every Aptoide app as a documented placeholder, not real per-app disclosure — using a field that can't actually distinguish apps would make the collection look data-driven when it isn't. A three-app collection is the honest result of today's 12-app catalog, not a bug; the general mechanism this leaf built is what scales once `5.h.vi` (still open — the catalog is capped at 12 hand-curated packages) grows the catalog.

    **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both pass clean (`/collections`, `/collections/[slug]` listed as real routes; also caught and fixed an unrelated pre-existing gap while syncing — `npm install` was needed to pick up the `qrcode` package `4.c.ii.zi` added before `tsc` would pass, nothing to do with this leaf's own code). `next start` + `curl`: `/collections` returns the "Privacy Tools" card with a correct 3-app count; `/collections/privacy-tools` returns Firefox/Obsidian/ReadEra; `/collections/nonexistent` triggers the `EmptyState`'s "not found" `notFound()` path — confirmed its `200` status code is Next's standard App Router streaming behavior for `notFound()` (the shell starts streaming before the not-found boundary resolves), not a bug introduced here, by checking `/app/totally-fake-slug` and `/categories/totally-fake-category` return the identical `200`-with-not-found-content pattern on this same build. Home page's nav confirmed to render the new "Collections" link at `/collections`.

**4.d — Local (non-account) Personalization**
- 4.d.i — Favorites
  - [x] 4.d.i.zi — IndexedDB-based local favorites — Done: `lib/favorites.ts`, a per-device favorites store — IndexedDB (not `localStorage`, as this leaf specifies) since favorites are an unbounded, open-ended collection a visitor may grow over time, unlike a single per-app record (`lib/install-status.ts`'s use case). One object store (`d-store-favorites`/`favorites`, keyed by `slug`) with `listFavorites`/`isFavorite`/`addFavorite`/`removeFavorite`, all fail-soft to an empty/false default on any IndexedDB error (private browsing, disabled storage) — same posture `lib/install-status.ts` already established for `localStorage`. A `useFavorite(app)` hook (loaded/favorited/toggle) mirrors `useInstallStatus`'s shape: `loaded` gates the first client render so there's no hydration-mismatch flash before the async IndexedDB read resolves, and a same-tab `CHANGE_EVENT` custom event keeps multiple mounted instances for the same app in sync without lifting state, the same cross-instance-sync mechanism `useInstallStatus`/`useInstallProgress` already use. New `components/FavoriteButton.tsx`/`.module.css` — a Save/Saved toggle wired into the app detail page's `installRow`, next to `ShareButton` (`4.c.ii.zi`'s placement precedent for a secondary per-app action beside Install/Download) — added so the store has a real write call site to verify against, not left unreachable pending `4.d.i.zo`. Styling reuses `ShareButton.module.css`'s outlined secondary-action treatment, filling to an accent pill on `aria-pressed="true"`. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both pass clean. The dedicated "Saved apps" view reading this same store is `4.d.i.zo`, next.
  - [x] 4.d.i.zo — "Saved apps" view (no login) — Done: new `app/saved/page.tsx`/`page.module.css`, a client component (not the usual server-component listing-page shape — the favorites list only exists in this browser's IndexedDB, which a server component can't reach). On mount, calls `listFavorites()` (`lib/favorites.ts`, client-side) for the saved slugs, then a new `lib/favorites-actions.ts` server action (`getFavoritedAppsAction`, same "thin `\"use server\"` wrapper so a client component can call existing `lib/catalog.ts` logic" pattern `searchAppsAction` already established for `SearchBar`) resolves them against the live merged catalog via `getAppBySlug` for full, current `App` data — the IndexedDB record only ever carries slug/name/icon (`FavoriteRecord`), not a full `App`, so this always re-fetches rather than trusting a stale local copy. Three states: loading (`ShelfGrid` of `SkeletonCard`s, same shape `SkeletonShelf` already uses for an async fetch), empty (`EmptyState`, `kind="filter"`, linking back to `/`), and populated (the same `ShelfGrid`/`AppCard` pairing every other listing page uses, so a saved card's badges — origin label, Editors' Pick — stay accurate since it's live data, not a cached snapshot). A favorite whose app has since left the catalog is silently dropped by `getFavoritedAppsAction` (same convention `getCollectionApps` already uses for a removed collection member) rather than rendered as a broken card; its stale IndexedDB record isn't actively pruned here, out of this leaf's scope. New `/saved` nav link added to `Header.tsx`, next to Collections, same "once the page exists" convention every other nav link here documents. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both pass clean (`/saved` listed as a real route). Verified via `next start` + `curl`: `/saved` returns `200` and server-renders the "Saved apps" heading (the loading-skeleton branch, correctly, since there's no `window`/IndexedDB during SSR); the home page's nav now includes the `/saved` link. This closes out `4.d.i` (Favorites) in full.
- 4.d.ii — Recommendations
  - [x] 4.d.ii.zi — Category-affinity "For You" row — Done: new `getCategoryAffinityApps(favoritedSlugs, limit=12)` (`lib/catalog.ts`), ranking categories by how many of the visitor's `4.d.i` favorites fall into each (ties broken by recency — `listFavorites()`'s own most-recently-favorited-first order), then pulling apps from those categories in that order, each sorted by `install_count` descending, excluding apps already favorited. No accounts and no view-history store exist yet, so favorites are the only per-device signal both deliberate and category-resolvable today; a richer signal (view history) is explicitly left to `4.d.ii.zo`, next. New `getForYouAppsAction(favoritedSlugs)` (`lib/favorites-actions.ts`) is the same thin server-action wrapper `getFavoritedAppsAction` already established, so the new client component `components/ForYouShelf.tsx` can call the catalog logic without duplicating it. `ForYouShelf` follows `/saved`'s "read `listFavorites()` client-side, resolve real `App` data via a server action" pattern, but renders nothing at all (no loading skeleton, no empty state) until a non-empty result resolves — most visitors have no favorites yet on any given device, so "no shelf" is the common case, and a skeleton/empty-state reservation for an optional bottom-of-home row would be noise `/saved`'s dedicated page doesn't have (a visitor there already went looking for saved apps specifically). Wired into `app/page.tsx` as the very last section, after Editor's Picks — deliberately excluded from `firstNonEmptyShelf`'s LCP-priority calculation (`3.d.ii.zo`) since it only resolves after a post-hydration client effect, never a first-paint candidate. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both pass clean; verified via `next start` + `curl` that `/` still renders (200, no server errors) with the row correctly absent from the server-rendered HTML (no favorites exist server-side to base one on) rather than flashing an empty heading. This closes out `4.d.ii.zi`; `4.d.ii.zo` (personalization tuning from local history) is next.
  - [x] 4.d.ii.zo — Personalization tuning from local history — Done: new `lib/view-history.ts`, a `localStorage`-backed local view-history store (`recordView`/`listViewHistory`) — `localStorage`, not IndexedDB, since this is a bounded, self-pruning recency window (`MAX_ENTRIES = 30`, deduped by slug on re-view) rather than favorites' unbounded curated collection, the same size-shaped distinction `lib/install-status.ts`'s own comment already draws. Stores `{slug, category, viewedAt}` — just the category, not a full `App`, since that's the only field this leaf's signal needs. New `components/ViewHistoryRecorder.tsx` (mirrors `ViewPing`'s "fire once on mount" shape, `useRef` double-fire guard included) wired into the app detail page alongside `ViewPing` — deliberately a separate component/store from `ViewPing`'s sitewide public view-counter (`3.b.i.zo`): one is a per-device personalization signal that never leaves the browser, the other is an aggregate public number, and folding the local write into `ViewPing` would have mixed those concerns. `getCategoryAffinityApps` (`lib/catalog.ts`) now takes a second `viewedCategories` parameter and blends both signals with a weighted score (`FAVORITE_WEIGHT = 3`, `VIEW_WEIGHT = 1` per occurrence) rather than treating them as equally strong — favoriting is a deliberate, durable signal; viewing is weaker and noisier, so it tunes the ranking without letting a handful of idle clicks outrank an actual favorite. Ties are broken by whichever signal touched a category most recently across both newest-first lists (`firstSeen`, replacing the old favorites-only `categoryOrder` tiebreak). Already-favorited apps are still excluded from results; already-viewed apps are not (opening a page once isn't the commitment favoriting is, and a visitor may want it resurfaced). `getForYouAppsAction` (`lib/favorites-actions.ts`) and `ForYouShelf` (`components/ForYouShelf.tsx`) both updated to read/pass `listViewHistory()`'s categories alongside favorited slugs; `ForYouShelf`'s "renders nothing until a non-empty result resolves" behavior is unchanged, just now gated on *either* signal being present rather than favorites alone. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both pass clean; verified via `next start` + `curl` that both `/` and a real app detail page (`/app/termux`) still return 200 with no server errors. This closes out `4.d.ii` and `4.d` (Local Personalization) in full.

**5.f — Catalog Database & Platform Risk**

**Requirement — migrations and edge functions live in-repo, not written against a live project:** connecting a real Supabase project to this repo must be a matter of running commands against files already committed here, not a session of writing SQL/function code interactively once a project exists. Concretely:
- Every schema change is a numbered SQL file under `supabase/migrations/` (Supabase CLI's own convention — `supabase migration new <name>` generates the timestamped filename), committed to this repo as part of the leaf that needs it, not applied by hand via the Supabase dashboard. Connecting a project and running `supabase link` + `supabase db push` should replay the entire schema from scratch with no further authoring needed.
- Any server-side logic that needs to run close to the database (webhooks, scheduled jobs, the eventual write-side of `5.g`'s Console read contract, etc.) is a Supabase Edge Function under `supabase/functions/`, committed the same way — `supabase functions deploy` should be the only step, not a first draft written after the project exists.
- This applies retroactively to `5.f.i.zi`/`5.f.i.zo` below: provisioning Supabase and migrating the schema means writing the migration files here first, then pointing the CLI at a real project — not the reverse.
- Local dev/preview never needs a live Supabase project because of this — `supabase start` (the CLI's local Postgres via Docker) can run the exact same committed migrations, matching Phase 0's existing no-live-backend-dependency posture.
- 5.f.i — Catalog database
  - [x] 5.f.i.zi — Provision Supabase (Postgres) as the store-owned data store — ratings, reviews, counters, abuse reports (catalog and developer/agreement metadata come from the Console's signed index, `5.g`) — via a committed `supabase/migrations/` file per the requirement above, not dashboard-authored SQL — Done: per the track's own "migrations and edge functions live in-repo, not written against a live project" requirement, "provisioning" here means committing the project scaffold itself, not running `supabase link` against a real project (there is none yet, and no network path to Supabase from this sandbox either). Generated `supabase/config.toml` with the real CLI (`npx supabase init`, v2.118.0 — available in-sandbox, unlike a Postgres/Docker runtime), then trimmed the 415-line default template to this project's actual shape rather than committing it wholesale: `[auth]` and `[storage]` are explicitly disabled, not left at their default `enabled = true`, each with an inline comment tracing back to a decision already recorded elsewhere in this file — `[auth]` to D-STORE.md's "no accounts" design (`ConsentBanner`'s "No account required" copy, `Review`/`ReportFlag`'s anonymous-by-design schema; `/admin/*`'s own HTTP Basic gate, `3.c.iv.zi`, is unrelated to Supabase Auth) and `[storage]` to the "Resolved — binary ownership" note (D-Store never stores binaries; that's the Console/Zealot's job). `[realtime]`/`[analytics]` are also disabled — nothing here subscribes to Postgres changes (every write is a plain Route Handler request/response, `3.b.i.zi`/`zo`) or needs Supabase's own log backend (the traffic dashboard reads this repo's own app-native counters, the "Resolved — `3.b` counter sourcing" note). `[edge_runtime]` stays enabled, unlike those: `5.f`'s own track heading names Edge Functions as real, scoped future work ("the eventual write-side of `5.g`'s Console read contract"), not speculative scope. `[db.seed]` disabled with a comment — no `seed.sql` exists yet, and there's nothing honest to seed a fresh local DB with until `5.f.i.zo` migrates the real schema. Verified two ways, both with the real CLI: `python3`'s `tomllib` parses the trimmed file with no errors (confirmed every section resolves to the intended `enabled` value); `npx supabase migration list --local`/`status` both fail with a clean "Docker not found"/`ECONNREFUSED` error, not a config-parse error — confirming the CLI itself accepts this config, the only thing missing is the Docker runtime this sandbox doesn't have (same limitation every `1.a` SQL conversion already noted for "no live Postgres in-sandbox"). No schema/table migration is part of this leaf — that's `5.f.i.zo`'s explicit, separate scope, next.
  - [x] 5.f.i.zo — Migrate schema from existing Symfony/Doctrine `Application`/`Category` entities into Supabase — as additional committed migration files, same requirement — Done: new `supabase/migrations/20260914235900_create_application_and_category.sql`, timestamped *before* the five existing `1.a` migrations in this directory (`20260915140000`+) so it plays the role each of their own headers already flagged as missing — "no foundational 'create the application table' migration exists yet, that's closer to 5.f.i.zo's scope." `category` is created before `application` (Doctrine's `manyToOne.category` FK direction), both as `text primary key` matching `review`/`report_flag`'s existing `application_id` convention. Fields mirror `Application.orm.yml`/`Category.orm.yml` 1:1 **minus** the four columns that already have their own dedicated migration in this directory (`is_featured`/`is_editors_pick` → `20260915140000`, `sha256_checksum` → `20260915140100`, `play_store_rejection_reason` → `20260915140200`) — adding them again here would conflict with those `ALTER`s. Doctrine's `type: date` on `created_at`/`updated_at` is mapped to `timestamptz`, not a bare `date`, matching the `timestamptz` convention `review`/`report_flag` already use (Doctrine's mapping type name isn't a design intent to drop time-of-day); `type: float` (`avg_rating`) → `double precision`; every Doctrine `nullable: true` field stays nullable with no invented default, same "no sensible zero-value to backfill" reasoning `sha256_checksum`/`play_store_rejection_reason` already used. Structurally verified with `sqlparse` (no live Postgres in-sandbox, same limitation every prior `1.a`/`5.f.i.zi` migration in this directory has noted): two well-formed `CREATE TABLE` statements. `supabase/config.toml`'s `[db.seed]` section needed no change — it was already disabled by `5.f.i.zi` with a comment anticipating this exact leaf ("nothing to seed a fresh local DB with until `5.f.i.zo` migrates the real schema"), which is still accurate: this leaf creates the tables, not seed data. This closes out `5.f.i` (Catalog database) in full.

**5.g — Developer Console Integration (cross-repo contract)**
*This repo never submits, uploads, authenticates developers, compiles, signs or stores binaries — it only reads the signed catalog index the separate Console (Zealot) publishes and links to the APK the Console holds. These leaves are about the read-side contract, not building the Console itself.*
- 5.g.i — Metadata read contract
  - [x] 5.g.i.zi — Signed-index reader in this repo: fetch the Console's catalog index, verify its signature and timestamp, verify each referenced file's SHA-256, cache to `storage/downloads`. Read-only — no write access, no login, matches the no-account scope decision. *(Replaces the read-only Supabase client; see "Resolved — catalog contract" in Section 0.)*
    - Done: `lib/sources/zealot.ts` (reader) + `lib/sources/zealot-trust.ts` (pinned keys, Ed25519 verify, anti-rollback, expiry — the `5.g.iv.zo` half, done together with this leaf since neither is meaningful alone). Wired into `lib/catalog.ts`'s `createZealotSource()`, replacing the mock-`apps` wrapper that function's own comment had already flagged as temporary.
    - Two gaps flagged, not silently closed: (1) this leaf's own text says "verify each referenced file's SHA-256" — this reader carries each version's `sha256` field through from the signed index into `App.sha256_checksum` for display, but does NOT download and hash the APK itself at read time to cross-check that claim (downloading every listed binary on every catalog read to verify a hash nobody has asked to download yet was judged not worth the bandwidth/latency cost for this slice — revisit if/when `0.f.i` "Verify this APK" needs a stronger guarantee than "trust the signed index's own claim"). (2) `storage/downloads` writes (both the verified-index cache and the anti-rollback state file) use plain `fs`, which only actually persists between requests where the runtime has persistent local disk — true in dev/this sandbox, **not** true on Vercel per this file's own `5.g.iv.zi` note two lines below. On Vercel as deployed today, both files get re-created from scratch on every cold start, which means: anti-rollback state does not actually survive across serverless invocations yet, and the cache-fallback-on-failure path has nothing to fall back to after a cold start until the first successful live fetch. `5.g.iv.zi`'s build-time-snapshot-plus-deploy-hook design is what actually fixes this on Vercel; this leaf's reader code is written to work correctly once that's in place (same `storage/downloads` paths, no reader-side changes needed), but isn't sufficient by itself in production until `5.g.iv.zi` lands.
    - Verified: two standalone harnesses (no test runner in this sandbox, same situation as Zealot's own 29a/29b) — one exercising `zealot-trust.ts`'s Ed25519 verify/rollback/expiry logic directly (14 assertions), one mocking `fetch` end-to-end through `createZealotSource().getApps()` against a realistic v2 fixture, covering happy path, tampered/bad signature, replay, expiry, and wrong-`schema_version` rejections, each falling back to the last verified cache rather than going empty (23 assertions). Both signed with a fresh in-process Ed25519 keypair (never the real production private key, which this repo never has); the real pinned public key was separately checked to decode to a well-formed 32 bytes. Both harnesses deleted after the run, per this codebase's "one-off check, not a permanent test file" convention. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.
  - [x] 5.g.i.zo — Review the Console's index schema (now Zealot Task 29, v2) as the consumer, and sign off the field names/types this repo needs. — Done, out-of-band (see below): field-by-field review against `docs/catalog_index_v2.md`/`.schema.json`, re-checked against the real `5.g.i.zi` reader above rather than left as a static field-list comparison (session recorded in "Cross-repo review", item 1, in Section 0).
