- 5.g.ii — Binary hand-off (Zealot compiles, signs and stores; this repo only links)
  - [x] 5.g.ii.zi — Download button/link resolves to the stable download URL carried by the contract (Zealot's download route) — never a stored signed URL, never a copy hosted here. *(Replaces the original `bundletool` compile leaf, which is now Zealot's.)* — **Confirmed by the product owner this session** (was flagged assumed, not yet confirmed): proceed as written, and mirror the real Play Store's own Install pattern as the reference point — no persistent, visible link to the raw file anywhere in the page (that treatment stays `ThirdPartyDownloadButton`'s, honestly a link to another site for Aptoide apps), the click itself resolves directly to the Console's own served URL. Done: `InstallButton.tsx` gains a required `apkUrl` prop (`App.apk`, which `lib/sources/zealot.ts`'s `5.g.i.zi` reader already carries straight through from the signed index's per-version `download_url` — the data-plumbing half of this leaf was already in place, the button just never used it). On click, a transient `<a href={apkUrl}>` is created, clicked, and immediately removed — never appended visibly, never `target="_blank"` (an APK response isn't renderable, so the browser downloads it in place with no navigation and no blank tab), so the real file now lands wherever the visitor's browser sends downloads, sourced directly from the Console's own URL — D-Store never stores or proxies a copy, satisfying "never a copy hosted here" literally, not just in the data model. `apkUrl === ""` (no release attached in the Console's index yet) renders the same disabled "Install unavailable" treatment `ThirdPartyDownloadButton` already uses for its own missing-URL case (`data-state="unavailable"`, reused via the existing `composes` CSS rule — no new styles needed) — checked before the `!loaded` localStorage read, since `apkUrl` is a plain prop with no hydration-mismatch risk. Threaded through both `InstallButton` call sites (`app/app/[slug]/page.tsx`'s header instance and `StickyInstallBar`, which gained its own new required `apkUrl` prop to forward to the `InstallButton` it renders). **Flagged, not fixed here (deliberately smaller scope than a full rebuild):** the progress fill/ring and the flip to "Open" are still timed by `SIMULATED_INSTALL_MS`, not driven by the real download's actual bytes — there is still no browser API to ask "did that fetch finish / is the file actually installed" (same limitation `lib/install-status.ts`'s own header already documents), so the click is now real but the completion signal is not yet. Byte-accurate progress would mean streaming the response and driving the fill from real `content-length` bytes, threaded through `lib/install-status.ts`'s shared progress event into both the button fill and `WavyProgressRing` — a bigger, separately-scoped change, not bundled into this leaf. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` both pass clean. **Sandbox note:** no first-party (Zealot-origin) app is currently reachable to click-test against — `lib/mock-data.ts`'s `apps` array is empty (`5.h.iv.zi` already removed the last dummy apps/developers in favor of `getMergedApps()`), and this sandbox's network allowlist has no path to a live Zealot deployment (same class of limitation `5.h.ii`'s Aptoide fetch and every `1.a`/`5.f.i` migration already flagged for their own live dependencies) — verified by code review and the type/build checks above instead of an end-to-end click-through.

- 5.g.iv — Snapshot and trust anchor *(new)*
  - [x] 5.g.iv.zi — Build-time index snapshot: a prebuild step fetches the index (through the GitHub API for the Pages repo, not the Pages CDN, so it never reads a stale copy), verifies signature, timestamp and every referenced file's SHA-256 per `5.g.i.zi`, and writes the result to `storage/downloads` so it ships with the deployment. Zealot calls a Vercel Deploy Hook after each publish. If the fetch fails, the build keeps the last good snapshot. Reason: Vercel serverless functions have no persistent local disk at runtime. — Done: new `scripts/snapshot-zealot-index.ts`, wired as `package.json`'s `"prebuild"` (npm runs it automatically before `"build"`, which is what `vercel.json`'s `buildCommand` invokes, so a Vercel Deploy Hook -> new build -> this script runs first, no separate scheduler needed). Fetches `index.json`/`index.json.sig` via the GitHub Contents API (`Accept: application/vnd.github.raw`, reads the git blob directly, never the Pages CDN) from a new `ZEALOT_CATALOG_PAGES_REPO`/`ZEALOT_CATALOG_PAGES_BRANCH` config (unset = quiet skip, same posture `ZEALOT_CATALOG_INDEX_BASE_URL`/`APTOIDE_INGEST_PACKAGES` already use), optional `GITHUB_TOKEN` to lift the 60/hr unauthenticated Contents API limit. `lib/sources/zealot.ts`'s old `fetchLiveIndex` (`5.g.i.zi`) is split into `validateIndex` (signature/schema/expiry/rollback only, no I/O beyond reading the last state) and `commitIndexState` (the actual `STATE_FILE`/`CACHE_FILE` write) — both now exported — so this script can insert the new per-file SHA-256 check *between* them: `commitIndexState` only runs if every check, including every file hash, passes; `fetchLiveIndex` itself is unchanged in net behavior, just calls the two in sequence. Per-file check closes the exact gap `5.g.i.zi`'s own Done note flagged and deferred ("does NOT download and hash the APK itself at read time") — now done once per build, not once per request. **Deliberately incremental, not exhaustive every run:** a new `filterUnverifiedVersions` diffs the candidate index against the previously-committed cache and only re-hashes (app, version) pairs whose `(download_url, sha256)` isn't already present there — a pair already in the last trusted cache was already hashed and confirmed on a prior successful run, so re-downloading every binary on every publish would be pure cost for zero new assurance. Any mismatch (or a file that can't be fetched at all) rejects the WHOLE index for that run and keeps the last good snapshot — same "collapse every rejection reason to one outcome" convention `validateIndex` already uses. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` (with the new `prebuild` step actually firing) both pass clean. **Verified in-sandbox:** ran the script directly with `ZEALOT_CATALOG_PAGES_REPO` unset — confirmed the quiet-skip log line, exit 0, `storage/downloads/zealot-index-cache.json` untouched; a throwaway harness (deleted after the run, per this repo's "one-off check" convention) confirmed `filterUnverifiedVersions` correctly returns empty for an unchanged candidate and exactly the new/changed pairs for a changed one, and cross-checked `sha256Hex`'s hashing against a known SHA-256 test vector. **Sandbox note, same class as every other live-Zealot-dependent leaf:** no real `ZEALOT_CATALOG_PAGES_REPO` is reachable here (no live Zealot deployment), and a live probe of the GitHub Contents API against a real public repo (`octocat/Hello-World`) hit this sandbox's own unauthenticated rate limit rather than exercising a real signed index — confirms the request shape/headers are right, not the full live path end-to-end. **Flagged, not fixed here** (both noted in the script's own header comment): (1) each candidate file is pulled fully into memory to hash it (`res.arrayBuffer()`), not streamed — real peak memory for a large APK, streaming through `createHash` would remove this; (2) one flaky download while hashing any single file rejects the whole run's index, same collapse-to-one-outcome tradeoff `validateIndex` already accepts, but worth a bounded retry in a follow-up rather than living with it forever.

Never take the trust anchor from the same place the index comes from.

Current pinned key: `key_id dbfa9202d7894165`, confirmed directly against a live run of Zealot's `POST /ops/catalog_index_bootstrap` and cross-checked against that route's own commit history on the Zealot side. Rotation is supported (add a second `PinnedKey` entry to the array in `lib/sources/zealot-trust.ts`, remove the old one only after the rotation is confirmed complete) but not yet exercised against a real second key.

Deliberately left "Not provided" rather than mapped: `license` (Aptoide doesn't carry one), `sha256_checksum` (Aptoide gives `md5sum`, not SHA-256), and `signing_certificate_fingerprint` (Aptoide's `signature.sha1` is a real fingerprint but SHA-1, and `SignatureInfo.tsx`'s label is hardcoded "(SHA-256)" — mapping it in would be a real inaccuracy, not just a gap; left for `5.h.iii.zi` to either make the label algorithm-aware or suppress the whole "Verify this APK" block for third-party apps, which is that leaf's documented plan anyway). **Decision recorded (resolves ❓1 by action, not by a written-down comparison):** ingestion goes through Aptoide's raw REST API directly (`https://ws75.aptoide.com/api/7`), not through either MCP candidate — that's what the operator's own probe hit, it needs no separate server process, and it's the schema this adapter is now built and verified against; revisit only if a concrete reason to prefer an MCP shows up. ❓2 (Aptoide's re-presentation/linking terms) is still genuinely unchecked, not resolved by this decision — flagged again in `lib/sources/aptoide.ts`'s header. `scripts/ingest-aptoide.ts` (new) is the manual/cron-able script `5.h.ii.zo`'s eventual scheduled job would call — `--packages a,b,c` and/or `--search "query"`, writes `storage/downloads/aptoide-snapshot.json`. **Sandbox note:** this session's network allowlist doesn't include Aptoide's domain, so the live-fetch path (`fetchAptoideApp`/`fetchAptoideSearch`) is schema-correct but was not exercised end-to-end here — run `scripts/ingest-aptoide.ts` from an environment with normal internet access to actually ingest new packages. What *was* run end-to-end in-sandbox: the operator's real WhatsApp probe data was normalized through `normalizeAptoideApp` and written to `storage/downloads/aptoide-snapshot.json` as a seed, then `getApps({ origin: "aptoide" })` and `getAppBySlug("whatsapp")` were called directly (`npx tsx`) and confirmed to return it correctly shaped (real 73.6MB size, real 9 screenshots, real changelog text, `min_android_version` correctly derived as "6.0" from SDK 23, `data_safety.provided: false`) alongside the 13 pre-existing dummy "aptoide"-origin entries, which this leaf does not touch or remove — only new `5.h.ii.zo` ingestion runs replace those, package by package. `next build` and `tsc --noEmit` both pass clean. One combined commit/patch, branch `feat/task-5h-ii-aptoide-adapter`.
  - [x] 5.h.ii.zo — Aptoide ingestion job: server-side and scheduled, calls the chosen Aptoide MCP, normalizes results to `App`, snapshots into `storage/downloads`; never a per-request call. — Done: `.github/workflows/ingest-aptoide.yml` (new), a GitHub Actions workflow (`workflow_dispatch` + weekly `schedule`) that installs deps, runs `scripts/ingest-aptoide.ts` (`5.h.ii.zi`'s already-built, already-normalizing script) with a package/search list resolved from either one-off dispatch inputs or the `APTOIDE_INGEST_PACKAGES`/`APTOIDE_INGEST_SEARCHES` repo Variables, and opens a PR with the updated `storage/downloads/aptoide-snapshot.json` rather than pushing to master directly — satisfies "server-side and scheduled... never a per-request call" without touching Supabase (Section 0's "scheduled jobs are a Supabase Edge Function" requirement is about logic that runs close to the Postgres store; this job never touches Supabase — no schema, no `5.f.i` dependency — it only refreshes a file the Next app already reads locally, so a GitHub Actions workflow, not an Edge Function, is the right home for it). Considered and rejected: a Next.js API route hit by an external cron (e.g. Vercel Cron) — the app's own request-time storage read is documented as local-file-only (Section 3's "Key location" rule), and a live Vercel function has no durable place to persist the refreshed snapshot back into the repo the way a workflow's own checkout does. Ran a Supabase Edge Function anyway to be sure of the negative and it doesn't fit either: an Edge Function is stateless per invocation with no repo checkout, so it would still need this same "commit the result somewhere" step layered on top — the PR-based commit is the actual missing piece either way, and building it once in the workflow that already has a checkout is simpler than building it twice. **Deliberately NOT wired to a real package list, and the `schedule` trigger is deliberately inert by default** — both `APTOIDE_INGEST_PACKAGES` and `APTOIDE_INGEST_SEARCHES` are left unset, and a "Skip if nothing configured" guard step makes that the quiet, non-failing default on every scheduled run rather than a red X or an invented package list. This is not the session choosing caution beyond its scope — it is *because* the two blockers `5.h.ii.zi`'s Current-position note already named are still exactly as open as it left them: ❓2 (Aptoide's re-presentation/linking terms) is unchecked, and a real target package list is a product decision, not a workflow-authoring one. Re-confirmed, not newly found, in this session: `curl -D -` against `ws75.aptoide.com` from this sandbox returns the same `x-deny-reason: host_not_allowed` the prior session described in different words ("network allowlist doesn't include Aptoide's domain") — GitHub's own runners don't have that restriction, which is the actual reason this belongs in Actions rather than being re-attempted here. Verified in-sandbox, everything that doesn't require reaching Aptoide: the workflow YAML parses cleanly (`python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(...)"`); `npx tsx scripts/ingest-aptoide.ts` with no args exits 1 with its existing "Nothing to ingest" message — confirming the workflow's own skip-guard is necessary, since the bare script would otherwise fail a scheduled run every week it's unconfigured; running it with `--packages com.whatsapp` hit the real 403/`host_not_allowed` response, confirmed via the `curl` above, and — since that run writes its result unconditionally — **overwrote the committed `aptoide-snapshot.json` with an empty array**, immediately caught via `git status`/`git diff --stat` and reverted with `git checkout -- storage/downloads/aptoide-snapshot.json` before anything else touched it; worth flagging forward since it means the ingestion script itself has no "don't clobber a good snapshot with a failed run" guard — `5.h.ii.zi`'s script always writes whatever it collected, including zero results, and this session's workflow doesn't add that safety net either (the PR step means a human reviews the diff before merge, which catches it, but the local file on disk between script-run and PR-open is briefly clobbered either way). `next build` and `npx tsc --noEmit` both pass clean against the unchanged app code — this leaf touches no `app`/`lib`/`components` files, only adds the workflow. One commit/patch, branch `feat/task-5h-ii-zo-ingest-scheduler`.

**5.k — Web Push for Saved-App Updates** *(new, split out of `5.c.i.zo` — see that leaf's note. Operator call: true Web Push, subscriptions stored server-side.)*
*Design, as recorded so the leaves below stand alone: the standard model — opt-in on a user gesture, an anonymous per-device subscription (endpoint + keys, no account) stored with the slugs it wants updates for, a server signing with VAPID keys, an event-driven sender, and pruning of expired subscriptions. iOS delivers Web Push only to sites added to the Home Screen (16.4+), so most iPhone visitors are reachable only after installing the PWA. Per-device subscription rows are the one new piece of server-side per-device state in a repo that otherwise keeps favorites/install status/ratings in the browser — `5.k.iv.zo` discloses it. Writes here are the same class as the reviews/reports/counters D-Store already owns in Supabase; the Section 3 rule against live per-request Supabase calls is about catalog/asset reads, not these.*
- 5.k.i — Subscription storage
  - [x] 5.k.i.zi — Supabase migration (`supabase/migrations/`, same posture as the existing six: written and structurally checked, no live Postgres in-sandbox): `push_subscription` (unique `endpoint`, `p256dh`, `auth`, `created_at`, `last_seen_at`; no user id), `push_subscription_app` (subscription FK `on delete cascade`, `slug`), and `push_notified_version` (`slug` primary key, last notified `version`, `notified_at`) — the diff baseline `5.k.iii` reads.
    - Done: `supabase/migrations/20260929120000_create_push_subscription_tables.sql`. Three new event handlers, appended after the two fetch behaviors, which are untouched (`install`/`activate`/`fetch` and `CACHE_VERSION` unchanged; the cached contents did not change, so no cache bump). **The header scope statement is rewritten**, as this leaf required: it now says the worker does two things to *requests* and adds three push events that never touch the fetch path. **`push`:** reads `event.data.json()` inside a `try`; a payload is used only if it is an object with a valid `slug` (same pattern and length cap as `lib/push-validate.ts`, repeated in the file because a worker cannot import TypeScript — the comment says to keep them equal). `name` and `version` are plain text, with control characters and bidirectional overrides turned into spaces, whitespace collapsed and a cut by code point (80 and 40), so nothing renders as markup and a surrogate pair is never split. **It always shows a notification** — no data, non-JSON, non-object, or no valid slug gets a generic "D-Store — An app you saved has an update." — because the subscription is `userVisibleOnly`. If `showNotification` rejects it retries with the plainest options, then gives up quietly. Per-app notifications use `tag: d-store-update-<slug>` with `renotify`, so a newer version replaces the older one and alerts again; the generic one uses a fixed tag. **`notificationclick`:** closes the notification, then focuses an existing window whose path is exactly the target, else opens a new one. The target is rebuilt from a re-validated `data.slug` (`/app/<slug>`) and is otherwise `/saved`; a stored URL is never used, so only those two paths can be opened. A window on some other page is not navigated. **`pushsubscriptionchange`:** uses `event.newSubscription` when the browser provides it, else subscribes with `event.oldSubscription.options.applicationServerKey` (the worker is a static file, so `NEXT_PUBLIC_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` is not available to it); with no key from either it does nothing. **Decision, recorded (the one the previous note left open): the worker reads the slugs itself, from the `d-store-favorites` IndexedDB** (read-only, same selection rules as `selectSlugs`: valid, newest first, de-duplicated, capped at 200), then `POST`s them to `/api/push/subscribe` and, if the endpoint changed, best-effort removes the old one via `/api/push/unsubscribe`. If the database cannot be read (error, missing store, or it does not exist — the worker aborts the open rather than create it) it sends **nothing**, because the subscribe route replaces the whole set and "could not read" must never be sent as "follows nothing"; a genuinely empty list is sent as `[]`. **No rollback on failure**, unlike `enablePush`: nobody is looking at the screen, and keeping the subscription lets `PushSync` re-send the slugs on the next page load (it does whenever a subscription exists). Nothing in the file logs. **Verified:** `node --check`; a 48-assertion scratch Node test (not committed, same posture as earlier leaves; the committed baseline suite is `5.d.iii.zi`) that runs the real `public/sw.js` in a `vm` against a fake worker global — valid, partial, hostile (control/bidi/markup/oversized/astral) and 14 malformed payloads; click routing including hijack, traversal and scheme slugs; the re-subscribe paths (given/derived subscription, no key, unreadable favorites in four ways, 503, network error, `subscribe` rejecting, same endpoint, 250 favorites capped to 200); and that the existing `fetch` handler still ignores POSTs and cross-origin requests and still intercepts navigations. A one-line mutation (sending `[]` when favorites are unreadable) made 4 tests fail, so the guard is exercised. **Not verified:** nothing ran in a real browser or against a real push service — no notification was actually displayed, `pushsubscriptionchange` was never fired by a browser, and iOS Home-Screen behavior is untested; no TypeScript changed (comment only), so `tsc`/`next build` were not re-run. **Flagged forward:** (1) notification `icon`/`badge` are deliberately omitted — the only site icons are SVG (`/icon.svg`), which several platforms will not draw in a notification; a PNG asset is needed before adding them. (2) Text is English only, like the rest of the site. (3) `pushsubscriptionchange` does nothing when the browser gives neither a new subscription nor the old key; the visitor then sees notifications as off on their next visit. (4) The click handler cannot navigate a window that is on a different page and does not close the notification if `openWindow` is blocked; both are the platform's rules. (5) **Nothing sends yet** — `5.k.iii.zi`/`zo` (sender) and Held `5.k.iv.zi` (trigger) are open, so setting `NEXT_PUBLIC_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` now would let visitors subscribe to notifications that cannot arrive; the previous note's "nothing should set the VAPID key in production until this leaf lands" is now satisfied for the worker, but the operator should still hold the key until the sender exists.

The plan therefore needs a third outcome besides "notify" and "nothing": *record the version silently*. That is why the plan carries two lists, `notify` and `baseline`, and why `5.k.iii.zo` must also write `push_notified_version` for the `baseline` entries (it still writes only after the batch was attempted). (b) The leaf says "through the existing seam," but `getApps()` sleeps 200 ms (`SIMULATED_LATENCY_MS` via `resolveAfterDelay`) and can region-filter; the dispatch wants neither. `getMergedApps()` is not exported. (c) `getMergedApps()` is tenant-scoped by the request's `Host` (`6.b.ii.zo`), while `push_subscription` rows carry no tenant. A dispatch therefore reads the catalog of whichever host was called — a decision, recorded in `5.k.xii.zo`. (d) "Which slugs have subscribers" cannot be a plain PostgREST read: it has no `DISTINCT`, so `select=slug` returns one row per (device, slug) and `supabase/config.toml`'s `max_rows = 1000` truncates it silently long before the distinct list would. That needs a SQL function (`5.k.xi.zi`) and a rule that a full page is treated as unavailable, never as a complete answer. (e) Authentication: the leaf says "shared-secret header." Vercel Cron, one of the two trigger options in the Held `5.k.iv.zi`, sends `Authorization: Bearer <CRON_SECRET>` (as far as I know — re-check current Vercel docs before `5.k.iv.zi`), so `Authorization: Bearer` is the recommended format here; it keeps option (b) open without a second scheme. Which env var the cron path uses is `5.k.iv.zi`'s call, not decided here. (f) `docs/VERCEL-SETUP.md` lists none of the push env vars (`SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`), so the operator has nowhere to learn them — `5.k.xiii.zo`. **Split:** `5.k.x` (pure plan: `.zi` per-app decision, `.zo` whole-catalog plan), `5.k.xi` (inputs: `.zi` SQL function, `.zo` store read), `5.k.xii` (`.zi` auth helper, `.zo` catalog read and tenant scope), `5.k.xiii` (`.zi` the route, read-only; `.zo` env docs and a real `next start` smoke test). The route in `5.k.xiii.zi` only *computes and returns* the plan — it sends nothing and writes nothing — so it is safe to deploy before `5.k.iii.zo` exists; `5.k.iii.zo` then adds the send and the baseline write on top of the same plan. **Order note, so the next session does not "correct" it:** `5.k.x`–`5.k.xiii` are prerequisites of `5.k.iii.zo`, so they run ahead of it in path order (the `5.k.v`/`5.k.vii` precedent). **Cross-repo check, done by cloning Zealot @ `2af75cba` and Storeapp @ `afae5a1` (both unchanged since the last check):** neither contains `push/dispatch`, `PUSH_DISPATCH` or `push_notified`; nothing here is blocked by them. **Focus run:** item 7 is replaced by 7a–7h, so the run is now 28 leaves, not 21; no new leaf completed (`still 10 done ✅ of 28`).

A TTL and a per-slug `Topic` so a newer message replaces a stale one, one coalesced notification per device per run, delete a subscription on `404`/`410`, and update `push_notified_version` only after the batch was attempted.     - Done: Per Section 1, this leaf was bigger than one session, so it was split before any code was written, and the split is this session's one task (same shape as `5.k.iii.zi` → `5.k.x`–`5.k.xiii`). Nothing under `app/`, `lib/`, `public/`, `supabase/` or `package.json` changed. **Why it was too big — it bundled six pieces verified five different ways:** (1) reading who to send to (device endpoint and keys per slug) — a SQL function, checkable only in a real Postgres; (2) recording `push_notified_version` for the `notify` and `baseline` entries — an atomic multi-row write, also SQL; (3) pure fan-out logic (which payloads go to which device, and how many) — checkable in plain Node; (4) the `web-push` dependency and a single send with its result classified — checkable only against a fake transport; (5) the orchestrator that reads, sends with bounded concurrency, prunes and writes baselines — checkable only against fakes of (1)–(4); (6) wiring it to a route and documenting the new env vars. **Things found by reading the code that the leaf text does not say:** (a) *"One coalesced notification per device per run" cannot be built as written.* `readPushPayload` in `public/sw.js` accepts exactly one `{ slug, name, version }` object and rejects an array, so a push naming several apps would show only the generic "An app you saved has an update." The choices are: one push per (device, app) with a per-slug `Topic` (the worker already shows one notification per app, tagged `d-store-update-<slug>`, so this needs no worker change; "coalesced" then means a newer undelivered message replaces an older one, not that a device gets one message), one payload-less push per device (loses which app), or extending the already-done worker (`5.k.ii.zo`) to accept a list. This is `5.k.xvi.zi`'s call; suggested: one push per (device, app) with a per-device per-run cap, and record it. (b) *A slug cannot always be used as the `Topic`.* Slugs are up to 100 characters (`MAX_SLUG_LENGTH` in `lib/push-validate.ts`) and match `^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$`; as far as I know RFC 8030 limits a `Topic` to 32 characters from the URL-safe base64 alphabet (re-check against the RFC and the `web-push` docs before relying on it). The alphabet is fine, the length is not, so the Topic has to be a fixed-length hash of the slug. (c) *The recipients read hits the same 1000-row cap `5.k.xi` documented.* `supabase/config.toml` sets `max_rows = 1000`; a (device, slug) recipient list can exceed that, and PostgREST truncates silently. `5.k.xiv.zi` must page it (or refuse a full page as `unavailable`, the rule `readDispatchState` already follows) — never send to a partial list and then record the version as notified. (d) *"Update `push_notified_version` only after the batch was attempted" loses alerts on transient failures.* The baseline is per slug, not per device, so a `5xx` or timeout for some devices cannot be recorded separately: if the version is written anyway, those devices are never told. Options: record after the attempt regardless (the leaf text; simple, lossy), or hold a slug's version back when any send to it failed transiently so the next run retries it (a device already told gets a second push, which the per-slug `Topic` replaces). `5.k.xvii.zi`'s call; suggested: hold back on transient failures, and record it. (e) *Pruning needs no new store code.* `deleteSubscription(endpoint)` in `lib/push-store.ts` already deletes by endpoint and cascades; it makes one request per dead endpoint, which is fine at this scale. A batch delete is an optimisation for a later leaf, not this one. (f) *The read-only route's guarantee.* `POST /api/push/dispatch` is documented as read-only and "safe to deploy before the sender". Adding sending to it would make every call with the secret send. `5.k.xvii.zo` should keep that route as it is and add sending behind an explicit opt-in (a separate route is the suggestion), and it must not resolve Held `5.k.iv.zi` (the trigger) by accident. (g) *Runtime and key checks.* `web-push` uses Node's `crypto` and `https`, so as far as I know the sending route needs `export const runtime = "nodejs"` (verify). Its `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` must equal `NEXT_PUBLIC_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` — browsers bind a subscription to the key they subscribed with, so a mismatch fails every send — and the sender should refuse to run rather than send when they differ. The endpoint allowlist and no-redirect hardening flagged by `5.k.v.zi` belong to `5.k.xvi.zo`. **Split:** `5.k.xiv` (recipients: `.zi` SQL function, `.zo` store read), `5.k.xv` (baseline write: `.zi` SQL function, `.zo` store write), `5.k.xvi` (`.zi` pure fan-out in `lib/push-fanout.ts`, `.zo` `web-push` and `lib/push-send.ts`), `5.k.xvii` (`.zi` the orchestrator in `lib/push-run.ts`, `.zo` route wiring, env docs and a smoke test). **Order note, so the next session does not "correct" it:** `5.k.xiv`–`5.k.xvii` are the pieces of `5.k.iii.zo`, so they run in its slot in the focus run, ahead of `5.k.iv.zi` in path order (the `5.k.x`–`5.k.xiii` precedent). **Cross-repo check, done by cloning Zealot @ `2af75cba` and Storeapp @ `afae5a13` (both unchanged since the last check):** neither contains `web-push`, `push/dispatch`, `PUSH_DISPATCH` or `push_notified`; nothing here is blocked by them. **Focus run:** item 8 is replaced by 8a–8h, so the run is now 35 leaves, not 28; no new leaf completed (`still 18 done ✅ of 35`). **Still needed for end-to-end delivery, not by this split:** a decision on Held `5.k.iv.zi` (trigger), Held `5.k.vi.zo` (throttle), a live Supabase project and a VAPID keypair; until the sender and its trigger exist the operator should keep `NEXT_PUBLIC_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` unset.

It cannot call an authenticated endpoint, so it is not the trigger as it stands. Options: (a) a second Zealot hook (`DSTORE_PUSH_HOOK_URL`) sending the secret header, a Zealot-repo leaf tracked here for visibility; (b) a Vercel Cron entry in `vercel.json` — cron frequency is plan-dependent, check current limits before choosing.

    - Done: `app/privacy/page.tsx` gains an **"Update notifications (optional)"** section, placed after Reports and before "What we don't do". Every statement in it is conditional on the visitor turning notifications on, so it is accurate today, before `5.k.ii.zi` exists. It says what is stored — the browser's push address (endpoint), the two encryption keys, the list of subscribed apps, and created/last-refreshed times — and what is not: no name, email, account or user ID, no IP address, and no link to ratings, reports or other activity. It states plainly that the push address is still a per-browser identifier, that it is used only to send update notifications and is not shared or sold, that the notification names the app and its new version, that delivery goes through the browser vendor's push service (which already knows the address because it issued it), and when the record is deleted: when the visitor turns notifications off in D-Store, or when the push service reports the address invalid. It admits that revoking permission in browser settings may not be noticed until the next send, and that with no account a record cannot be looked up by identity. **Dependent statements corrected in the same commit, because they would otherwise have become false:** the "No accounts" section (now points to the one optional exception), the "What we don't do" account bullet (now names the notification subscription as the only per-device record, deletable by the visitor), the Children's privacy section (reworded from "we don't collect personal information" to names/email/accounts, plus a sentence that notifications are optional for everyone), the page `description` metadata, and `LAST_UPDATED` (September 15 → September 29, 2026, as the page's own "Changes" section promises).     - Done: new `lib/push-validate.ts`, pure (no I/O, no `node:` imports, no new dependency), every function takes `unknown`, returns `{ ok, value } | { ok: false, error }`, and never throws (each is wrapped, so a throwing getter or Proxy in the input cannot escape). Exports `validateEndpoint`, `validateP256dh`, `validateAuth`, `validatePushSubscription` (the browser-standard `PushSubscription.toJSON()` shape: `{ endpoint, keys: { p256dh, auth } }`, extra fields such as `expirationTime` ignored) and `validateSlugs`, plus the caps as constants. The request envelope around a subscription is `5.k.vi.zi`'s to define, not this module's. **Decisions, recorded:** (1) endpoint must start with the literal `https://` (also what the table's `like 'https://%'` check needs), is returned **unchanged**, not normalized, so unsubscribe matches what subscribe stored; length cap 2048; no whitespace/control characters, credentials, fragment or non-default port; host must be a dotted name, not an IPv4/IPv6 literal (the URL parser already rewrites `0x7f.1`, `2130706433`, `127.1` to dotted decimal, so those are caught too), not `localhost`, and not an internal suffix (`.local`, `.internal`, `.lan`, `.home.arpa`, …). (2) `p256dh` is exactly 87 characters decoding to 65 bytes with first byte `0x04`; `auth` is exactly 22 characters decoding to 16 bytes; both **unpadded base64url only** and canonical (non-zero trailing bits refused), so one key has one accepted spelling. (3) Slugs use Zealot's own pattern `^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$`, at most 100 characters each, at most **200** per request; over the cap is **refused, not truncated**, so a client never believes it is subscribed to slugs that were dropped; any bad entry refuses the whole list; duplicates collapse in first-seen order; an empty list is valid. Aptoide's `uname` is used as a slug verbatim (`lib/sources/aptoide.ts`), and all 12 ingested today match the pattern; if Aptoide ever emits one that does not, subscribing with that slug would `400`. **Flagged forward, not decided:** (a) the endpoint checks block the obvious server-side-request-forgery targets and **do not close it** — a public DNS name can resolve to a private address and a redirect can point anywhere. The robust controls are a host allowlist of known push services (a compatibility decision: an unknown future push service would be refused, so it needs the operator's call) and a sender that does not follow redirects; both belong to `5.k.iii.zo`. (b) `p256dh` is not checked to be a point on the curve (that needs `node:crypto`); an off-curve key fails at encryption time, so `5.k.iii.zo` must treat that failure as "drop this row", not retry it forever. **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean. Deliberate-breakage check: 9 single-line mutations of the validator, 8 caught by the test; the survivor is the explicit IPv6-literal branch, which is redundant with the dotted-name check (the URL parser serializes IPv6 hosts as bracketed hex with no dots), kept as defense in depth. **Not exercised:** nothing imports this module yet (the routes are `5.k.vi.zi`), so `next build` type-checks it but does not bundle it; and no real browser was available in-sandbox to produce a genuine `PushSubscription.toJSON()`, so the key encodings are checked against the RFC 8291 sizes and encoding, not against browser output.

One transaction: upsert the `push_subscription` row on `endpoint` (refreshing `p256dh`/`auth`/`last_seen_at`), delete that subscription's `push_subscription_app` rows, insert the new set (duplicates collapsed; `null` array treated as empty). `revoke all ... from public, anon, authenticated` and `grant execute ... to service_role`. **Verified by running it, not by parsing it:** installed PostgreSQL 16.15 in the sandbox (`apt-get update` then `apt-get install postgresql`; the first attempt 404s on stale indexes, and `apt-get update` prints an unrelated nodesource 403 that can be ignored; the server must be started by hand with absolute paths under `su postgres`, since `su` drops `PATH`), created scratch `anon`/`authenticated`/`service_role` roles (the last with `BYPASSRLS`) and default privileges that hand tables and functions to all three, as Supabase does, then applied `5.k.i.zi`'s migration and this one. Results: (a) replace shrinks 3→1, grows 1→4, empties to 0 while keeping the subscription row, a `null` array behaves as empty, repeated calls leave one row per endpoint (not duplicates), a second device is unaffected; (b) a `null` slug element and an `http://` endpoint both raise, and the previous keys and slug set are still there afterwards — the failed call rolled back including its upsert; (c) `has_function_privilege` is `f`/`f`/`t` for anon/authenticated/service_role, calling as `anon` or `authenticated` gives `permission denied for function`, and `anon` reading the tables directly sees 0 rows; (d) as a backstop check, temporarily granting `anon` execute still failed with a row-level-security violation and wrote nothing, then the grant was removed; re-running the migration is idempotent and leaves anon without execute; (e) two concurrent calls for one endpoint serialize on the row lock (the second waited ~2 s behind the first) and the later caller's slug set won in full, not interleaved. **Not exercised, stated plainly:** plain Postgres is not PostgREST or Supabase — nothing here proves the function is reachable at `/rpc/replace_push_subscription` through PostgREST, that PostgREST's schema cache picks it up, or how `supabase-js`/`fetch` serialize a `text[]` argument (a JSON array is the documented form; `5.k.vii.zo` should confirm against the docs). (2) The function does not touch `push_notified_version`; that table belongs to `5.k.iii`. (3) `deleteSubscription` needs no function — `delete from push_subscription where endpoint = ...` cascades to the slug rows — but it is a table write, so `5.k.vii.zo` must reach it through PostgREST with the service-role key, which does bypass RLS.

Requirements whichever is chosen: a request timeout, never log or return the endpoint, keys or service-role key (including inside error messages), and every failure returns a result instead of throwing.     - Done: new `lib/push-store.ts` (server-only by convention; reads env per call, never at import, so `next build` and the site are unaffected when unset — `next build` was run with both vars unset and completed; note nothing imports the module yet, so that proves only that it compiles and is inert). Exports `upsertSubscription(sub, slugs, deps?)`, `deleteSubscription(endpoint, deps?)`, `isPushStoreConfigured(env?)` and `PUSH_STORE_TIMEOUT_MS` (8000). Both operations return `{ ok: true } | { ok: false, reason }` with `reason` one of `"not_configured"` (the routes map to `503`), `"unavailable"` (any request failure — network, timeout, refused redirect, non-2xx — deliberately one reason; routes treat it as a server-side failure) or `"invalid_input"` (backstop: arguments re-checked with `lib/push-validate.ts` before any request, and checked *before* config). Never throws. **Decisions, recorded:** (1) **plain `fetch`, not `@supabase/supabase-js`** — two calls against a table and a function this repo owns; no new dependency. (2) `upsertSubscription` POSTs to `/rest/v1/rpc/replace_push_subscription` with exactly `p_endpoint`/`p_p256dh`/`p_auth`/`p_slugs`; `deleteSubscription` sends `DELETE /rest/v1/push_subscription?endpoint=eq.<encodeURIComponent(endpoint)>` (slug rows cascade). (3) Any 2xx is success and the body is never read: the void RPC returns 200 or 204 depending on PostgREST version, and — the real reason — Postgres error details quote the failing row, endpoint and keys included, and PostgREST forwards them, so response bodies are never parsed or logged. The only log line is a fixed label plus the status code (`push-store: subscribe failed, status 500` / `... no response`); caught error objects are not logged either, since a fetch error message can contain the URL. (4) `SUPABASE_URL` must be `https://`, or `http://` only for `localhost`/`127.0.0.1` (the local Supabase stack), with no credentials in it; otherwise it counts as not configured rather than sending the service-role key in the clear. (5) `redirect: "error"` so the key header is never forwarded to a redirect target, `cache: "no-store"`, an 8 s abort timeout. (6) `deleteSubscription` of a string that fails endpoint validation returns `ok` without a request: it cannot be in the table (subscribe validates, and the column has an `https://` check). An unknown but valid endpoint is also `ok` (DELETE matching zero rows is a 204), so unsubscribe reveals nothing. **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; a scratch test with a fake `fetch` (11 groups: unset/blank/unusable env, exact URL/method/headers/body, 200 and 204, 3xx/4xx/5xx, rejecting and synchronously-throwing fetch whose message contains the secrets, hung request aborted, invalid input incl. null arguments, delete URL shapes, hostile endpoint staying a single filter, and a check that all 18 log lines are fixed-format and contain none of the key, endpoint, `p256dh`, `auth` or host); and a second scratch test over a real local HTTP server and real `fetch` (request reaches the server intact; a `301` is refused with exactly one request and the key not forwarded; a server that never answers is aborted at the timeout). Scratch tests are not committed; the committed baseline suite is `5.d.iii.zi`. **Not exercised, stated plainly:** no live Supabase project and no PostgREST — the real PostgREST's accepted response codes, its handling of a JSON array for the `text[]` argument, and schema-cache pickup of the new function are unverified (the SQL function itself was run in a real PostgreSQL 16 by `5.k.vii.zi`, the two halves were never run together). **Flagged forward:** (1) the store has no idea who is calling — abuse protection is `5.k.vi.zo` (Held), and until it lands `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` must stay unset in production. (2) `5.k.iii` (sender) will need reads — subscriptions by slug and `push_notified_version` — which this module does not provide; it belongs to that track, not here. (3) PostgREST `DELETE` with a service-role key bypasses RLS as intended, but a wrong `SUPABASE_URL` pointing at another project would fail closed (401/404 → `unavailable`).

No response body echoes the endpoint or keys. `next build` must pass with the env vars unset.
    - Done: `app/api/push/subscribe/route.ts` and `app/api/push/unsubscribe/route.ts` (POST only; Next answers 405 for other methods) plus a small shared helper `lib/push-request.ts` (standard `Request`/`Response`, no `next/server`). **Envelope, defined here as `5.k.v.zi` left it to this leaf:** subscribe takes `{ "subscription": PushSubscription.toJSON(), "slugs": string[] }` (`slugs` required; `[]` is valid and means subscribed to nothing; re-posting is how the client re-syncs when favorites change), unsubscribe takes `{ "endpoint": string }`. **Status codes, recorded:** `204` stored/removed (no body; unsubscribe of an unknown endpoint is also `204`); `400` invalid JSON, non-object body, or validation failure (the messages come from `lib/push-validate.ts` and are fixed strings that never interpolate input); `413` body over the cap; `415` Content-Type not `application/json`; `502` the store was configured but the request to it failed (`unavailable`); `503` store not configured — checked **first**, before the content type or body is looked at, so an unconfigured deployment does no work for a hostile caller. **Decision, recorded (the one `5.k.vii.zo` left open): `unavailable` → `502`, not `503`,** so "not set up" and "set up but failing" stay distinguishable in logs. `invalid_input` from the store cannot occur (the routes validate first) and would map to `502` if it did. **Choices worth knowing:** (1) `application/json` is required, which also blocks a cross-site form `POST` — a browser will not send that content type cross-origin without a CORS preflight, and these routes grant none. (2) The body cap is enforced **on the stream**, not after: 32 KiB for subscribe (room for 200 × 100-char slugs, a 2048-char endpoint and both keys), 4 KiB for unsubscribe; a declared `Content-Length` over the cap is refused before reading, and an endless or lying-length body is cut off as soon as it passes the cap (a test streamed an endless body with a declared length of 10 and it was abandoned after 5 chunks, ~40 KiB). (3) Every response carries `Cache-Control: no-store`; no response body ever contains the endpoint, keys or service-role key. (4) Anyone who knows an endpoint can unsubscribe it — inherent to an account-less design and low-stakes (the endpoint is a secret URL only the device and push service hold); documented in the route. **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `next build` with `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` unset completes and lists both routes as dynamic (`ƒ /api/push/subscribe`, `ƒ /api/push/unsubscribe`); a scratch handler test (15 groups, not committed — the committed baseline suite is `5.d.iii.zi`) importing the real route modules and stubbing the global `fetch`, so it exercises route → validation → `lib/push-store.ts` together: `503` on both routes with the env unset (even for a bad body/content type) and no upstream call; `204` with empty body and the exact RPC payload (deduped slugs, `expirationTime` ignored); charset/case variants of the content type accepted; `415` for missing/form/text/near-miss types; `413` with and without `Content-Length` and for an endless stream; `400` for empty, malformed, non-object and bad-UTF-8 bodies and for 11 invalid-subscription cases (http/IP/localhost endpoints, bad keys, bad/too many slugs), each with nothing echoed and no upstream call; `502` for an upstream `500` and for a `fetch` that throws a message containing the secrets; and no log line containing the endpoint, keys, service key or host. **Not run, stated plainly:** (a) `next start` with real HTTP requests to the routes — skipped at the operator's request, so the routes were verified by calling their handlers directly, not through Next's server, its middleware or its body handling; (b) no live Supabase/PostgREST project (as in `5.k.vii.zo`), so route + real database were never run together. **Flagged forward:** (1) **Not throttled.** `5.k.vi.zo` is Held and outside the focus run; until it lands `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` must stay unset in production, which makes both routes answer `503` and write nothing. (2) `middleware.ts` matches these paths too, so a request with no region cookie triggers one `ipapi.co` lookup and gets a `Set-Cookie` on its response — the same as `/api/apps/*` today, harmless to correctness, but an unauthenticated write route that also spends a third-party lookup per cookieless request is worth excluding when `5.d.ii` is designed. Not changed here. (3) A slug the catalog does not contain is accepted (the table stores slugs as text and the catalog is not consulted); the sender (`5.k.iii`) simply never finds a version for it.

Decide whether this leaf builds a small shared limiter that `5.d.ii` later reuses, or waits for `5.d.ii`. Either way the choice needs somewhere to keep counters that survives across serverless instances. **Until this lands, do not set `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` in production:** the routes are inert (`503`) without it, and unthrottled write access to a secret-bearing table is the one thing this track must not ship.

  - [x] 5.k.viii.zi — `lib/push-support.ts`: pure functions, no I/O, no DOM writes, no React, no new dependency, and nothing that runs at import time. (1) `decodeVapidPublicKey(input: unknown)`: unpadded base64url → `Uint8Array`, must decode to exactly 65 bytes with first byte `0x04` (an uncompressed P-256 point); typed result, never throws. (2) `getVapidPublicKey()`: reads `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` **written out literally** (Next inlines `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` only for a literal member access; `process.env[name]` reads `undefined` in the browser), trims it, returns the decoded key or `null` when unset **or malformed** — a bad key must behave exactly like no key: no control, never a broken subscribe. (3) `getPushSupport(env)`: takes an injected `{ navigator, window, matchMedia? }`-shaped object so Node can test it, returns `"unsupported" | "needs_home_screen" | "supported"`. `supported` needs `serviceWorker`, `PushManager` and `Notification`. `needs_home_screen` is an iOS-family device (iPhone/iPad/iPod, or iPadOS reporting as `MacIntel` with `maxTouchPoints > 1`) that is not running standalone (`navigator.standalone` not true and no `(display-mode: standalone)` match), and it must be checked **before** the API check, because outside the Home Screen iOS has no `PushManager` and would otherwise read as a dead "unsupported". (4) `selectSlugs(favorites, max = MAX_SLUGS)`: from `{ slug, addedAt }` records return slugs de-duplicated, newest first, capped at `MAX_SLUGS` **imported from `lib/push-validate.ts`, not copied**, plus `truncated: boolean`. **Decide and record:** `validateSlugs` refuses over-cap lists (`5.k.v.zi`), so the client must cap before sending; recommendation is keep the newest 200 and have the UI say so. Exports: the types `PlanApp` (the structural subset `{ slug, name, version, rollout_percentage, rollout_status }`) and `Classification`, `classifyApp(app, baselineVersion)` → `"notify" | "baseline" | "unchanged" | "held_back"`, and `normalizeVersion(input)` (trimmed, non-empty string or `null`). **`normalizeVersion` is beyond the leaf text:** `5.k.x.zo`'s `buildPlan` must put into the plan exactly the version `classifyApp` compared, so both share one definition instead of a second `trim()` that could drift. **The three decisions, recorded (they are also in the file's header):** (1) rollout — notify only when `rollout_status === "complete"` **and** `rollout_percentage === 100`; anything else, including the two fields disagreeing (`active` at 100, `complete` at 99), is `held_back`, which writes nothing, so the app is looked at again next run. (2) no baseline — `baseline` (record the version, send nothing), which also stops the first run after deployment from notifying every subscriber about every app. (3) version comparison — trimmed-string inequality, case-sensitive; **flagged, not fixed:** a rollback to an older version notifies, and `1.10` and `1.1` are different versions. **Decided beyond the three, and why:** (a) *order of the checks.* With no baseline, rollout is checked first, so an app that is still rolling out is `held_back` rather than `baseline` — recording an unfinished version as the baseline would make it `unchanged` the moment it completes and it would never notify. With a baseline, equality is checked first, so an app whose version has not moved is `unchanged` however its rollout fields read, not `held_back` (which would claim a new version is waiting). (b) *Bad input is `unchanged`*, the one classification with no side effect (no notification, no baseline row): a non-object app, a `slug` that is not a non-empty string, a `name` that is not a string (an empty string is allowed), a `version` that is not a string or is blank. A baseline that is present but not a string is also `unchanged`, because reading it as "absent" would overwrite whatever the store holds; a `null` or blank baseline **is** treated as absent, since a nullable `push_notified_version` column is the likely source. (c) Every field is read exactly once, so a getter cannot answer differently the second time, and everything is inside a `try` so a throwing getter or Proxy reads as `unchanged`. `classifyApp` deliberately does **not** check that `slug` matches the catalog slug pattern: a slug the subscribe route would refuse can never have a subscriber, so filtering by `subscribedSlugs` in `5.k.x.zo` already excludes it, and importing `lib/push-validate.ts` here would give a pure decision function a dependency it does not need. **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; The plan depends on `apps` order only when slugs collide. (3) *Sorted by slug in plain code-unit order* — not `localeCompare`, which is locale-dependent — so the same input gives a byte-identical plan (`a10` sorts before `a2`, uppercase before lowercase; slugs are lowercase in practice). (4) *`counts`:* `considered` is the number of distinct subscribed slugs found in the catalog, and `considered = notify + baseline + unchanged + held_back + deferred` always; `counts.notify`/`counts.baseline` are the entries actually in the plan (after the cap). (6) *One bad app does not stop the plan:* each app's fields are read once into a plain snapshot inside a `try`; an app whose getter throws is treated as `unchanged` (and owns its slug), one whose `slug` cannot be read is skipped. (7) a key that will not encrypt (off-curve `p256dh`) fails inside `web-push` and is `unsendable`, not `gone`. **Known gap, not closed:** `web-push` reads a response body into memory without a cap; the socket timeout and deadline bound the time but not the size, and capping it would mean replacing its transport. **What was NOT done — at the operator's request nothing was type-checked, built, tested or run:** `npm ci`, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `next build` with every push env var unset, and the leaf's fake-transport test were **not** carried out. **Owed:** `npm ci` then `tsc` (in particular the dynamic `import("web-push")` cast and `@types/web-push` under `moduleResolution: bundler`); `next build` with every push env var unset, and a check that the dynamic import of a CommonJS package bundles and runs under `next start` (a `serverExternalPackages` entry may be needed — unverified); a fake-transport test of every branch (unset and each malformed VAPID value; `vapid_mismatch` and the unset-client-key case; invalid recipient; each disallowed host, plus `localhost`, an IP literal, a subdomain of an allowed host and a look-alike such as `evilfcm.googleapis.com.example.com`; a backslash, `@` and percent-escape in the host; each invalid message field; `201`, `404`, `410`, a `3xx`, `400`, `401`, `413`, `429`, `500`, `503`, a network error with a `code`, `Socket timeout`, an error with no code, a transport that never settles against a short `deadlineMs`, a transport that returns a non-number; the exact request handed to the transport; no result field containing an endpoint, key or body); a mutation check; and a real `web-push` call against a fake HTTPS server to confirm the request headers (`Topic`, `TTL`, `Urgency`) and that a `302` is not followed. Also still owed from earlier leaves: `tsc` and scratch tests for `5.k.xiv.zo`, `5.k.xv.zo` and `5.k.xvi.zi` (including the hand-written SHA-256's known-answer check), the real-Postgres runs of `5.k.xiv.zi` and `5.k.xv.zi`, and the route checks owed since `5.k.xiii.zi`. `npm` also printed audit findings during the lockfile update; they were not examined. **Carries forward into `5.k.xvii.zi`:** `not_configured` and `vapid_mismatch` mean stop the whole run (every device will fail the same way) and record nothing; `gone` is the only result that justifies `deleteSubscription`; `endpoint_not_allowed`, `invalid_recipient` and `unsendable` are per-device and not retryable, and the orchestrator must decide whether to leave those rows or drop them (this module never prunes them); `retryable` describes the cause only, and the transient-failure policy (finding (d)) is still the orchestrator's to record; resolve `message.deviceIndex` against the same `devices` array passed to `buildFanout`; bound concurrency and mind that one send can take up to `PUSH_SEND_DEADLINE_MS`; never log a `SendRecipient`, a `PushTransportRequest` or a result with anything added to it.
- 5.k.xvii — Run and wire *(new, split out of `5.k.iii.zo`.)*
  - [x] 5.k.xvii.zi — `lib/push-run.ts`: read the plan inputs, read recipients (`5.k.xiv.zo`), fan out (`5.k.xvi.zi`), send with bounded concurrency inside a stated time budget, prune `gone` endpoints with the existing `deleteSubscription`, then write baselines (`5.k.xv.zo`) for the `baseline` entries and for the `notify` entries that qualify. Record the decision on transient failures (see the `5.k.iii.zo` Done note, finding (d)). A partial read of recipients is never sent to and never recorded. Return counts only. Tested over fakes of every dependency, with a mutation check.
    - Done: new `lib/push-run.ts` (server-only, never throws, never logs, nothing runs at import). Exports `runPush(catalog, deps?)` returning `{ ok: true, counts, stopped_early, anomaly, baseline_write }` or `{ ok: false, reason: "not_configured" | "unavailable" | "sender_not_configured" | "unexpected" }`, the constants `PUSH_RUN_BUDGET_MS` (40000), `PUSH_RUN_RESERVE_MS` (8000) and `PUSH_RUN_CONCURRENCY` (8), and the types `RunDeps`, `RunCounts`, `RunResult`, `RunFailureReason`. No other file changed. The catalog is a parameter, not read here, because `getDispatchCatalog()` is bound to the current request; `5.k.xvii.zo` passes it in. Every dependency (`readState`, `readRecipients`, `send`, `prune`, `write`, `now`, budget, reserve, send deadline, concurrency) is injectable, defaulting to `readDispatchState`, `readRecipients`, `sendPush`, `deleteSubscription` and `writeNotifiedVersions`. Order: read state and `buildPlan`; `readRecipients` for the `notify` slugs (skipped when there is nothing to notify); `buildFanout`; send through a worker pool; prune `gone` endpoints; write baselines. **Decisions recorded (the header comment holds the full list of eleven):** (1) *transient failures (finding (d)):* a `notify` slug's baseline is held back when any of its messages failed retryably, threw, or was never attempted because the budget ran out, so the next run retries it; a repeated notification is chosen over a missed one (the per-slug `Topic` replaces the earlier push). (2) *non-retryable per-device failures* are counted and neither held back nor pruned, so one bad device cannot stall an app for everyone else. (3) only `gone` prunes. (4) `not_configured` and `vapid_mismatch` stop the run and record nothing (`sender_not_configured`). (5) a failed or partial state or recipients read stops the run before anything is sent or recorded, baseline entries included. (6) slugs in `capped_slugs` are held back. (7) any fan-out skip (`notify_skipped`, `devices_skipped`) is an anomaly and holds back every `notify` baseline for that run, since a skip cannot be attributed to a slug; `baseline` entries are still written. (8) a `notify` slug with no message qualifies (nobody left to tell). (9) *time:* no send starts when the elapsed time plus `PUSH_SEND_DEADLINE_MS` plus `PUSH_RUN_RESERVE_MS` would pass `PUSH_RUN_BUDGET_MS`; pruning also stops when the budget is spent. (10) a failed baseline write leaves the run `ok: true` with `baseline_write: "failed"`. (11) the result is counts only. **What was NOT done, at the operator's request: nothing was type-checked, built, tested or run.** `npx tsc --noEmit`, the fake-dependency test of every branch and the mutation check the leaf requires were **not** carried out, so the code is unexecuted. **Owed:** `tsc` (in particular the `for (;;)` worker with closure-mutated `let` flags, whether `stopped`/`senderStopped` narrowing complains under this repo's `tsconfig`, and `new Array(n).fill(false)` typing); a test over fakes of: state `not_configured` and `unavailable`; an empty plan (no recipients read, no write); baseline-only plan (no recipients read, one write); one device/one app sent; several apps and devices; `gone` pruned once per device even with several gone messages; a prune that fails or throws; a retryable failure holding its slug back while other slugs are recorded; a non-retryable failure not holding back; a sender that throws; `not_configured` and `vapid_mismatch` returning `sender_not_configured` with no write and no prune; recipients `unavailable`/`not_configured`/`invalid_input` with no send and no write; `buildFanout` `ok: false`; capped slugs held back; an anomaly holding back every `notify` slug but writing `baseline` entries; the budget stopping new sends with a fake clock and `unsent` counted and held back; concurrency never exceeding the limit; a `deviceIndex` that resolves to nothing; a `write` that fails or throws; a `notify` slug with no message being recorded; no result field or thrown value containing an endpoint, key, slug or version; then a mutation check. Also still owed from earlier leaves: `tsc` and scratch tests for `5.k.xiv.zo`, `5.k.xv.zo`, `5.k.xvi.zi` (including the hand-written SHA-256 known-answer check) and `5.k.xvi.zo`, `npm ci`, the real-Postgres runs of `5.k.xiv.zi` and `5.k.xv.zi`, and the route checks owed since `5.k.xiii.zi`. **Carries forward into `5.k.xvii.zo`:** call `getDispatchCatalog()` inside the route and map `DispatchTenantError` to `421` as the read-only route does; `await checkDispatchAuth` first; check `isPushStoreConfigured()` before any work; map `ok: false` reasons to fixed-string statuses (`not_configured` and `sender_not_configured` to `503`, `unavailable` and `unexpected` to `502`); return the counts as they are, never adding an endpoint, key or slug; set `maxDuration` at least `PUSH_RUN_BUDGET_MS` plus the store read time and a margin, and record what the hosting plan allows; keep `POST /api/push/dispatch` read-only and use a separate route behind an explicit opt-in (finding (f)); `export const runtime = "nodejs"`; a run with `counts.deferred > 0` or `stopped_early` means call again; do not resolve Held `5.k.iv.zi` (the trigger).

Built against that contract with the publisher absent, the same way Storeapp's `1.c.ii.zi` was built with no publisher (blank config = default tenant only). **New files:** `lib/tenant-config.ts` (pure: `TenantConfig` types, `validateTenantRecord`, `parseTenantRegistry`, `findTenantForHost`, `brandingCss`, compiled-in `DEFAULT_TENANT`), `lib/tenant-host.ts` (import-free `normalizeHost` + `TENANT_HOST_HEADER`, so the Edge middleware bundle stays light), `lib/tenant-registry.ts` (fetch/verify/cache), `lib/tenant.ts` (`getCurrentTenant()`, per-request `cache()`d). **Edited:** `middleware.ts`, `app/layout.tsx`, `components/Header.tsx`/`Footer.tsx`, and the `<title>` of `app/{about,privacy,terms,dmca}/page.tsx`. **Real decisions, flagged:** (1) **Resolution is not literally "in the middleware"**, despite this leaf's text: Next here is 15.0, where middleware is Edge-only, and the trust check needs `node:crypto` (Ed25519) plus the filesystem (anti-rollback state). Middleware does the edge-safe half — normalizes `Host`, *deletes any client-supplied `x-tenant-host`*, forwards the clean value — and `lib/tenant.ts` does the verified lookup Node-side. (2) **The registry publish shape is my proposal, not an agreed contract** — the spec fixes the per-tenant record but not how a reader finds the record for a host, and 37b/37c don't exist: one signed document `{schema_version, sequence, generated_at, expires_at, tenants:[TenantConfig…]}` at `TENANT_REGISTRY_URL`, detached Ed25519 signature at `TENANT_REGISTRY_URL + ".sig"` (same sidecar convention Storeapp's `1.c.ii.zo` invented). (3) **Trust is `5.g.i.zi`'s, reused verbatim:** `verifySignature` against the same `PINNED_KEYS` (one signer for both documents), `isExpired`, `isRollback`, state in `storage/downloads/tenant-registry-state.json`, last-verified copy in `storage/downloads/tenant-registry-cache.json` (new cache files — per Section 3's cache-location rule); any failure serves the last verified registry, nothing ever verified = default tenant only. The cache is re-parsed on read, not trusted blindly. (4) **The default tenant is compiled in and can't be overridden:** a registry record claiming `tenant_id: "default"` is dropped; `is_default_tenant` is never read off the wire. Unknown hosts, `localhost` and previews get the default tenant — never a 404 — and `brandingCss` emits nothing for it, so both existing themes' separately-tuned accents render byte-for-byte as before. (5) **A domain claimed by two tenants resolves to neither** (falls to default), independent of registry order. Optional `TENANT_BASE_DOMAIN` enables single-label `<tenant_id>.<base>` resolution. (6) `Host` only is trusted, not `X-Forwarded-Host` (spoofable without a trusted proxy that overwrites it) — confirm the Vercel/Render setup before enabling subdomain mode. (c) `logo_url`/`logo_sha256` are parsed but not rendered (no logo slot exists in Header, and rendering a remote image needs the hash check the spec calls a MUST); (d) `cdn_base`/`catalog_index_base_url` are parsed and carried on the resolved tenant but unread here — that is `6.b.ii.zo`, now unblocked, since it needs exactly this resolver. `6.b.ii.zi` is the same work as Storeapp's `1.c.iii.zi`.

    - Done: **What shipped.** `lib/sources/zealot.ts` no longer reads one process-wide env var and one process-wide memo. It takes a `CatalogScope` `{tenantId, baseUrl}`, derived from the resolved tenant by the new pure `catalogScopeForTenant(tenant, env)`: the **default tenant's scope is `ZEALOT_CATALOG_INDEX_BASE_URL`, unchanged** (unset still means "no live fetch, serve the last verified disk cache", which is how the build-time snapshot reaches a runtime with no env var), and any other tenant's scope is its own `catalog_index_base_url`. `resolveIndex` memoizes one promise per scope (tenant + URL), so concurrent first requests share one fetch. `createZealotSource(scope)` and `getZealotCollections(scope)` now require the scope. `validateIndex`/`commitIndexState` gained an optional trailing `tenantId` (default `"default"`), so `scripts/snapshot-zealot-index.ts` is untouched. `lib/catalog.ts`: `getMergedApps()` resolves the scope from `getCurrentTenant()` and caches the merged catalog per scope instead of once per process, so all ~20 catalog functions, plus `getCollections`/`getCollectionBySlug`, are tenant-scoped with **no caller changed**. **Cache location (Section 3 rule):** the default tenant keeps `storage/downloads/zealot-index-{state,cache}.json`; every other tenant gets `zealot-index-{state,cache}.<tenant_id>.json` in the same directory (new files; `tenant_id` is `[a-z0-9-]`, so it is always a safe file-name fragment). Anti-rollback state is per tenant on purpose: a shared file would make two unrelated indexes look like rollbacks of each other. **Real decisions, flagged:** (1) **A non-default tenant with no `catalog_index_base_url` (absent, `null`, `""`, or non-`https://`) has no Zealot source: empty catalog, no fetch, and it never falls back to the default tenant's index or disk cache.** The spec says `null` means "nothing to fetch, not an error" but does not say what a D-Store reader shows; falling back to the default catalog would show one tenant's apps under another's brand, and serving a leftover cache would resurrect a catalog the operator removed. Only the default tenant reads its cache without a configured URL. (2) **Bug fix inside this leaf's scope:** `validateTenantRecord` rejected an explicit `catalog_index_base_url: null`, which `tenant-config.schema.json` types as `["string","null"]` (default `null`), so a spec-valid record for a tenant with no catalog was dropped whole, taking the tenant's branding and domain with it. Now accepted; the `TenantConfig` type is `string | null`. (3) `getCatalogScope()` deliberately does not catch a `headers()` failure and fall back to the default tenant: Next signals "render dynamically" by throwing there, and swallowing it would bake the default catalog into a static page. (4) **`/sitemap.xml` went from static (`○`) to dynamic (`ƒ`)** because it reads the catalog, which now depends on the request `Host`. Checked against a baseline build of the parent commit; every other route was already `ƒ`. That is the intended behavior (a tenant's sitemap must list its own apps), but it is a change. **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `next build` clean; a scratch Node test (not committed; the baseline suite is `5.d.iii.zi`, still open), 32 checks, all passing: scope resolution (default = env var, tenant ignores the env var, null/blank/undefined/`http://` handled), record validation incl. the `null` fix, two tenants and the default reading three different signed indexes, an older `generated_at` on tenant B *not* treated as a rollback of tenant A, concurrent requests sharing one fetch pair, per-tenant cache/state file names, a null-URL tenant returning an empty catalog with **no fetch even when the default cache exists on disk**, network-down fallback staying inside each tenant's own cache, a replayed older index rejected and a genuine advance accepted per tenant, and a tampered index rejected; and an end-to-end `next start` run with a seeded tenant registry and per-tenant index caches, curling with different `Host` headers: `/search` and `/sitemap.xml` list only the default apps on the default host, only `acme`'s app on `acme.test`, and nothing on a null-URL tenant's host; `/app/<slug>` renders for the host's own app and 404s for another tenant's; a spoofed `x-tenant-host` on the default host is ignored. **Not exercised:** the live HTTPS fetch path against a real signed per-tenant index (the unit test stubs `fetch`; the end-to-end run used an unreachable `.invalid` URL to force the cache path), and anything on a deployed host. **Flagged forward, not built:** (a) **`app/sitemap.ts` and `app/feed.xml/route.ts` hardcode `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` (falling back to the default deployment), so a tenant's sitemap/feed now lists the tenant's apps under the default site's URLs**; they need a tenant-aware base URL, which is its own leaf. (b) **The Aptoide snapshot is still merged into every tenant's catalog** behind its own Zealot index; it has no tenant concept, and whether a white-label tenant should carry it is a product call. (c) **No build-time snapshot or per-file SHA-256 verification (`5.g.iv.zi`) for non-default tenants:** the prebuild script only snapshots the default index, so a non-default tenant relies on the live fetch plus a best-effort disk cache (which may not persist on a read-only serverless filesystem). (d) **Nothing in the index binds it to a tenant:** every index is signed by the same pinned key, so a mis-pointed `catalog_index_base_url` would serve another tenant's validly signed catalog; the only binding is the signed registry record. Zealot's Task 37b/37c should decide whether the index carries a tenant id. (e) The per-scope memo is per process lifetime with no TTL, and a failed first resolution is memoized as empty until restart, both unchanged from before, now per tenant. (f) If a tenant's `catalog_index_base_url` changes, the fallback cache is still that tenant's file from the old URL, since the cache holds no record of which URL produced it.
- 6.b.iii — Storeapp per-tenant config
  - [ ] 6.b.iii.zi — *(Storeapp-repo leaf, tracked here for visibility, not built by this repo)* **Superseded text, this session — originally recorded as:** "The client knows which tenant's Zealot/D-Store instance it's pointed at (build-time config or a first-run selection) — a white-label spin-off ships its own branded Storeapp fork pointed at its own tenant, never at this program's own." **Corrected:** no fork. Android's OS-level constraints make `applicationId` and the launcher name/icon genuinely build-time-fixed — those, and only those, are isolated to Gradle product-flavor selection (one flavor per distributable tenant, same codebase). Everything else — in-app branding, which CDN/API endpoints the app trusts, feature flags — is a `TenantConfig` object fetched on launch, cached with a TTL and last-known-good fallback, verified with the same Ed25519 pinned-key/anti-rollback posture as the catalog index (tenant config decides what the app trusts, so it gets the same trust treatment). Concrete breakdown lives in Storeapp's own `HANDOVER.md`, Track c (`1.c.i.zi`–`1.c.iii.zo`), same convention as the `6.a` cross-repo leaves above.

---

## 3. Standing Handoff Process (mandatory, every session)

This is the same process used to hand off the D-Store documentation itself — it is now the fixed rule for every session, no exceptions.

**Key location — cache files:** the storefront reads catalog/asset data from local cache files only, never live per-request calls to Supabase or the Console (Zealot) — this keeps the app populated and responsive even if either upstream is briefly unreachable. Cached/downloaded files live at **`storage/downloads`**. Any leaf that touches caching, downloads, or catalog population must read from and write to this location; note it explicitly in the commit body when a leaf adds or changes what's cached there.

0. **Check upstream first, before doing anything else:** `git fetch origin` and compare against `origin/master`. If origin has moved since the local clone/session was last synced (earlier patches already applied and pushed, for instance), rebase local work onto the current `origin/master` (`git rebase origin/master`) before starting the leaf and before generating any patch. A patch built against a stale base will fail to apply with `git am` even when the content it wants is logically identical to what's already there — this step is what prevents that.
1. **Do the one assigned leaf task** (Section 1 — nothing more).
2. **Update this file**: flip the completed leaf's `[ ]` to `[x]`, and move the "Current position" line (Section 0) to the next open leaf in path order (`zi` before `zo`; within a milestone before moving to the next `i/ii/iii`; within a track before the next `a/b/c/d`; within a phase before the next `1/2/3/4`).
3. **Commit** the code change and the `HANDOVER.md` update **together**, in one commit, with a message that starts with the leaf path:
   ```
   git add -A
   git commit -m "1.a.i.zi: add install_count, avg_rating, rating_count fields"
   ```
4. **Generate the patch**, based on the current `origin/master` (from step 0), for that single commit:
   ```
   git format-patch -1 HEAD -o patches/
   ```
5. **Hand the patch file to the user** — never push directly unless explicitly told to. The patch is the deliverable that closes the session. **Always hand off exactly one patch file, never more than one.** If a handoff spans several commits (e.g. a multi-commit resequencing session, or several small fixes batched together), combine them into a single file with `git format-patch origin/master --stdout > patches/000X-<description>.patch` — this produces one mbox-style file containing all the commits since the real upstream state, in order, which `git am` applies in one shot. Do not hand off several separate `.patch` files for one handoff.
6. The next session applies it with:
   ```
   cd ~/D-Store
   git am ~/storage/downloads/000X-<leaf-path>-<description>.patch
   git push origin master
   ```

**Focus-run reporting (only while the "Focus run" checklist in Section 0 is active):** after step 6's hand-off, end the final message with `N done ✅` as defined there. The checklist tick, the count and Current position are updated in the leaf's own commit (step 2), so the patch and the count can never disagree.

If a leaf can't be finished in one session, do not commit partial work as done — mark it `[~]`, commit what exists with a `WIP:` prefix, note in the commit body exactly what's left, and still hand off a patch. The next session resumes that same leaf before touching "Current position."

---

*This file is the single source of truth for what to work on. If it disagrees with any other document, HANDOVER.md wins for sequencing; D-STORE.md wins for feature/design specification.*
